# 11. Sūrat Hūd

**123 verses.** It is widely reported that it was revealed during the Meccan period. It takes its name from the prophet **Hūd**, whose story is told between the fiftieth and the sixtieth verse — he is one of the six prophets named in the sūra, and the sūra owes its name to one of them alone.

The sūra carries **the densest sequence of prophets in the Qurʾān**: Noah (Nūḥ), Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Abraham (Ibrāhīm)–Lot (Lūṭ), Shuʿayb and Moses (Mūsā) are told one after another, and **from the twenty-fifth verse to the ninety-ninth — that is, seventy-five verses** — are given over to this sequence. Two thirds of the body of the sūra is narrative.

But the sūra is not an anthology of narratives. The narratives are set out as the answer to a question, and the question is put openly in the twelfth verse: **the messenger is pressed hard enough to leave aside part of what is revealed to him** (*fa-laʿallaka tārikun baʿḍa mā yūḥā ilayka wa ḍāʾiqun bihī ṣadruk*). **The hundred and twentieth verse states the reason for the sequence:** *mā nuthabbitu bihī fuʾādak* — "[the accounts] with which We make your heart firm".

**The sūra therefore tells its narratives not to convey information but to build a power to hold out.** This is the sūra's own statement; it is not an inference.

---

## The structure of the sūra

| Block | Verses | Subject | What closes the block |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | 1-24 | The description of the Book, the call, concealment, the proofs of provision and creation, the objections, the parable of the two parties | *hal yastawiyāni mathalā, a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn* (24) |
| **II** | 25-49 | **Noah** — the most detailed account in the Qurʾān: the dispute, the ark, the flood, the son, the prayer and the answer | *fa-ṣbir inna'l-ʿāqibata li'l-muttaqīn* (49) |
| **III** | 50-60 | **Hūd** — ʿĀd | *alā buʿdan li-ʿĀdin qawmi Hūd* (60) |
| **IV** | 61-68 | **Ṣāliḥ** — Thamūd | *alā buʿdan li-Thamūd* (68) |
| **V** | 69-83 | **Abraham's guests, and Lot** | *wa mā hiya mina'l-ẓālimīna bi-baʿīd* (83) |
| **VI** | 84-95 | **Shuʿayb** — Midian | *alā buʿdan li-Madyana kamā baʿidat Thamūd* (95) |
| **VII** | 96-99 | **Moses and Pharaoh** — the shortest link in the chain | *biʾsa'l-rifdu'l-marfūd* (99) |
| **VIII** | 100-119 | The ruling drawn from the narratives: the towns that were seized, the end of the two groups, the permanence of disagreement | *wa tammat kalimatu rabbik* (119) |
| **IX** | 120-123 | Closing: the function of the narratives, waiting, service and reliance | *wa mā rabbuka bi-ghāfilin ʿammā taʿmalūn* (123) |

**The backbone of the sūra is *how* the narratives are told** — not what is told. Most of the same six prophets are told in al-Shuʿarāʾ as well; **the difference in Hūd is one of method, and that difference can be counted.** The section below establishes it from the data of the text.

### The verse-ending

The verse-endings of this sūra are not tied to a single pattern; **the case is the reverse of the single-voiced order of al-Shuʿarāʾ.** What dominates are **closings that end with two names** (*ḥakīmun khabīr*, *ʿazīzun ḥamīd*, *raḥīmun wadūd*, *qawiyyun ʿazīz*, *ḥamīdun majīd*), and throughout the sūra these tie the last clause of the verse to **a statement of attribute**.

**I record this as an observation, and I do not enter into any reckoning of letters or numbers** — that road is closed in this commentary (`002-bakara.md`). What is recorded is this: what closes a block in Hūd is not a **refrain** but **a pair of names**; and the names change each time according to the subject of the block.

---

## The backbone of the sūra — six narratives, a pattern different from al-Shuʿarāʾ

**Every table in this section is a count from the text. Where a place is interpretation, it is marked as such.**

In `026-suara.md` it was tabulated that the narratives of the seven prophets sit on **four shared patterns**: the announcement of denial, the opening with *akhūhum*, the threefold introduction (*innī lakum rasūlun amīn · fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin*) and **a two-verse seal repeated identically eight times.** In `037-saffat.md` a seal **in four parts, and not struck in full on everyone**, was treated. **I rely on both of those sections and do not repeat their frameworks here.**

**Hūd takes a third road. I gather the difference under four headings and give each of them with a count.**

### Table 1 — The size of the narratives

| Prophet | **Verse range in al-Shuʿarāʾ** | Verses | **Verse range in Hūd** | Verses |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Noah** | 105-122 | **18** | **25-49** | **25** |
| **Hūd** | 123-140 | 18 | 50-60 | 11 |
| **Ṣāliḥ** | 141-159 | 19 | 61-68 | 8 |
| **Abraham** | 69-104 | 36 | 69-76 | 8 |
| **Lot** | 160-175 | 16 | 77-83 | 7 |
| **Shuʿayb** | 176-191 | 16 | 84-95 | 12 |
| **Moses** | 10-68 | 59 | 96-99 | **4** |

**The table corrects a widespread impression, and this must be recorded plainly: the narratives of Hūd are not, taken together, longer than those of al-Shuʿarāʾ.** In Hūd only the **Noah** narrative lengthens (18 → 25); all the others shorten, and Moses comes down to four verses.

**The difference is not in the size but in what is placed inside the size.** The second table shows this.

### Table 2 — The turns of speech in the Noah narrative: the two sūras

| Turn | **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/105-122** | **Hūd 11/25-49** |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Noah (106-110) — the shared five-sentence opening | Noah (25-26) — the warning |
| 2 | **The people (111)** — "the lowest sort have followed you" | **The people (27)** — three objections: a mere human, the lowest sort, no superiority |
| 3 | Noah (112-115) — the reckoning is not mine | Noah (28) — *a-nulzimukumūhā* |
| 4 | **The people (116)** — the threat of stoning | Noah (29) — payment, and not driving anyone away |
| 5 | Noah (117-118) — prayer | Noah (30) — "who would protect me" |
| 6 | Narration (119-120) — deliverance and drowning | Noah (31) — **four negative sentences: no treasure, no unseen, no angel, no belittling** |
| 7 | The seal (121-122) | **The people (32)** — "you have disputed with us, and disputed much; bring it on" |
| 8 | — | Noah (33-34) — "the one who will bring it is God" |
| 9 | — | **God → Noah (36-37)** — revelation: no one more will believe, build the ark |
| 10 | — | Noah (38-39) — the answer to mockery |
| 11 | — | **God (40)** — the command to load |
| 12 | — | Noah (41) — *irkabū fīhā* |
| 13 | — | **Noah → his son (42)** |
| 14 | — | **The son → Noah (43a)** |
| 15 | — | **Noah → his son (43b)** |
| 16 | — | **The address to earth and sky (44)** |
| 17 | — | **Noah → his Lord (45)** — prayer |
| 18 | — | **God → Noah (46)** — the answer |
| 19 | — | **Noah → his Lord (47)** — the withdrawal |
| 20 | — | **God → Noah (48)** — permission to disembark |

**In al-Shuʿarāʾ there are seven turns of speech and every one of them is between Noah and his people. In Hūd there are twenty turns of speech, and nine of them pass not with the people but with God or with his son.**

**What Hūd adds, then, is the messenger's speech not with *his people* but with *his own life*.** I record this as the result of a count; the verses named in the table can be verified.

### Table 3 — Closings: five patterns instead of a single seal

**In al-Shuʿarāʾ eight blocks closed with the same two verses, identically. In Hūd the closing is distributed over five separate patterns, and none of them is struck on all the narratives.**

| Pattern | Noah | Hūd | Ṣāliḥ | Lot | Shuʿayb | Moses |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **(fa-)lammā jāʾa amrunā** | **40** — *ḥattā idhā jāʾa amrunā* | **58** | **66** | **82** | **94** | none |
| **najjaynā … wa'lladhīna āmanū maʿahū bi-raḥmatin minnā** | none | **58** | **66** | none | **94** | none |
| **(alā) buʿdan li-…** | **44** — *buʿdan li'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn* | **60** | **68** | none | **95** | none |
| **ka-an lam yaghnaw fīhā** | none | none | **68** | none | **95** | none |
| **A closing of its own** | **48-49** — *ihbiṭ bi-salāmin minnā* + *tilka min anbāʾi'l-ghayb* | — | — | **83** — *wa mā hiya mina'l-ẓālimīna bi-baʿīd* | — | **98-99** — walking ahead of them at the gathering |

**Five patterns, six narratives; no narrative takes all five, and no pattern is struck on all six narratives.**

**This is the difference from al-Shuʿarāʾ, and it can be counted:** in al-Shuʿarāʾ the closing is **one** and **eight out of eight**; in Hūd the closings are **five** and **not one of them is complete.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the seal of al-Shuʿarāʾ worked to **make the narratives resemble one another** — the same sentence, the same outcome. **The scattered closings of Hūd, by contrast, *separate* the narratives from one another:** the end of each people is named with its own name (*li-ʿĀd*, *li-Thamūd*, *li-Madyan*), while the end of Noah closes with a **greeting of peace**. This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### Table 4 — Hūd's own repetitions

**Hūd has no seal, but it has repetitions. And the repetitions settle not at the end of a block, as a seal does, but *inside* the block.**

| Repetition | Where it occurs | How many |
|---|---|---|
| **uʿbudū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh** | 50 (Hūd), 61 (Ṣāliḥ), 84 (Shuʿayb) | **3** |
| **istaghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayh** | **3 (the sūra's own opening)**, 52 (Hūd), 61 (Ṣāliḥ), 90 (Shuʿayb) | **4** |
| **a-raʾaytum in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī** | 28 (Noah), 63 (Ṣāliḥ), 88 (Shuʿayb) | **3** |
| **yā qawmi** (the messenger's address) | 28, 29, 30 · 50, 51, 52 · 61, 63, 64 · 78 · 84, 85, 88, 89, 92, 93 | **16** |
| **lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi …** | 29 (*mālan*), 51 (*ajran*) | **2** |
| **iʿmalū ʿalā makānatikum innī ʿāmil / ʿāmilūn** | 93 (in Shuʿayb's mouth), **121 (in the Prophet's mouth)** | **2** |

**Four data points are worth recording:**

**One — the command in the third verse of the sūra comes back in the mouths of three prophets.** *istaghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayh* is first the sūra's own call (3), and then Hūd (52), Ṣāliḥ (61) and Shuʿayb (90) frame the same sentence. **The sūra, that is, distributes its own opening call across the narratives.**

**Two — *a-raʾaytum in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī* is word for word the same in the mouths of three separate prophets** (28, 63, 88), and **in all three it begins with a question.** The shared sentence of al-Shuʿarāʾ was a **statement** (*innī lakum rasūlun amīn*); the one in Hūd is a **question.**

**Three — the address *yā qawmi* occurs sixteen times.** In al-Shuʿarāʾ the messengers do not call out to their peoples with this address; the address there is *a-lā tattaqūn*. **In Hūd the messenger calls the one he addresses "my people" every single time.**

**Four — the sentence Shuʿayb speaks in the ninety-third verse is commanded to the Prophet in the hundred and twenty-first.** *iʿmalū ʿalā makānatikum innī ʿāmil* (93) → *iʿmalū ʿalā makānatikum innā ʿāmilūn* (121). **This is the clearest place where the sūra ties the narrative to the one addressed, and it can be verified from the text.**

### Table 5 — A comparison of the two sūras

| | **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26** | **Hūd 11** |
|---|---|---|
| **How many prophets** | 7 | **6** |
| **Verses given to the sequence** | 182 (10-191) | 75 (25-99) |
| **Opening pattern** | *kadhdhabat … al-mursalīn* + *idh qāla lahum akhūhum* | *wa ilā … akhāhum* (3 times) / *wa laqad arsalnā* (2 times) |
| **Shared opening sentence** | *innī lakum rasūlun amīn* — **a statement**, 5 times | *a-raʾaytum in kuntu ʿalā bayyina* — **a question**, 3 times |
| **Closing** | **A single seal, 8/8 word for word** | **Five separate patterns, none of them complete** |
| **The people's speech** | 1-2 short sentences per narrative | A list of reasoned objections per narrative |
| **The messenger's speech with God** | Prayer only (Noah 117-118) | **Four separate turns in the Noah narrative** (36, 40, 45-46, 47-48) |
| **How the destruction is told** | A single verb in most of them (*aghraqnā*, *ahlaknā*) | **Deliverance and destruction in the same verse** (58, 66, 94) |
| **The emphasis in the seal** | *wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn* — **the majority** | *alā buʿdan li-…* — **the name of the people** |
| **What is said to the messenger** | *laʿallaka bākhiʿun nafsak* (3) — **do not grieve** | *fa-ṣbir* (49), *fa-staqim* (112), *wa'ʿbudhu wa tawakkal* (123) — **commands** |

**The last row shows the point at which the two sūras part, and I record this as my own reading:**

**al-Shuʿarāʾ gives the messenger a *consolation*: "do not grieve, those before you met the same answer."** **Hūd gives the messenger an *instruction*: "be upright, do not lean, perform the prayer, be patient, wait."**

**The ground for this is what the two sūras say after the narratives.** al-Shuʿarāʾ, after the sequence, discusses the source of the Qurʾān (192-227). **Hūd, after the sequence, gives a four-verse list of commands (112-115), and every item on that list answers to a behaviour seen in the narratives.** This is an explanation, not a ruling; but the data counted in the table can be verified.

---

## 11/1 — الٓر كِتَٰبٌ أُحْكِمَتْ ءَايَٰتُهُۥ ثُمَّ فُصِّلَتْ مِن لَّدُنْ حَكِيمٍ خَبِيرٍ

*Alif-lām-rā · kitābun uḥkimat āyātuhū thumma fuṣṣilat min ladun ḥakīmin khabīr*

"Alif-lām-rā. **This is a Book whose verses have been made firm, then set out in detail** — from the presence of One who is wise and fully aware."

**The note on the disconnected letters (*ḥurūf muqaṭṭaʿa*) has been entered many times across this series** (`002-bakara.md`, `026-suara.md` 26/1, `013-rad.md` 13/1); **I do not repeat it here.**

**This verse is the densest sentence in the sūra, and two verbs need dwelling on: *uḥkimat* and *fuṣṣilat*.** Both are passive, both take the same subject (*āyātuhū*), between them stands **thumma** — and **they report two distinct operations.**

### أُحْكِمَتْ — root ح-ك-م

**The concrete sense of the root was worked out at `002-bakara.md` 2/269 (in the discussion of *ḥikma*), and I rely on that treatment.** The entry there ran:

> **ح-ك-م: to prevent, to make firm. The concrete sense the lexicographers record is striking: *ḥakama* is the bit that turns an animal's head.** From this comes the sense "to hold a thing in its place, to keep it from swerving".

**And the picture of the bit, which is the root's original sense, explains the verb here directly.** A bit does not stop the animal; **it keeps it from swerving.** Nor does *uḥkimat* freeze the text; **it keeps it from falling apart.**

**The pattern is the passive of Form IV: *aḥkama → uḥkimat* — "it was made firm, brought into a firm state".** And the word **مُحْكَم** (*muḥkam*), from the same root, occurs in the Qurʾān as an attribute of verses.

**The word's relatives show that the root carries a single idea:**

| Derivative | Meaning | The shared idea |
|---|---|---|
| **حَكَمَة** | The animal's bit | **Preventing a swerve** |
| **حُكْم** | Ruling, decision | Tying a matter down to one place |
| **حَاكِم** | Judge | The one who ties it down |
| **حِكْمَة** | Wisdom | Knowledge standing in its place |
| **مُحْكَم** | Firm, not shifting | The structure not coming undone |
| **إِحْكَام** | Making firm | **The verb here** |

### فُصِّلَتْ — root ف-ص-ل

**The root was worked out in `041-fussilet.md`, on the occasion of that sūra's name, and I rely on that treatment.** The entry there ran:

> **The concrete sense of the root: to separate two things from one another, to put a distance between them.** … **The pattern here is Form II, and it changes the sense:** *tafṣīl* — **to divide a whole into its parts, to set it out in detail.** For a text to be *mufaṣṣal* is for its subjects to have been separated from one another, their boundaries drawn.

**And one further datum was recorded there: in Sūrat Fuṣṣilat the word occurs both as an affirmative statement (41/3) and as an objection (41/44 — *law lā fuṣṣilat āyātuh*).** **In Hūd it occurs only as a statement.**

**The root's relatives:**

| Derivative | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **فَصْل** | Separating; a section; a season |
| **فَاصِلَة** | That which separates; a verse-ending |
| **مِفْصَل** | A joint — the place where two bones part |
| **فَيْصَل** | The decisive separator, the arbiter |
| **تَفْصِيل** | Setting out in detail — **the pattern here** |

**The word مِفْصَل (joint) gives the picture of the root best:** a joint **both separates and binds** two bones. To be parted is not to be broken off.

### The two verbs coming together — the real question of the verse

**The verse attaches two verbs to the same subject, and *thumma* stands between them. Three things must be recorded separately.**

**One — the two verbs look opposed.**

| Verb | What it does | Its direction |
|---|---|---|
| ***uḥkimat*** | **Making firm** — preventing dispersal | **Inward: gathering** |
| ***fuṣṣilat*** | **Setting out in detail** — dividing into parts | **Outward: opening** |

**To make a text firm and at the same time to divide it into parts are, at first sight, two operations that undo one another.** The verse ascribes both to the same Book.

**Two — the resolution lies in the concrete sense of the root *ف-ص-ل*, and this is an observation about the language.** *Faṣl* is **a parting, not a breaking**; as in the example of the joint, what separates is at the same time what binds. **So *tafṣīl* does not undo *iḥkām* — it makes it usable.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the lexicographers' definitions of the two roots:** however firm a structure may be, **if it is not divided into sections it cannot be entered.** The verse describes the Book first as **a whole** (*muḥkam*), then as **accessible** (*mufaṣṣal*).

**Three — the particle *thumma*.** In Arabic *thumma*, unlike *fāʾ*, reports **a distance in between**. **This was treated at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/13 (*qālū rabbunā'llāhu thumma'staqāmū*) and repeated at `041-fussilet.md` 41/30; I rely on that treatment.**

**The exegetes differ over the distance here, and I give it in a table:**

| Reading | What *thumma* reports | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A sequence in time** — the verses were first present, gathered and firm, in the Preserved Tablet, and were then set out in detail, spread across twenty-three years | The primary sense of *thumma* is an interval of time |
| 2 | **A sequence of rank** — "moreover, and further"; the two attributes are present together, neither coming after the other | In Arabic *thumma* is used for ordering by rank as well |
| 3 | **A sequence of function** — firmness is the base, and the detailing derives from it | The logic of the sentence |

**All three readings are transmitted in the classical sources; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the reason for the piecemeal revelation was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/32** (*kadhālika li-nuthabbita bihī fuʾādak*) — **the first reading sits on that line;** but this does not invalidate the others.

### مِن لَّدُنْ حَكِيمٍ خَبِيرٍ — two verbs, two names

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: two names follow the two verbs, and the names come in the same order as the verbs.**

| Verb | Name | The link |
|---|---|---|
| *uḥkimat* — **was made firm** | **حَكِيم** — *ḥakīm* | **The same root: ح-ك-م** |
| *fuṣṣilat* — **was set out in detail** | **خَبِير** — *khabīr* | The one who knows the detail |

**The first link is one of wording and is beyond dispute: *uḥkimat* and *ḥakīm* are from the same root.**

**The second link I record as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the root **خ-ب-ر**, as the lexicographers record it, is **to know the inner face of a thing, its hidden side**; *khabīr* is the one who knows not what shows from outside but **what belongs to the inside**. **And setting out in detail (*tafṣīl*) is likewise the opening of a thing's inner divisions.** The two words look at the same field.

**مِن لَّدُنْ** — "from the presence of". ***Ladun* reports a narrower nearness than *ʿind*:** the lexicographers record that *ladun* is used only for **direct presence**. **The word occurred at `018-kehf.md` 18/10 and 18/65 (*ʿilman min ladunnā*).**

### Context — the place of the verse in the sūra

**The sūra opens with a description of the Book, and the two words of that description will be applied across the whole of it.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the structure of the sūra:**

| Attribute | What answers to it in the sūra |
|---|---|
| ***uḥkimat*** — a whole that does not disperse | **All six narratives arriving at the same conclusion** (100-102) |
| ***fuṣṣilat*** — set out in detail | **The long form here, against the short form of the same narrative in al-Shuʿarāʾ** |

**The sūra, that is, names its own method of narration in the first verse: the same thing, in detail.**

### What this says to the present

**The two verbs also name two distinct errors in the reading of a text.**

- **A reading that looks only to *iḥkām*:** it takes the text as a whole and does not descend into detail; the result is a general grasp with nothing filled in.
- **A reading that looks only to *tafṣīl*:** it stays in the detail and misses the structure that binds the parts together.

**The verse gives the two together. I record this as a principle of reading; it is not a conclusion drawn from the wording of the verse but an application of the structure the verse builds.**

---

## 11/2-4 — أَلَّا تَعْبُدُوٓا۟ إِلَّا ٱللَّهَ إِنَّنِى لَكُم مِّنْهُ نَذِيرٌ وَبَشِيرٌ · وَأَنِ ٱسْتَغْفِرُوا۟ رَبَّكُمْ ثُمَّ تُوبُوٓا۟ إِلَيْهِ يُمَتِّعْكُم مَّتَٰعًا حَسَنًا إِلَىٰٓ أَجَلٍ مُّسَمًّى وَيُؤْتِ كُلَّ ذِى فَضْلٍ فَضْلَهُۥ · إِلَى ٱللَّهِ مَرْجِعُكُمْ وَهُوَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ قَدِيرٌ

*allā taʿbudū illā'llāh, innanī lakum minhu nadhīrun wa bashīr · wa ani'staghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayhi yumattiʿkum matāʿan ḥasanan ilā ajalin musamman wa yuʾti kulla dhī faḍlin faḍlah · wa in tawallaw fa-innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin kabīr · ilā'llāhi marjiʿukum wa huwa ʿalā kulli shayʾin qadīr*

"**That you serve none but God.** I am to you, from Him, a warner and a bearer of good news. · **And ask forgiveness of your Lord, then turn back to Him, and He will grant you good enjoyment until an appointed term and give every possessor of merit his merit.** If you turn away, I fear for you the punishment of a great Day. · **Your return is to God; and He has power over all things.**"

### The content of the Book: two commands

**The first verse described the Book; the second and third give its content under two heads.**

| # | Command | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *allā taʿbudū illā'llāh* | **Whom to serve** |
| 2 | *istaghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayh* | **What is to be done** |

**And the second command will become the most repeated sentence in the sūra:** Hūd (52), Ṣāliḥ (61) and Shuʿayb (90) will speak the same sentence to their peoples. **The second verse of the sūra, that is, is placed in the mouths of three prophets.** This was tabulated in the section on the backbone.

### نَذِيرٌ وَبَشِيرٌ — the order itself

**The two words occur together, and in the Qurʾān the order most often begins with *bashīr*** (*bashīran wa nadhīrā* — `025-furkan.md` 25/56, `041-fussilet.md` 41/4). **Here it is reversed: *nadhīr* first.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and I issue no ruling.** The whole of the sūra is six narratives of destruction; **that the warning should stand first is consistent with the subject of the sūra.** This is an explanation.

### ٱسْتَغْفِرُوا۟ … ثُمَّ تُوبُوٓا۟ — two verbs, again with *thumma*

**The particle that came between *uḥkimat* and *fuṣṣilat* in the first verse comes here between *istighfār* and *tawba*.**

The root **غ-ف-ر** — **to cover, to put a covering over the top; *mighfar*, the helmet, is from the same root** — was treated at `071-nuh.md` 71/7 and, as noted there, in `110-nasr.md`. **I do not repeat it here.**

The root **ت-و-ب**: **to turn.** *Tawba* — a turning back.

**The order of the two verbs is worth recording, and I give it as an observation:** *istighfār* looks to the past (the covering over of what has been), *tawba* to the future (the change of direction). **And *thumma* sets a distance between them:** asking forgiveness and changing direction are not the same moment.

### يُمَتِّعْكُم مَّتَٰعًا حَسَنًا إِلَىٰٓ أَجَلٍ مُّسَمًّى

The root **م-ت-ع**: **to draw benefit from a thing; and to last long.** *Matāʿ* — provision, the thing one draws benefit from.

**The construction *ajalin musammā* has been treated in many places across this series** (`013-rad.md` 13/2, `046-ahkaf.md` 46/3, `039-zumer.md` 39/42, `035-fatir.md` 35/45, `031-lokman.md` 31/29, `029-ankebut.md` 29/53). **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the side of the sentence that must be recorded is this: the return is promised *in this world*.** *ilā ajalin musammā* — until an appointed term; that is, for the length of a lifetime.

**The same line will run again in Hūd's mouth (52): *yursili'l-samāʾa ʿalaykum midrāran wa yazidkum quwwatan ilā quwwatikum* — rain and strength.** This is a link repeated within the sūra and it can be verified from the text.

**A limit, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** no rule such as "the believer will grow rich in this world" is to be drawn from this. **What the verse says is *matāʿun ḥasan* — "good enjoyment"; an adjective that reports quality, not quantity.**

### وَيُؤْتِ كُلَّ ذِى فَضْلٍ فَضْلَهُۥ

**In the sentence *faḍl* occurs twice, and the two report different things.**

| Occurrence | Expression | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *kulla **dhī faḍlin*** | **The person who possesses merit** |
| 2 | *faḍlah* | **The return that is to be given to him** |

**The exegetes differ over the referent of the pronoun:**

| Reading | To whom the pronoun in *faḍlahū* belongs |
|---|---|
| 1 | **To the person** — each receives the return for his own merit |
| 2 | **To God** — each is given out of His bounty |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### إِلَى ٱللَّهِ مَرْجِعُكُمْ — fronting

**In the sentence *ilā'llāh* has been brought forward.** In Arabic the normal order would be *marjiʿukum ilā'llāh*.

**That fronting reports restriction (*ḥaṣr*) has been recorded in many places across this series.** **That is: "your return is to God alone."**

---

## 11/5 — أَلَآ إِنَّهُمْ يَثْنُونَ صُدُورَهُمْ لِيَسْتَخْفُوا۟ مِنْهُ أَلَا حِينَ يَسْتَغْشُونَ ثِيَابَهُمْ يَعْلَمُ مَا يُسِرُّونَ وَمَا يُعْلِنُونَ إِنَّهُۥ عَلِيمٌۢ بِذَاتِ ٱلصُّدُورِ

*alā innahum yathnūna ṣudūrahum li-yastakhfū minh, alā ḥīna yastaghshūna thiyābahum yaʿlamu mā yusirrūna wa mā yuʿlinūn, innahū ʿalīmun bi-dhāti'l-ṣudūr*

"**Take note: they bend their breasts to hide from Him. Take note: even when they wrap themselves in their garments, He knows what they keep secret and what they make public.** He is indeed the Knower of the inmost of the breasts."

**This verse is the most concrete depiction of the effort to hide in the Qurʾān. Two bodily movements are named, and both are acts of covering.**

### يَثْنُونَ صُدُورَهُمْ — to bend the breasts

The root **ث-ن-ي**: **to fold a thing, to bend it, to double it over.** **The root has occurred in several places across this series** (`022-hac.md`, `039-zumer.md`, `034-sebe.md`, `068-kalem.md`); **the pattern here needs separate attention.**

**The derivatives of the root all carry a single picture:**

| Derivative | Meaning | The shared idea |
|---|---|---|
| **ثَنَى** | He folded, he bent | **To close a thing over onto itself** |
| **اِثْنَان** | Two | A thing becoming double when folded |
| **مَثْنَى** | Two by two | The same root |
| **ثِنْتَان** | Two (feminine) | The same root |
| **اِسْتَثْنَى** | He made an exception | To set a thing apart and fold it away |
| **ثَنِيَّة** | A mountain pass; a front tooth | The point of the fold |

**And *mathānī* (an attribute of the Qurʾān; it occurs at `039-zumer.md` 39/23 in the construction *kitāban mutashābihan mathāniya*) is from this root as well** — repeated, folded.

**The use of the word here is concrete: to bend the breast.** That is, **to lean forward, to draw the shoulders in, to make oneself small.**

**More than one explanation of what this movement is is transmitted in the classical sources:**

| Explanation | What it describes |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Bodily concealment** — the person bends, turns his back, tries not to be seen |
| 2 | **Hiding what is inside** — "the bending of the breast" as a figure for the folding away and hiding of a thought |
| 3 | **Turning away** — another way of describing the turning of the back |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **But what the three have in common must be recorded: every one of them is the verb *to hide*.**

**As an occasion of revelation, reports are transmitted to the effect that a man bent over, or covered his head, as he passed by the Prophet. I do not give these as an established event;** **the verse itself speaks not of one person but of a group (*innahum*).**

### يَسْتَغْشُونَ ثِيَابَهُمْ — to wrap oneself in a garment

**This expression was treated at `071-nuh.md` 71/7 in exactly the same pattern, and I rely on that treatment.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> **Root غ-ش-ي.** The concrete sense of the root is **to cover the top of a thing, to wrap it, to come over it and enfold it**; *ghishāwa* is the veil over the eye (al-Baqara 2/7).
>
> **The verb here is Form X: *istaghshā*.** This form reports "to seek" or "to do for oneself". So *yastaghshūna thiyābahum* does not mean "their garments cover them"; it means **"they make their garments a covering for themselves"**.
>
> **ثياب**, root **ث-و-ب**: to return. *Thawb* (garment), *thawāb* (recompense), *mathāba* (the place returned to) are all from this root.

**And this verse (Hūd 11/5) was already cited in `071-nuh.md`, where this entry was made:** *"The Qurʾān records this movement not as an oddity peculiar to Noah's people but as a recurring human behaviour."*

**What belongs here is the three differences between the two verses, and all three can be read from the text:**

| | **Nūḥ 71/7** | **Hūd 11/5** |
|---|---|---|
| **The tense of the verb** | *wa'staghshaw* — **perfect**, done and over | *yastaghshūna* — **imperfect**, ongoing |
| **Who is speaking** | **Noah**, complaining to his Lord | **God**, informing the one addressed |
| **The movement beside it** | Stopping the ears with the fingers — **not to hear** | Bending the breast — **not to be seen** |

**The third difference deserves attention, and I record this as my own reading:**

**Noah's people were trying to block the *sound* coming from the messenger.** Both movements were directed at the ear (the finger, then the cloth).

**In Hūd 11/5 what is being blocked is *being seen*.** And the one they hide from is not the messenger — the sentence says *li-yastakhfū **minhu***; the exegetes differ over the referent of the pronoun:

| Reading | Whom they hide from | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **From God** | The continuation of the verse: *yaʿlamu mā yusirrūna wa mā yuʿlinūn* — the answer is given by God's knowing |
| 2 | **From the Prophet** | In the verses just before it is the messenger who speaks; and the reports transmitted as the occasion of revelation point this way |
| 3 | **From the Qurʾān** | So as not to hear it, not to meet it |

**All three readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **But the answer the verse gives supports the first reading:** the sentence meets the hiding with a piece of **knowledge** — "even when they wrap themselves in their garments, He knows."

### The structure of the verse — two occurrences of *alā*

**أَلَا (take note) occurs twice in the verse, and each time it stands at the head of a sentence.**

| Occurrence | What it reports |
|---|---|
| **The first *alā*** | **The fact** — they are hiding |
| **The second *alā*** | **The answer** — they cannot hide |

**The verse, that is, uses the particle of attention twice, and by doing so separates the claim from its answer.** This is a feature of the word order that can be verified from the text.

### حِينَ — "at the very moment"

**And the placement of the adverb of time is worth recording: *ḥīna yastaghshūna thiyābahum* — "when they wrap themselves in their garments".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse does not say "He knows in every state"; **it picks the moment at which the hiding is at its most intense and says that they are known there.** The place where the covering is thickest is made the example.

### عَلِيمٌۢ بِذَاتِ ٱلصُّدُورِ

**The construction occurs in many places in the Qurʾān and has been treated across this series** (`035-fatir.md`, `039-zumer.md`, `042-sura.md`, `064-tegabun.md`, `067-mulk.md` 67/13). **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is a link: the verse opens with the word *ṣadr* (breast) and closes with the word *ṣudūr* (breasts).**

| The beginning of the verse | The end of the verse |
|---|---|
| *yathnūna **ṣudūrahum*** — they bend their breasts | *ʿalīmun bi-dhāti'l-**ṣudūr*** — the Knower of the inmost of the breasts |

**The same word, at the two ends.** The human being bends his breast; God knows what is inside the breast. **This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.**

**And the sūra will return to this word once more: in the twelfth verse it will be said of the Prophet *wa ḍāʾiqun bihī ṣadruk* — "your breast is straitened by it".**

| Verse | Who | What happens to the breast |
|---|---|---|
| **5** | **The one addressed** | *yathnūna* — **it is bent**, in order to hide |
| **12** | **The messenger** | *ḍāʾiq* — **it is straitened**, from carrying |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the nearness of the two verses:** on the first page of the sūra both sides are named by their breasts; **one is hiding, the other is carrying.**

### What this says to the present

**The movement the verse describes is given not as a matter of belief but as *body language*** — bending, covering oneself, trying not to be seen. **And what these movements have in common is that they show the one addressed is not self-sufficient:** a person is aware of what he avoids; **the avoidance is the proof of the awareness.**

**I record this not as a conclusion drawn from the wording of the verse but as my own reading.**

---

## 11/6 — وَمَا مِن دَآبَّةٍ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ إِلَّا عَلَى ٱللَّهِ رِزْقُهَا وَيَعْلَمُ مُسْتَقَرَّهَا وَمُسْتَوْدَعَهَا كُلٌّ فِى كِتَٰبٍ مُّبِينٍ

*wa mā min dābbatin fi'l-arḍi illā ʿalā'llāhi rizquhā wa yaʿlamu mustaqarrahā wa mustawdaʿahā, kullun fī kitābin mubīn*

"**There is no creature that moves on the earth whose provision does not rest upon God. He knows its resting place and the place where it is left in trust.** All of it is in a clear book."

### The place of the verse — a question of context

**The fifth verse was about hiding. The sixth turns suddenly to provision. At first sight the link is not visible.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verb the two verses share: *yaʿlamu*.**

| Verse | Expression | What is known |
|---|---|---|
| **5** | *yaʿlamu mā yusirrūna wa mā yuʿlinūn* | **What a human being hides** |
| **6** | *wa yaʿlamu mustaqarrahā wa mustawdaʿahā* | **The place of every creature** |

**The same verb, in two consecutive verses.** The verses are not changing the subject — **they are widening the scope of the knowledge:** first the inside of a human being, then the whole of the earth.

### دَآبَّة — root د-ب-ب

**The root has been treated in several places across this series** (`027-neml.md`, `031-lokman.md`, `024-nur.md`, `034-sebe.md`, `045-casiye.md`, `042-sura.md`). **In summary:** **د-ب-ب — to move slowly and lightly, to stir, to walk.** *Dābba* is **every living thing that moves on the earth**; the word is wide enough to take in the birds and the human being as well.

**And the choice of word is worth recording:** the verse does not say *ḥayawān* (living thing) or *khalq* (created thing). It says **that which moves**. **The question of provision is the question of a being that moves** — a thing that does not rise from its place has no search for provision.

### عَلَى ٱللَّهِ رِزْقُهَا — the weight of the particle *ʿalā*

**The preposition in the sentence is *ʿalā*, and in Arabic *ʿalā* reports undertaking and obligation.**

**That the same particle does the same work at `026-suara.md` 26/113 (*in ḥisābuhum illā ʿalā rabbī*) and at 26/109 (*in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*) was treated there; I rely on that treatment.** What was recorded there was this: *"In both sentences *ʿalā* attaches the obligation to God: the payment and the reckoning are alike His."*

**And Hūd 11/6 attaches a third thing to the same particle: provision.**

**I enter a note, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** the *ʿalā* here does not mean that a **necessity** is imposed upon God. **I do not enter the classical theological dispute;** the wording of the verse reports an **undertaking**, and the source of that undertaking is again God Himself.

### Read together with 29/60

**The same field was treated at `029-ankebut.md` 29/60 (*wa ka-ayyin min dābbatin lā taḥmilu rizqahā, Allāhu yarzuquhā wa iyyākum*), and I rely on that treatment.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> ***lā taḥmilu rizqahā* — "it does not carry its own provision". The lexicographers explain this in two ways: it cannot store up its food and carry it about, or it has no power to secure its provision.**
>
> **The link with the verses before it: in verse 56 migration had been commanded. The most concrete obstacle to migration is anxiety about livelihood — and this verse is placed exactly there.**
>
> **The proof is brought not from the human being but from the animal** — by saying that a creature which cannot carry its load is nevertheless fed, it is shown **that storing up food is not a condition**.

**The difference between the two verses must be recorded, and it is a datum of the text:**

| | **al-ʿAnkabūt 29/60** | **Hūd 11/6** |
|---|---|---|
| **Scope** | *ka-ayyin min dābba* — **"how many a creature"**, a portion of them | *mā min dābbatin … illā* — **all of them, without exception** |
| **Structure** | An affirmative statement | **Negation + exception (*ḥaṣr*)** — "there is none that does not…" |
| **Emphasis** | **Those that cannot carry** — powerlessness | **Knowledge of place** — *mustaqar* and *mustawdaʿ* |
| **Context** | **The command to migrate** — removing anxiety about livelihood | **The scope of knowledge** — the continuation of verse 5 |

**al-ʿAnkabūt, that is, attaches provision to a question of *courage*, and Hūd to a question of *knowledge*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the setting in which each verse stands.** The question answered in al-ʿAnkabūt is "how shall I live?"; **the question answered in Hūd is "where shall I hide?"** — because the verse before it was about hiding.

### مُسْتَقَرَّهَا وَمُسْتَوْدَعَهَا — two words, two places

**Both words are nouns of place / passive participles from Form X, and they build an opposed pair.**

The root **ق-ر-ر**: **to stand in a place, to settle, to be fixed.** **The root has been treated in many places across this series** (`023-muminun.md`, `027-neml.md`, `032-secde.md`, `036-yasin.md`, `075-kiyame.md`, `077-murselat.md`). *Mustaqar* — **the place where one comes to rest.**

The root **و-د-ع**: **to leave; and to entrust.** **The root was treated at `093-duha.md` 93/3 (*mā waddaʿaka rabbuk*) and in `033-ahzab.md`.** *Wadīʿa* — **a trust.** *Mustawdaʿ* — **the place where a trust is left.**

**The difference between the two words is the difference between the two roots:**

| Word | Root | The character of the place |
|---|---|---|
| **مُسْتَقَرّ** | ق-ر-ر — to stand | **Lasting** — the place settled in |
| **مُسْتَوْدَع** | و-د-ع — a trust | **Temporary** — the place left in |

**The exegetes differ over what the words refer to, and I give it in a table:**

| Reading | *Mustaqar* | *Mustawdaʿ* |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The place where it lives** — its nest, its homeland | **The place where it dies / is buried** |
| 2 | **The womb** | **The *ṣulb*** (in the father's loins) — or the reverse |
| 3 | **Its place in this world** | **Its place in the hereafter** |
| 4 | **The place it is settled in** | **The place it is in temporarily** |

**All four readings are transmitted in the classical sources; I do not impose a preference.**

**But there is something to record, and this is an observation about the language: all four readings carry the same structure — one lasting, the other temporary.** **What the words point to, that is, is disputed; what they do is not.**

**A limit, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** the second reading is sometimes pressed into service for modern biology. **That road is closed in this commentary** (the same limit was set at `013-rad.md` 13/8); **the wording of the verse reports a scope of knowledge, it does not describe a mechanism.**

### كُلٌّ فِى كِتَٰبٍ مُّبِينٍ

**And the verse closes with a "book". The sūra had opened with a "book" too.**

| Verse | Expression | Which book |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *kitābun uḥkimat āyātuh* | **The one sent down** |
| **6** | *kullun fī kitābin mubīn* | **The record** |

**I record this as an observation; I do not claim that the two are the same thing.** What is recorded is that the word is used for two distinct offices within six verses.

---

## 11/7 — وَهُوَ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضَ فِى سِتَّةِ أَيَّامٍ وَكَانَ عَرْشُهُۥ عَلَى ٱلْمَآءِ لِيَبْلُوَكُمْ أَيُّكُمْ أَحْسَنُ عَمَلًا وَلَئِن قُلْتَ إِنَّكُم مَّبْعُوثُونَ مِنۢ بَعْدِ ٱلْمَوْتِ لَيَقُولَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوٓا۟ إِنْ هَٰذَآ إِلَّا سِحْرٌ مُّبِينٌ

*wa huwa'lladhī khalaqa'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa fī sittati ayyāmin wa kāna ʿarshuhū ʿalā'l-māʾi li-yabluwakum ayyukum aḥsanu ʿamalā · wa la-in qulta innakum mabʿūthūna min baʿdi'l-mawti la-yaqūlanna'lladhīna kafarū in hādhā illā siḥrun mubīn*

"**It is He who created the heavens and the earth in six days, and His throne was upon the water — so that He might test which of you is best in deed.** And if you say '**You will be raised after death**', those who disbelieve will certainly say, '**This is nothing but plain sorcery**'."

### فِى سِتَّةِ أَيَّامٍ

**This construction has been treated across this series** — `032-secde.md` 32/4, `050-kaf.md` 50/38, `057-hadid.md` 57/4. **I do not repeat it here, and I keep the same limit: no conclusion about modern cosmology or geological timescales is to be drawn from it.**

**What belongs here is only the office of the construction within the sūra:** the verse does not describe the creation and stop there; **it attaches it at once to a purpose** — *li-yabluwakum.* This is treated separately below.

### وَكَانَ عَرْشُهُۥ عَلَى ٱلْمَآءِ

**I enter a note per the method (`STYLE.md`), and I do not open the dispute.**

**This sentence falls within the general frame of the sentences about the throne.** **At `032-secde.md` 32/4 (*thumma'stawā ʿalā'l-ʿarsh*) the two main postures of the classical tradition were tabulated, and this was repeated at `020-taha.md` 20/5. I rely on that treatment and bring the table here as well, because the verse adds a new element here: *al-māʾ*.**

| Posture | What it says |
|---|---|
| **Consignment (*tafwīḍ*)** | The wording is accepted as it stands and its modality is consigned to God; the question "how" is not asked |
| **Interpretation (*taʾwīl*)** | The wording is interpreted as the establishing of sovereignty / the taking of the matter in hand |

**Both postures are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The shared principle is al-Shūrā 42/11** (*laysa ka-mithlihī shayʾ*) and **it was treated in `042-sura.md`:** no depiction whatever permits a likeness to be drawn.

**And various explanations of *al-māʾ* (the water) are transmitted in the classical sources. I pass them on and prefer none of them:**

| Explanation | What it says |
|---|---|
| 1 | **It is reported that water existed before the creation of the heavens and the earth** |
| 2 | **The sentence reports not the position of the throne but that nothing else was present at that time** |
| 3 | **The wording is left as it stands; its modality is not known** (the posture of consignment applied to this verse) |

**I set an explicit limit, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** **no scientific claim about the material beginning of the universe is to be drawn from this sentence.** This commentary does not hunt for scientific miracles (`STYLE.md`); **and the verse itself does not offer this information as a proof** — the sentence passes in the middle of the verse as **an aside**, and the verse continues at once with *li-yabluwakum*.

**And I enter a note of *tanzīh*:** every expression in the Qurʾān that reports place or direction with respect to God **is read inside the limit al-Shūrā 42/11 draws.** **In this commentary no description of modality is built on such expressions.**

### لِيَبْلُوَكُمْ أَيُّكُمْ أَحْسَنُ عَمَلًا — the centre of gravity of the verse

**This sentence was treated at `018-kehf.md` 18/7 in exactly the same structure, and I rely on that treatment without reservation.**

> **al-Kahf 18/7:** *innā jaʿalnā mā ʿalā'l-arḍi zīnatan lahā **li-nabluwahum ayyuhum aḥsanu ʿamalā***
>
> **Hūd 11/7:** *…wa kāna ʿarshuhū ʿalā'l-māʾi **li-yabluwakum ayyukum aḥsanu ʿamalā***

**What was recorded in the al-Kahf section was this:** the two verses (7-8) set up **the frame of every narrative in that sūra** — what is on the earth is both **an ornament** and **an instrument of testing**, and its end is **bare dry ground.** And this entry was made there: *"Before entering its four narratives, the sūra says both what is in hand and how it ends."*

**And exactly the same thing happens here. This is the strongest structural overlap between the two sūras, and it can be tabulated:**

| | **al-Kahf 18/7** | **Hūd 11/7** |
|---|---|---|
| **Where the sentence stands** | The **seventh** verse of the sūra | The **seventh** verse of the sūra |
| **What comes before it** | The messenger's grief (6) — *bākhiʿun nafsak* | Hiding and provision (5-6) |
| **What comes after it** | **Four narratives** (9-98) | **Six narratives** (25-99) |
| **The pattern** | *li-**na**bluwahum* — **first person plural** | *li-**ya**bluwakum* — **third person, addressing "you"** |
| **Those tested** | *-hum* — **they** (third person) | *-kum* — **you** (second person) |

**Two differences must be recorded, and both are data of the text:**

**One — in al-Kahf those tested are in the third person (*ayyuhum*), in Hūd in the second (*ayyukum*).** **Hūd, that is, turns the same sentence directly onto the one addressed.**

**Two — in al-Kahf the instrument of the testing is named (*mā ʿalā'l-arḍ* — what is on the earth); in Hūd it is not.** In Hūd the testing is attached to **the creation itself**.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** both sūras set up the same frame before entering the sequence of narratives — **what is to be told is not a history but a set of examples of a testing.** al-Kahf does it with four narratives, Hūd with six.

### أَحْسَنُ عَمَلًا — quality, not quantity

**The side of the sentence that must be recorded is what the question is.**

**The verse does not say *ayyukum aktharu ʿamalā* ("which of you will do the most"). It says *aḥsanu ʿamalā*: "the best".**

The root **ح-س-ن**: **to be beautiful, to be good.** **And *aḥsan* is an elative.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the measure is not **number** but **quality.** And the same distinction is repeated in the Qurʾān — *li-yabluwakum ayyukum aḥsanu ʿamalā* occurs at al-Mulk 67/2 as well and **was treated in `067-mulk.md`.** **The same sentence in three sūras (al-Kahf, Hūd, al-Mulk); and in all three the measure is the same.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

### وَلَئِن قُلْتَ … لَيَقُولَنَّ — two conditions, two oaths

**The second half of the verse is the first instance of a pattern that will be repeated four times in the sūra: *la-in … la-yaqūlanna*.**

| Verse | Condition | Response |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | *la-in qulta innakum mabʿūthūn* | *la-yaqūlanna … in hādhā illā siḥrun mubīn* |
| **8** | *la-in akhkharnā ʿanhumu'l-ʿadhāb* | *la-yaqūlanna mā yaḥbisuh* |
| **9** | *la-in adhaqnā'l-insāna minnā raḥmah* | *innahū la-yaʾūsun kafūr* |
| **10** | *la-in adhaqnāhu naʿmāʾa baʿda ḍarrāʾ* | *la-yaqūlanna dhahaba'l-sayyiʾātu ʿannī* |

**Four verses, the same pattern.** And the **lāms** are the lām of the oath: in Arabic the structure *la-in … la-yafʿalanna* reports **that the other side's reaction is certain**.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verses **say in advance what the one addressed will say** — and the way of saying it is the mood of an oath. **What is described, that is, is not a guess but a pattern.**

### إِنْ هَٰذَآ إِلَّا سِحْرٌ مُّبِينٌ — the place of the word

**The answer given to the news of the resurrection is not "a lie" but "sorcery".**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the choice of word:** the charge of *siḥr* concedes that the speech is **effective**. **The objection, that is, is aimed not at the ineffectiveness of the speech but at the source of its effect.** **The same charge was treated at `037-saffat.md` 37/15 and `046-ahkaf.md` 46/7; I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 11/8 — وَلَئِنْ أَخَّرْنَا عَنْهُمُ ٱلْعَذَابَ إِلَىٰٓ أُمَّةٍ مَّعْدُودَةٍ لَّيَقُولُنَّ مَا يَحْبِسُهُۥٓ أَلَا يَوْمَ يَأْتِيهِمْ لَيْسَ مَصْرُوفًا عَنْهُمْ وَحَاقَ بِهِم مَّا كَانُوا۟ بِهِۦ يَسْتَهْزِءُونَ

*wa la-in akhkharnā ʿanhumu'l-ʿadhāba ilā ummatin maʿdūdatin la-yaqūlunna mā yaḥbisuh, alā yawma yaʾtīhim laysa maṣrūfan ʿanhum wa ḥāqa bihim mā kānū bihī yastahziʾūn*

"**If We defer the punishment from them to a numbered span, they will certainly say, 'What is holding it back?'** Take note: on the day it comes to them it will not be turned away from them; and what they mocked will close in upon them."

### أُمَّةٍ مَّعْدُودَةٍ — *umma* here means "a span of time"

**The sense of the word beyond "community" was treated at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/22, where the sense of *umma* as "the road followed, the line kept to" was tabulated. I rely on that treatment.**

**Here the word comes in a third sense: *an interval of time, a span*.** The idea of "priority, being at the source" in the root **أ-م-م** reaches also to the countability of a stretch of time. **And the adjective confirms it: *maʿdūda* — "numbered".**

The root **ع-د-د**: **to count.** *Maʿdūd* — counted, of a known number.

**The sentence therefore reports not an indefiniteness but a *definiteness*:** there is a deferral, but the span is **numbered.** **And the sūra will come back to this word: in the hundred and fourth verse, *wa mā nuʾakhkhiruhū illā li-ajalin maʿdūd* — exactly the same idea.**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **8** | *ilā ummatin **maʿdūdah*** |
| **104** | *illā li-ajalin **maʿdūd*** |

**The same word, at the two ends of the sūra.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### مَا يَحْبِسُهُۥ — "what is holding it back?"

The root **ح-ب-س**: **to hold, to detain, to prevent.** From the same root comes *ḥabs* (imprisonment).

**The kind of question must be recorded, and I give it as an observation:** the question does not dispute the **reality** of the punishment; it asks about its **delay**. **The mockery, that is, is aimed not at the content of the claim but at its calendar.**

**The demand that the punishment be hastened has been treated in many places across this series** (`013-rad.md` 13/6, `034-sebe.md` 34/29, `029-ankebut.md` 29/53, `037-saffat.md` 37/176). **I rely on that treatment.**

### وَحَاقَ بِهِم — the verb that encircles

The root **ح-ي-ق**: **to come down around a thing and enclose it, to encircle.** **The root was treated at `040-gafir.md` 40/45 and `046-ahkaf.md` 46/26.**

**And note the mood of the verb: *ḥāqa* is a perfect** — something that will happen in the future is told **as if it had happened**. In Arabic this reports **certainty**.

---

## 11/9-11 — وَلَئِنْ أَذَقْنَا ٱلْإِنسَٰنَ مِنَّا رَحْمَةً ثُمَّ نَزَعْنَٰهَا مِنْهُ إِنَّهُۥ لَيَـُٔوسٌ كَفُورٌ · وَلَئِنْ أَذَقْنَٰهُ نَعْمَآءَ بَعْدَ ضَرَّآءَ مَسَّتْهُ لَيَقُولَنَّ ذَهَبَ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتُ عَنِّىٓ إِنَّهُۥ لَفَرِحٌ فَخُورٌ · إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ صَبَرُوا۟ وَعَمِلُوا۟ ٱلصَّٰلِحَٰتِ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ لَهُم مَّغْفِرَةٌ وَأَجْرٌ كَبِيرٌ

*wa la-in adhaqnā'l-insāna minnā raḥmatan thumma nazaʿnāhā minhu innahū la-yaʾūsun kafūr · wa la-in adhaqnāhu naʿmāʾa baʿda ḍarrāʾa massathu la-yaqūlanna dhahaba'l-sayyiʾātu ʿannī innahū la-fariḥun fakhūr · illā'lladhīna ṣabarū wa ʿamilū'l-ṣāliḥāti ulāʾika lahum maghfiratun wa ajrun kabīr*

"**If We let a human being taste a mercy from Us and then draw it away from him, he becomes despairing and ungrateful.** · **If We let him taste a favour after a hardship that had touched him, he says without fail, 'The evils have gone from me'; he is exultant and boastful.** · **Except those who are patient and do righteous deeds: for them there is forgiveness and a great reward.**"

### Two situations, two reactions — and the symmetry of the reactions

**The two verses describe two situations that are the reverse of one another, and each is given a pair of adjectives.**

| Verse | Situation | Reaction | The pair of adjectives |
|---|---|---|---|
| **9** | **Mercy → withdrawal** | Giving up hope | *yaʾūs* + *kafūr* |
| **10** | **Hardship → favour** | Claiming it as one's own | *fariḥ* + *fakhūr* |

**And all four adjectives are in patterns that report not a passing state but a settled one** (*faʿūl* and *faʿil*): they report not a passing state but **a settled disposition**.

### The adjectives resolved

The root **ي-أ-س**: **to give up hope.** *Yaʾūs* — **utterly without hope.**

The root **ك-ف-ر**: **to cover.** *Kafūr* — **deeply ungrateful**, that is, one who covers over the good he has received. **The "covering" core of the root has been treated in many places across this series.**

**I record this as an observation:** in the ninth verse the two adjectives look at **the same event from two directions**. *Yaʾūs* looks to the future (it will not come again), *kafūr* to the past (it had never come at all).

The root **ف-ر-ح**: **to rejoice; and to grow giddy.** The word is used in the Qurʾān both positively and negatively; **in its negative use *fariḥ* is joy that has lost its measure.** **The root was treated at `028-kasas.md` 28/76 (*lā tafraḥ inna'llāha lā yuḥibbu'l-fariḥīn*).**

The root **ف-خ-ر**: **to boast, to vaunt oneself.** *Fakhūr* — **much given to boasting.** **The root was treated at `057-hadid.md` 57/23 (*mukhtālin fakhūr*).**

### ذَهَبَ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتُ عَنِّى — the flaw inside the sentence

**And the construction of what is said in the tenth verse is worth recording.**

**The man does not say "God has removed the evils from me." He says "The evils have gone from me."** The verb is **intransitive**, and its grammatical subject is *al-sayyiʾāt*; **there is no agent in the sentence.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the intransitive construction of the verb:** the departure of the hardship is told as though it had **happened of itself.** **And the next adjective (*fakhūr* — boastful) fills that gap:** the man himself steps into the place of the agent.

**And the opposite is present within the sūra:** in the sentence Hūd speaks to his people, *yursili'l-samāʾa ʿalaykum midrārā* (52), **the agent is explicit.**

### إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ صَبَرُوا۟ — the place of the exception

**The eleventh verse makes an exception to both lists of adjectives at once.**

**And the exception has two conditions: *ṣabarū* and *ʿamilū'l-ṣāliḥāt*.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the content of the two verses:** the error in the ninth verse was made **in loss**, the one in the tenth **in gain.** **The first condition of the exception (*ṣabr*) answers to loss, the second (*ʿamal*) to gain.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

**And the word *ṣabr* will return in the backbone of the sūra: in the forty-ninth verse the Prophet will be told *fa-ṣbir*, and in the hundred and fifteenth *wa'ṣbir*.**

---

## 11/12 — فَلَعَلَّكَ تَارِكٌۢ بَعْضَ مَا يُوحَىٰٓ إِلَيْكَ وَضَآئِقٌۢ بِهِۦ صَدْرُكَ أَن يَقُولُوا۟ لَوْلَآ أُنزِلَ عَلَيْهِ كَنزٌ أَوْ جَآءَ مَعَهُۥ مَلَكٌ إِنَّمَآ أَنتَ نَذِيرٌ وَٱللَّهُ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ وَكِيلٌ

*fa-laʿallaka tārikun baʿḍa mā yūḥā ilayka wa ḍāʾiqun bihī ṣadruka an yaqūlū law lā unzila ʿalayhi kanzun aw jāʾa maʿahū malak, innamā anta nadhīr, wa'llāhu ʿalā kulli shayʾin wakīl*

"**Perhaps you will leave aside part of what is revealed to you, and your breast will be straitened by it, because they say, 'Why has no treasure been sent down to him, or no angel come with him?'** You are only a warner; and God is the One who takes charge of everything."

### لَعَلَّكَ — the heaviest sentence in the sūra

**The particle *laʿalla* occurs in a few places in the Qurʾān in a sentence directed at the messenger, and these have been treated across this series:** `026-suara.md` 26/3 (*laʿallaka bākhiʿun nafsak*) and `018-kehf.md` 18/6 (*fa-laʿallaka bākhiʿun nafsaka ʿalā āthārihim asafā*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded, and it is sharp:**

| Place | What follows *laʿallaka* |
|---|---|
| **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/3** | *bākhiʿun nafsak* — **grieving to the point of destroying oneself** |
| **al-Kahf 18/6** | *bākhiʿun nafsak … asafā* — the same, with "in sorrow" added |
| **Hūd 11/12** | ***tārikun baʿḍa mā yūḥā ilayk*** — **leaving aside part of what is revealed** |

**al-Shuʿarāʾ and al-Kahf were speaking of grief; Hūd is speaking of an *act*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is what the three verses report:** grieving is an inner state; **leaving part of it aside is a decision about delivery.** Hūd names the step beyond the same strain.

**A note, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** the sentence is built in a mood of **possibility** and is **not a report that it occurred.** It is widely reported in the classical sources that this verse is read not as a failure that took place but as **the forestalling of a possible strain.** **The same limit is kept in this commentary.**

### وَضَآئِقٌۢ بِهِۦ صَدْرُكَ

The root **ض-ي-ق**: **narrowness.** **The root was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/13 (*wa yaḍīqu ṣadrī*) in Moses' objection, and I rely on that treatment.**

**And a comparison of the two places is worth recording:**

| Place | Who | When |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/13** | **Moses** | **As the task is given** — before it has begun |
| **Hūd 11/12** | **Muhammad** | **While the task is under way** — after the objections have come |

**Two prophets, the same organ, the same root.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And the form of the word must be recorded: not *ḍāʾiq* but *ḍāʾiqun bihī ṣadruk*.** What is straitened, that is, is not **you** but **your breast**; and what straitens it is *bihī* — **not the revelation itself but the carrying of it.** This is an observation about the word order.

**And its link with the fifth verse was tabulated above:** the breast of the one addressed is *bent*, the breast of the messenger is *straitened*.

### لَوْلَآ أُنزِلَ عَلَيْهِ كَنزٌ أَوْ جَآءَ مَعَهُۥ مَلَكٌ

**Two demands: a treasure and an angel.**

**These two demands were treated together at `025-furkan.md` 25/7-8** (*law lā unzila ilayhi malakun fa-yakūna maʿahū nadhīrā · aw yulqā ilayhi kanzun*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the kind of demand must be recorded: both look at the messenger's *standing*, not at what he says.** **The same family was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/111 (*a-nuʾminu laka wa'ttabaʿaka'l-ardhalūn*) and at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/31 (*rajulin mina'l-qaryatayni ʿaẓīm*).**

### إِنَّمَآ أَنتَ نَذِيرٌ — the limit

**The pattern of restriction (*qaṣr*): *innamā*.**

**This limit has been tabulated in many places across this series** (`028-kasas.md` 28/56, `035-fatir.md` 35/8, `031-lokman.md` 31/23, `039-zumer.md` 39/41, `046-ahkaf.md` 46/35, `013-rad.md` 13/7). **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the second half of the sentence: *wa'llāhu ʿalā kulli shayʾin wakīl*.**

The root **و-ك-ل**: **to commit a matter to another, to leave it in trust.** *Wakīl* — **the one who takes the matter on.**

**And this word will return in the imperative in the last verse of the sūra: *wa tawakkal ʿalayh* (123).** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it will be tabulated in the closing section.

---

## 11/13-14 — أَمْ يَقُولُونَ ٱفْتَرَىٰهُ قُلْ فَأْتُوا۟ بِعَشْرِ سُوَرٍ مِّثْلِهِۦ مُفْتَرَيَٰتٍ وَٱدْعُوا۟ مَنِ ٱسْتَطَعْتُم مِّن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ إِن كُنتُمْ صَٰدِقِينَ · فَإِلَّمْ يَسْتَجِيبُوا۟ لَكُمْ فَٱعْلَمُوٓا۟ أَنَّمَآ أُنزِلَ بِعِلْمِ ٱللَّهِ وَأَن لَّآ إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا هُوَ فَهَلْ أَنتُم مُّسْلِمُونَ

*am yaqūlūna'ftarāh, qul fa'tū bi-ʿashri suwarin mithlihī muftarayātin wa'dʿū mani'staṭaʿtum min dūni'llāhi in kuntum ṣādiqīn · fa-illam yastajībū lakum fa'lamū annamā unzila bi-ʿilmi'llāhi wa an lā ilāha illā hū, fa-hal antum muslimūn*

"**Or do they say, 'He has invented it'? Say: 'Then bring ten sūras like it, invented, and call on whomever you can besides God — if you are truthful.'** · **If they do not answer you, then know that it was sent down only with God's knowledge, and that there is no god but He. Are you then submitting?**"

### The scale of the challenge

**This verse is the *ten sūra* scale of the challenge the Qurʾān makes about itself.**

**The challenge was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/23-24, where all the scales were tabulated; I rely on that treatment.** The entry there ran:

> **The Qurʾān makes this challenge in more than one place and at different scales: bring a discourse (al-Ṭūr 52/34), bring ten sūras (Hūd 11/13), bring one sūra (al-Baqara 2/23; Yūnus 10/38).** The classical interpretation reads this as a graded narrowing — the demand was made smaller and smaller and even so was not met. **But since the order in which these sūras were revealed is not known with certainty, the chronological construction "much was asked first, then it was reduced" is an assumption; this needs stating.**

**I keep that entry exactly as it stands and I do not impose a preference.** **At `017-isra.md` 17/88 (*bi-mithli hādhā'l-qurʾān*) the widest scale — the whole of the Qurʾān — occurs; that verse has not yet been treated under a heading of its own in the al-Isrāʾ section, so I do not rely on anything there, I only cite the verse.**

**A comparison of the four scales:**

| Scale | Verse | Expression | Who is called |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The whole** | al-Isrāʾ 17/88 | *bi-mithli hādhā'l-qurʾān* | **Humankind and jinn** |
| **Ten sūras** | **Hūd 11/13** | *bi-ʿashri suwarin mithlihī **muftarayāt*** | *mani'staṭaʿtum min dūni'llāh* |
| **One sūra** | al-Baqara 2/23; Yūnus 10/38 | *bi-sūratin min mithlih* | *shuhadāʾakum* / *mani'staṭaʿtum* |
| **One discourse** | al-Ṭūr 52/34 | *bi-ḥadīthin mithlih* | — |

### مُفْتَرَيَٰت — a word peculiar to Hūd

**And there is a word Hūd adds to the scale, one that occurs in only this of the four verses: *muftarayāt* — "invented".**

The root **ف-ر-ي**: **the splitting, the cutting of a hide; and from there, to invent.** *Iftirāʾ* — to bring a saying into being where there was none.

**The work the word does here must be recorded, and I give it as an observation about the word order:**

**The objection was "he has invented it" (*iftarāh*). The answer hands the objection back its own word: "then bring ten *invented* sūras yourselves."**

| Side | Word |
|---|---|
| **The objection (13a)** | *iftarā**h*** — "he invented **it**" |
| **The answer (13b)** | *muftarayāt* — "**invented** ones" |

**The answer, that is, takes the other side's claim as granted and lightens the condition:** "it need not be revelation; **bring something like it even if it is invented.**"

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the position of the word in the sentence.** This qualification is not present at al-Baqara 2/23 or at Yūnus 10/38; **it is peculiar to Hūd.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

### A comparison with al-Baqara 2/23 — without imposing a preference

| | **al-Baqara 2/23** | **Hūd 11/13** |
|---|---|---|
| **Quantity** | **One sūra** | **Ten sūras** |
| **Who is called** | *shuhadāʾakum min dūni'llāh* — **your witnesses** | *mani'staṭaʿtum min dūni'llāh* — **whoever you are able to call** |
| **Added condition** | none | ***muftarayāt*** — it is enough that they be inventions |
| **What precedes** | *in kuntum fī raybin* — **doubt** | *am yaqūlūna'ftarāh* — **a claim** |
| **What follows** | *fa-in lam tafʿalū wa **lan** tafʿalū* — a categorical negation | *fa-illam yastajībū lakum* — **a condition, with no mood of certainty** |

**The last row is worth recording:** al-Baqara has *lan tafʿalū* ("you will never do it"), and this is a categorical negation directed at the future. **Hūd does not have that sentence; in its place come a conditional clause and a consequence.**

**I record this as a datum of the text and build no chronology on it** — since it is not known with certainty which was revealed first, the entry in `002-bakara.md` stands.

### فَٱعْلَمُوٓا۟ أَنَّمَآ أُنزِلَ بِعِلْمِ ٱللَّهِ

**And the conclusion drawn in the fourteenth verse consists of two items:**

| # | Conclusion |
|---|---|
| 1 | *annamā unzila **bi-ʿilmi'llāh*** — **its source is knowledge** |
| 2 | *wa an lā ilāha illā hū* — **there is one God** |

**And the way the second item is attached here is worth recording.** The move is from the inimitability of the text to **tawḥīd**. **I record this as an observation about the word order:** the helpers called upon were *min dūni'llāh* — that is, the challenge was built from the start on **who could be called**; and the conclusion comes out of exactly that.

**فَهَلْ أَنتُم مُّسْلِمُونَ** — "are you then submitting?" **A question, not a command.** And it is a posture that will be repeated across the whole sūra: **the decision is left to the one addressed.**

---

## 11/15-16 — مَن كَانَ يُرِيدُ ٱلْحَيَوٰةَ ٱلدُّنْيَا وَزِينَتَهَا نُوَفِّ إِلَيْهِمْ أَعْمَٰلَهُمْ فِيهَا وَهُمْ فِيهَا لَا يُبْخَسُونَ · أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ لَيْسَ لَهُمْ فِى ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ إِلَّا ٱلنَّارُ وَحَبِطَ مَا صَنَعُوا۟ فِيهَا وَبَٰطِلٌ مَّا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

*man kāna yurīdu'l-ḥayāta'l-dunyā wa zīnatahā nuwaffi ilayhim aʿmālahum fīhā wa hum fīhā lā yubkhasūn · ulāʾika'lladhīna laysa lahum fi'l-ākhirati illā'l-nār, wa ḥabiṭa mā ṣanaʿū fīhā wa bāṭilun mā kānū yaʿmalūn*

"**Whoever desires the life of this world and its ornament, We pay them in full there for their deeds, and there they are not given short measure.** · **Those are the ones who have nothing in the hereafter but the Fire. What they made there has come to nothing, and what they used to do is void.**"

### Two roots: و-ف-ي and ب-خ-س

**Two roots come side by side in the verse, and both belong to the field of measure — the words of the Shuʿayb section later in the sūra.**

The root **و-ف-ي**: **to be complete, to give without shortfall.** **The root has been treated in many places across this series** (`017-isra.md` 17/34-35, `083-mutaffifin.md`, `053-necm.md`). *Nuwaffi* — **We pay in full.**

The root **ب-خ-س**: **to take a thing by lowering its value, to give less than its due.** **The root was worked out in `083-mutaffifin.md` and treated again at `026-suara.md` 26/183. I rely on that treatment.**

**And what must be recorded is this: these two roots will come back together, ninety-five verses later, in Shuʿayb's mouth.**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **15** | *nu**waffi** ilayhim aʿmālahum · lā yu**bkhasūn*** | **God** — about the return in this world |
| **85** | *a**wfū**'l-mikyāla wa'l-mīzāna bi'l-qisṭ · wa lā ta**bkhasū**'l-nāsa ashyāʾahum* | **Shuʿayb** — about buying and selling in the market |

**The same two roots, in the same order, in two distinct fields.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verbal overlap between the two verses:** the sūra tells of the return in this world **in the language of measure** — God gives no one short measure. **And what Shuʿayb tells his people is that a human being should imitate that measure.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### حَبِطَ — root ح-ب-ط

**The concrete sense of the root is recorded by the lexicographers and is striking: *ḥabiṭati'l-ibil* — the camel eats a harmful plant, its belly swells and it dies.** *Ḥabṭ*, that is, is **a ruin that looks from outside like fullness.**

**I ascribe this to the lexicographers;** the word's use in the Qurʾān is **the invalidation of a deed.**

**وَبَٰطِلٌ مَّا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ** — root **ب-ط-ل**: **to come to nothing, to be void.**

**The difference between the two words is worth recording:** *ḥabiṭa* is a verb (it happened), *bāṭil* is a noun (it is so). **A deed first came to nothing, and then it is said that it was void all along.** This is an observation about the word order.

---

## 11/17 — أَفَمَن كَانَ عَلَىٰ بَيِّنَةٍ مِّن رَّبِّهِۦ وَيَتْلُوهُ شَاهِدٌ مِّنْهُ وَمِن قَبْلِهِۦ كِتَٰبُ مُوسَىٰٓ إِمَامًا وَرَحْمَةً أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ يُؤْمِنُونَ بِهِۦ وَمَن يَكْفُرْ بِهِۦ مِنَ ٱلْأَحْزَابِ فَٱلنَّارُ مَوْعِدُهُۥ فَلَا تَكُ فِى مِرْيَةٍ مِّنْهُ إِنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ مِن رَّبِّكَ وَلَٰكِنَّ أَكْثَرَ ٱلنَّاسِ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ

*a-fa-man kāna ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbihī wa yatlūhu shāhidun minhu wa min qablihī kitābu Mūsā imāman wa raḥmah, ulāʾika yuʾminūna bih, wa man yakfur bihī mina'l-aḥzābi fa'l-nāru mawʿiduh, fa-lā taku fī miryatin minh, innahu'l-ḥaqqu min rabbik, wa lākinna akthara'l-nāsi lā yuʾminūn*

"**Is one who stands on a clear proof from his Lord, followed by a witness from Him, and before it the Book of Moses as a leader and a mercy [like one who does not]?** Those believe in it. **And whoever of the factions denies it, the Fire is his appointed place.** So be not in doubt about it; it is the truth from your Lord — but most people do not believe."

### The grammatical knot of the verse

**The sentence begins with a question, but the second half of the question is never spoken.** *a-fa-man kāna ʿalā bayyina…* — "one who stands on a clear proof from his Lord…" **and the side he is to be compared with has been elided.**

**The completion supplied in the classical sources is this: "…like one who desires the life of this world?"** — that is, it attaches to the verses just before (15-16). **This is a supplied reading; the verse does not say it in so many words.**

### بَيِّنَة — one of the key words of the sūra

The root **ب-ي-ن**: **to separate two things; and from there, clarity.** **The root was worked out at `026-suara.md` 26/2 (*al-kitābu'l-mubīn*); I do not repeat it here.**

**The word *bayyina* occurs four times in Hūd, and all four describe the standing of a prophet or of a believer:**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **17** | A believer | *ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbih* |
| **28** | **Noah** | *in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī* |
| **63** | **Ṣāliḥ** | *in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī* |
| **88** | **Shuʿayb** | *in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī* |

**And it occurs once more in the mouth of the other side, negated: *mā jiʾtanā bi-bayyina* — "you have brought us no clear proof" (53, the people of Hūd).**

**Five occurrences, one table.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text, and it belongs to the backbone of the sūra: **the two sides are divided by whether or not they hold a *bayyina*.**

### وَيَتْلُوهُ شَاهِدٌ مِّنْهُ — who is who

**Over the referents of the two pronouns in the sentence there is a wide disagreement in the classical sources. I give it in a table and impose no preference.**

| Reading | Who *man kāna ʿalā bayyina* is | Who the *shāhid* is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The Prophet** | **Gabriel (Jibrīl)** or the Qurʾān |
| 2 | **The Prophet** | **The Qurʾān itself** |
| 3 | **The believers** | The Qurʾān |
| 4 | **The Prophet** | Someone who bears witness to him |

**All four are transmitted.** **A note, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** sectarian arguments have also been conducted over this verse throughout history; **in this commentary no side is taken and the argument is not opened.**

**What is to be recorded is the work the sentence *does*:** three supports are listed — **a clear proof, a witness, an earlier book.** **The verse, that is, points not to a single source but to three testimonies laid one over another.**

### كِتَٰبُ مُوسَىٰٓ إِمَامًا وَرَحْمَةً

**Two circumstantial accusatives (words reporting a state): *imāman* and *raḥmatan*.**

The root **أ-م-م** — *imām*: **the one who stands in front, the one who is followed.** **The root was treated at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/22 and in `075-kiyame.md`.**

**And the same construction occurs in Sūrat al-Aḥqāf as well: *wa min qablihī kitābu Mūsā imāman wa raḥmah* (al-Aḥqāf 46/12) — word for word the same expression.** **That verse was treated in `046-ahkaf.md`.** **The same sentence in two sūras; this is a repetition that can be verified from the text.**

### مِرْيَة — doubt

The root **م-ر-ي**: **to milk a thing; and from there, to wrangle, to be in doubt.** *Mirya* — **doubt, hesitation.**

**The word occurs twice in the sūra: here (17) and in the hundred and ninth verse (*fa-lā taku fī miryatin mimmā yaʿbudu hāʾulāʾ*).** **In both places the command is the same: *fa-lā taku fī mirya*.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

---

## 11/18-22 — وَمَنْ أَظْلَمُ مِمَّنِ ٱفْتَرَىٰ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ كَذِبًا أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ يُعْرَضُونَ عَلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ وَيَقُولُ ٱلْأَشْهَٰدُ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ ٱلَّذِينَ كَذَبُوا۟ عَلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ … أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ خَسِرُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُمْ وَضَلَّ عَنْهُم مَّا كَانُوا۟ يَفْتَرُونَ · لَا جَرَمَ أَنَّهُمْ فِى ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ هُمُ ٱلْأَخْسَرُونَ

*wa man aẓlamu mimmani'ftarā ʿalā'llāhi kadhibā, ulāʾika yuʿraḍūna ʿalā rabbihim wa yaqūlu'l-ashhādu hāʾulāʾi'lladhīna kadhabū ʿalā rabbihim, alā laʿnatu'llāhi ʿalā'l-ẓālimīn · alladhīna yaṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāhi wa yabghūnahā ʿiwajā wa hum bi'l-ākhirati hum kāfirūn · ulāʾika lam yakūnū muʿjizīna fi'l-arḍi wa mā kāna lahum min dūni'llāhi min awliyāʾ, yuḍāʿafu lahumu'l-ʿadhāb, mā kānū yastaṭīʿūna'l-samʿa wa mā kānū yubṣirūn · ulāʾika'lladhīna khasirū anfusahum wa ḍalla ʿanhum mā kānū yaftarūn · lā jarama annahum fi'l-ākhirati humu'l-akhsarūn*

"**And who does greater wrong than one who invents a lie against God? Those will be brought before their Lord, and the witnesses will say, 'These are the ones who lied against their Lord.'** Take note: the curse of God is upon the wrongdoers. · **They are the ones who bar others from the path of God and would have it crooked, and who deny the hereafter.** · **They were never going to frustrate Him on the earth, and they had no protectors besides God. The punishment will be doubled for them. They were not able to hear, and they did not see.** · **Those are the ones who have lost their own selves; and what they invented has gone astray from them.** · **Beyond all doubt, in the hereafter they are the greatest losers.**"

### يَبْغُونَهَا عِوَجًا — to make the path crooked

The root **ع-و-ج**: **crookedness.** The lexicographers record two vocalisations: ***ʿiwaj*** (with kasra) **is crookedness in things not seen** — a religion, a word, a disposition; ***ʿawaj*** (with fatḥa) **is crookedness in things that are seen** — a wall, a tree.

**And the word here is *ʿiwajan*, that is, crookedness in the unseen field.** This is a note about the language.

**And the verb must be recorded: *yabghūnahā* — "they seek it, they work for it".** **The crookedness, that is, is not there in the path of itself; it is being sought.**

### مَا كَانُوا۟ يَسْتَطِيعُونَ ٱلسَّمْعَ — not being able to hear

**This expression belongs to a field that recurs across this series.** **The proof from "do they hear?" was treated at `041-fussilet.md` 41/5 (*fī ādhāninā waqr*) and in `046-ahkaf.md` and `035-fatir.md`. I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded, and it is an observation about the language: the sentence does not say *mā kānū yasmaʿūn* ("they did not hear"); it says *mā kānū yastaṭīʿūna'l-samʿ* — "they were *not able* to hear".**

**The insertion of the verb of ability (*istiṭāʿa*) deepens the negation by one degree:** the matter is not a failure to hear but **the loss of the very possibility of hearing.**

**And it can be compared with the pattern in the fifteenth verse:** there the return in this world was given **in full**; here the capacity to hear is **used up.** This is a contrast, and it can be read from the text.

### لَا جَرَمَ — the pattern itself

***lā jarama* is a pattern of emphasis in Arabic: "beyond all doubt, inescapably".**

The root **ج-ر-م**: **to cut, to sever; and from there, a crime.** **The root has been treated in many places across this series** (`026-suara.md`, `037-saffat.md`, `054-kamer.md`, `083-mutaffifin.md`). **The lexicographers explain the pattern *lā jarama* as "definitively, inescapably"** — the idea of "cutting" in the root has become the cutting-off of the argument.

**And the same root will occur once more in the sūra, in a wholly different office: *wa lā yajrimannakum shiqāqī* (89, Shuʿayb).** **The two occurrences do different work and that one will be treated separately in its place.**

### ٱلْأَخْسَرُونَ — the elative

The root **خ-س-ر**: **to diminish, to suffer loss.** **The root was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/181 (*al-mukhsirīn*).**

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: in the twenty-first verse a verb (*khasirū anfusahum*), in the twenty-second an elative (*al-akhsarūn*).** First the act, then the degree.

---

## 11/23-24 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَعَمِلُوا۟ ٱلصَّٰلِحَٰتِ وَأَخْبَتُوٓا۟ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ أَصْحَٰبُ ٱلْجَنَّةِ هُمْ فِيهَا خَٰلِدُونَ · مَثَلُ ٱلْفَرِيقَيْنِ كَٱلْأَعْمَىٰ وَٱلْأَصَمِّ وَٱلْبَصِيرِ وَٱلسَّمِيعِ هَلْ يَسْتَوِيَانِ مَثَلًا أَفَلَا تَذَكَّرُونَ

*inna'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿamilū'l-ṣāliḥāti wa akhbatū ilā rabbihim ulāʾika aṣḥābu'l-janna, hum fīhā khālidūn · mathalu'l-farīqayni ka'l-aʿmā wa'l-aṣammi wa'l-baṣīri wa'l-samīʿ, hal yastawiyāni mathalā, a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn*

"**Those who believe, who do righteous deeds, and who humble themselves before their Lord — those are the people of the Garden; there they remain.** · **The likeness of the two parties is that of the blind and the deaf, and of the seeing and the hearing. Are the two alike as a likeness? Will you not then take heed?**"

### أَخْبَتُوا۟ — root خ-ب-ت

**The root occurs here for the first time in this series, and its concrete sense must be recorded.**

The original sense of the root **خ-ب-ت** is given by the lexicographers thus: ***khabt* — low ground, a hollow, level land.** A place that lies **lower** than what surrounds it.

**And the verb is Form IV: *akhbata* — "to sink low, to bow, to come to stillness".**

| Derivative | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **خَبْت** | Low level ground, a hollow tract |
| **أَخْبَتَ** | He sank low, he bowed |
| **مُخْبِت** | One who bows, who yields from the heart |

**The picture in the word is worth recording, and I give it as an observation about the language:** submission is told here by way of **a landform** — ground lying lower than what is around it. **And low ground has one property: water gathers there.**

**I record this connection as my own reading, and it is not binding;** the verse draws no such conclusion. What is recorded is the concrete picture the root carries.

***Mukhbit* also occurs in the Qurʾān at al-Ḥajj 22/34-35** (*wa bashshiri'l-mukhbitīn*) **and was treated in `022-hac.md`.**
