### Two groups, four words

**The twenty-fourth verse closes the first block with a likeness. And the likeness is built from four words:**

| Faculty | Negative | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Sight** | *al-aʿmā* — the blind | *al-baṣīr* — the seeing |
| **Hearing** | *al-aṣamm* — the deaf | *al-samīʿ* — the hearing |

**And the arrangement must be recorded: the two negatives first, the two positives after.** The verse does not cross them as *aʿmā–baṣīr, aṣamm–samīʿ*; **it lists the two lacks first and the two intact senses after.**

**A similar series was treated at `035-fatir.md` 35/19-23** (blind–seeing, darknesses–light, shade–heat, the living–the dead). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here: in Fāṭir there were four separate pairs; here a single human being is described by two of his senses.**

**And the choice of these two senses ties back to the verse just before in the sūra:** in the twentieth verse it was said *mā kānū yastaṭīʿūna'l-samʿa wa mā kānū yubṣirūn* — **the same two senses.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**هَلْ يَسْتَوِيَانِ مَثَلًا** — the question is in the **dual** (*yastawiyāni*), because what is compared is **two groups**, not four persons. This is a grammatical note.

---

## The Noah narrative — 11/25-49 (introduction)

**This is the most detailed telling of the Noah narrative in the Qurʾān: twenty-five verses.**

**The comparative table was set up in `026-suara.md`, and I bring it here with one column added:**

| Sūra | Number of verses | What it brings forward | Where it was treated |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Nūḥ 71** | 28 | **The method of the call, the length of time, the prayer** | `071-nuh.md` |
| **Hūd 11/25-49** | **25** | **The dispute, the ark, the son, the prayer and the answer** | **here** |
| **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/105-122** | 18 | The objection "the lowest sort have followed you" | `026-suara.md` |
| **al-Ṣāffāt 37/75-82** | 8 | The deliverance and his progeny | `037-saffat.md` |
| **al-Qamar 54/9-16** | 8 | **The flood itself** | `054-kamer.md` |
| **al-ʿAnkabūt 29/14-15** | 2 | The length of time and the deliverance | `029-ankebut.md` |

**Hūd, that is, gives both the longest telling of the Noah narrative and the one with the most speech in it.**

**Per the rule — I record it explicitly here:** in this section **no detail the Qurʾān does not give will be entered into.** The dimensions of the ark, how long it took to build, the number of those aboard, the name of Noah's son, the geographical extent of the flood — **none of this is in the Qurʾān.** In the classical commentaries there are **reports of the *Isrāʾīliyyāt* kind that fill these gaps; this commentary does not enter into them** (`STYLE.md`). **Where the text is silent, silence is kept.**

---

## 11/25-26 — وَلَقَدْ أَرْسَلْنَا نُوحًا إِلَىٰ قَوْمِهِۦٓ إِنِّى لَكُمْ نَذِيرٌ مُّبِينٌ · أَن لَّا تَعْبُدُوٓا۟ إِلَّا ٱللَّهَ إِنِّىٓ أَخَافُ عَلَيْكُمْ عَذَابَ يَوْمٍ أَلِيمٍ

*wa laqad arsalnā Nūḥan ilā qawmihī innī lakum nadhīrun mubīn · allā taʿbudū illā'llāh, innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin alīm*

"**And We sent Noah to his people: 'I am to you a plain warner. · Serve none but God. I fear for you the punishment of a painful day.'**"

### The pattern of the opening — the difference from al-Shuʿarāʾ

**In `026-suara.md` the shared opening of five narratives was tabulated: *kadhdhabat … al-mursalīn* + *idh qāla lahum akhūhum … a-lā tattaqūn* + the threefold introduction.**

**The opening of Hūd is different, and the difference can be counted:**

| Element | **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/105-109** | **Hūd 11/25-26** |
|---|---|---|
| **The announcement of denial** | *kadhdhabat qawmu Nūḥini'l-mursalīn* (105) | **None** |
| ***akhūhum*** | present (106) | **None** |
| ***a-lā tattaqūn*** | present (106) | **None** |
| **Self-introduction** | *innī lakum rasūlun **amīn*** (107) | *innī lakum **nadhīrun mubīn*** (25) |
| **The sentence about payment** | 109 — at once | **29 — four verses later** |
| **The content of the command** | *fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn* (108) | ***allā taʿbudū illā'llāh*** (26) |
| **An expression of fear** | none | ***innī akhāfu ʿalaykum*** (26) |

**Three data points must be recorded:**

**One — in Hūd, Noah introduces himself not as *rasūlun amīn* but as *nadhīrun mubīn*.** The two adjectives differ: *amīn* reports trustworthiness, *mubīn* reports clarity.

**Two — in Hūd the content of the command is given (*allā taʿbudū illā'llāh*); in al-Shuʿarāʾ it is not** (*fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn* — be wary and obey). **Hūd, that is, states the *content* of the call.**

**Three — the sentence *innī akhāfu ʿalaykum* does not occur in al-Shuʿarāʾ at all.** In Hūd it occurs **twice:** in Noah's mouth (26) and in Shuʿayb's (84). **The messenger is afraid on behalf of the one he addresses.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

**And the sentence *allā taʿbudū illā'llāh* is word for word the same as the second verse of the sūra.** The first sentence placed in Noah's mouth, that is, is the sūra's own opening. This was tabulated in the section on the backbone.

---

## 11/27 — فَقَالَ ٱلْمَلَأُ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ مِن قَوْمِهِۦ مَا نَرَىٰكَ إِلَّا بَشَرًا مِّثْلَنَا وَمَا نَرَىٰكَ ٱتَّبَعَكَ إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ أَرَاذِلُنَا بَادِىَ ٱلرَّأْىِ وَمَا نَرَىٰ لَكُمْ عَلَيْنَا مِن فَضْلٍۭ بَلْ نَظُنُّكُمْ كَٰذِبِينَ

*fa-qāla'l-malaʾu'lladhīna kafarū min qawmihī mā narāka illā basharan mithlanā wa mā narāka'ttabaʿaka illā'lladhīna hum arādhilunā bādiya'l-raʾyi wa mā narā lakum ʿalaynā min faḍl, bal naẓunnukum kādhibīn*

"**The leading men of his people who disbelieved said: 'We see you as nothing but a human being like ourselves. Nor do we see any following you but the lowest among us, [and that] at first glance. Nor do we see in you any superiority over us. On the contrary, we think you are liars.'**"

### Three occurrences of *mā narāka* — one verb, three objections

**The word order of the verse must be recorded, and it is a datum of the text: the same verb (*narā* — we see) three times, and all three negated.**

| # | Expression | The subject of the objection |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *mā narā**ka** illā basharan mithlanā* | **The messenger himself** — his kind |
| 2 | *mā narā**ka**'ttabaʿaka illā'lladhīna hum arādhilunā* | **Those who follow him** — their standing |
| 3 | *mā narā **lakum** ʿalaynā min faḍl* | **Superiority as a whole** — the entire group |

**And the verb the three share is *raʾā*: "to see".** **The whole of the objection, that is, rests on a claim about *seeing*** — not on a proof but on an observation.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the threefold repetition of the verb:** the people bring no counter-proof; **they make what they see the measure.** **And in the twenty-eighth verse Noah's answer will land exactly here: *fa-ʿummiyat ʿalaykum* — "it was hidden from you".** The answer, that is, recalls the existence of something that cannot be seen.

### ٱلْمَلَأ — who is speaking

The root **م-ل-أ**: **to fill.** *Malaʾ* — **those who fill an assembly; the leading men.** **The word has been treated in many places across this series** (`027-neml.md`, `023-muminun.md`, `038-sad.md`).

**And what must be recorded is this: the one making the objection is the *malaʾ*, not the whole of the people.** **And the subject of the objection is a question of *standing*** — which is consistent with who is speaking.

### بَشَرًا مِّثْلَنَا — the first objection

**This objection recurs again and again in the Qurʾān and has been treated across this series** (`023-muminun.md` 23/24, 23/33; `036-yasin.md` 36/15; `014-ibrahim.md` 14/10; `026-suara.md` 26/154, 26/186). **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is that the objection comes *first*.** In al-Shuʿarāʾ the only objection of Noah's people was the matter of the lowest sort; **in Hūd that objection stands not third but second, and "you are a human being like us" has been placed in front of it.**

### أَرَاذِلُنَا بَادِىَ ٱلرَّأْىِ — the second objection

**This expression was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/111 (*wa'ttabaʿaka'l-ardhalūn*), and I rely on that treatment.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> The root **ر-ذ-ل**: **what is worthless, low, of no use in a thing.** *Ardhal* — an elative: **the lowest, the most worthless.** The Qurʾān uses the same word for the most decrepit stage of life as well: *ardhali'l-ʿumur* (al-Naḥl 16/70; al-Ḥajj 22/5).
>
> **And that the word has a social sense is clear from the telling of the same episode at Hūd 11/27:** *wa mā narāka'ttabaʿaka illā'lladhīna hum arādhilunā **bādiya'l-raʾy*** — "none has followed you but the lowest among us, [and that] at first glance". **The objection, that is, looks at economic and social standing.**
>
> **It is of the same family as the objection recorded at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/31:** *law lā nuzzila hādhā'l-qurʾānu ʿalā rajulin mina'l-qaryatayni ʿaẓīm.*

**The al-Shuʿarāʾ section had already cited this expression of Hūd's. Now the second half of the expression needs working out: *bādiya'l-raʾy*.**

### بَادِىَ ٱلرَّأْىِ — the construction that is disputed

**The construction consists of two words, and in each of them there is a question of reading.**

The root **ب-د-و**: **to appear, to come into view.** **The root was treated in `022-hac.md`, `024-nur.md`, `033-ahzab.md` and `060-mumtehine.md`.** *Bādī* — **what appears, what is out in the open.**

The root **ب-د-أ**: **to begin.** *Bādiʾ* — **what is at the beginning, the first.**

**The variant reading lies here, and it bears on the sense:**

| Reading | Root | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| ***bādiya*** (without hamza) | **ب-د-و** — to appear | **In appearance, by outward look** |
| ***bādiʾa*** (with hamza) | **ب-د-أ** — to begin | **At the first moment, without reflection, at first glance** |

**Both readings are transmitted in the sources for the variant readings; I name no reciter and I do not impose a preference.**

**And whom the construction belongs to has also been disputed:**

| Reading | Whom *bādiya'l-raʾy* qualifies |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Those who follow** — they are shallow of judgement, superficial people |
| 2 | **Those who object** — this is how we see them at first glance |

**Both readings are transmitted.** **But the point both readings arrive at is the same, and I record this as my own reading:** the measure is **the surface.** Either the judgement of the followers is counted superficial, or the gaze of the objectors stops at the surface.

**And this overlaps with the verb *narā*, repeated three times in the verse:** the whole of the objection is built out of **what is visible.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### بَلْ نَظُنُّكُمْ كَٰذِبِينَ — the third objection

***bal* (the particle of retraction, *iḍrāb*) was treated at `050-kaf.md` 50/2 and at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/22; I rely on that treatment.**

**And the verb must be recorded: *naẓunnukum* — "we think".** The root **ظ-ن-ن**: **conjecture, an opinion held without certainty.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the first two of the three objections were built with *narā* (we see) — a claim to observation. **The third is built with *naẓunn* (we think) — an opinion.** **The verse thereby shows, in the objection's own language, how weak it is:** even in the last word there is no claim to certainty.

---

## 11/28 — قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ أَرَءَيْتُمْ إِن كُنتُ عَلَىٰ بَيِّنَةٍ مِّن رَّبِّى وَءَاتَىٰنِى رَحْمَةً مِّنْ عِندِهِۦ فَعُمِّيَتْ عَلَيْكُمْ أَنُلْزِمُكُمُوهَا وَأَنتُمْ لَهَا كَٰرِهُونَ

*qāla yā qawmi a-raʾaytum in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī wa ātānī raḥmatan min ʿindihī fa-ʿummiyat ʿalaykum, a-nulzimukumūhā wa antum lahā kārihūn*

"He said: '**My people! Tell me: what if I stand on a clear proof from my Lord, and He has given me a mercy from Himself, and it has been hidden from you? Are we to force it upon you when you have no wish for it?**'"

### يَٰقَوْمِ — the address of the sūra

**This address occurs sixteen times in the sūra** (tabulated in the section on the backbone) **and does not occur at all in al-Shuʿarāʾ.**

The root **ق-و-م**: **to stand upright.** *Qawm* — the body of people who stand at the head of an affair. **And in the construction *yā qawmi* the *yāʾ* of the possessive pronoun (*ī*) has been dropped and the word shortened: "my people."**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the messenger calls the very body of people who are denying him **"my people".** **Belonging is not undone by opposition.**

### أَرَءَيْتُمْ — a question pattern that makes one think

***a-raʾaytum* in Arabic does not mean "did you see?" but "tell me now, what do you say?"** **The pattern has been treated across this series** (`025-furkan.md` 25/43, `053-necm.md` 53/33, `096-alak.md` 96/9, `107-maun.md` 107/1).

**And the work the pattern does here must be recorded: it places the other side *inside a supposition*.** "And what if it were so?" — no answer is wanted; **what is wanted is that he think.**

**And this pattern occurs in the sūra in word-for-word the same sentence in the mouths of three prophets** (28, 63, 88). This was tabulated in the section on the backbone.

### فَعُمِّيَتْ عَلَيْكُمْ — the direction of the blindness

The root **ع-م-ي**: **blindness.** **And the verb here is passive and in Form II: *ʿummiyat* — "it was made hidden, made closed".**

**What must be recorded is this, and it is a grammatical observation: the verb is used of the *mercy*, not of the one addressed.** The verse does not say "you went blind"; **it says "it remained closed to you".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the subject of the verb:** the sentence describes the situation without naming the one addressed with a fault. **And this is consistent with the refusal of compulsion in the next sentence.**

**A variant reading is transmitted:** it is also reported that the verb is read *ʿammaytuhā* ("I closed it", as it were); **the widespread reading is the passive.** **I do not go into the detail, because the sense of the widespread reading is given above.**

### أَنُلْزِمُكُمُوهَا وَأَنتُمْ لَهَا كَٰرِهُونَ — one of the most important sentences in the sūra

The root **ل-ز-م**: **to cling to a thing, to be inseparable from it.** *Ilzām* — **to make a thing obligatory for someone, to stick it onto him.**

**And the inflection of the verb is grammatically striking: *a-nulzimukum-ū-hā*.** **Two object pronouns** are joined onto one verb: *-kum* (you) and *-hā* (it). **The *ū* between them is a connecting letter that comes in to hold the two pronouns apart.** This is a combination rarely met with in Arabic, and it makes the word heavy.

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the heaviness of the word matches the heaviness of the act it describes — **to force two things to stick together.**

### The refusal of compulsion — its place across this series

**This sentence is one of the plainest statements of a principle the Qurʾān repeats, and it has been treated in many places across this series:**

| Verse | Expression | Where it was treated |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/4** | *in nashaʾ nunazzil ʿalayhim mina'l-samāʾi āyatan fa-ẓallat aʿnāquhum lahā khāḍiʿīn* | `026-suara.md` — **compulsion is possible, and is not chosen** |
| **Yūnus 10/99** | *a-fa-anta tukrihu'l-nāsa ḥattā yakūnū muʾminīn* | — |
| **al-Ghāshiya 88/22** | *lasta ʿalayhim bi-musayṭir* | `088-gasiye.md` |
| **Qāf 50/45** | *wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-jabbār* | `050-kaf.md` |
| **Hūd 11/28** | ***a-nulzimukumūhā wa antum lahā kārihūn*** | **here** |

**And what Hūd adds must be recorded: here the sentence is framed not by God but by *the messenger himself*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** in the other verses the limit was **announced** to the messenger; here the messenger **states the limit himself.** **Noah, that is, knows it before he is told.**

**And the circumstantial clause at the end of the sentence must be recorded: *wa antum lahā kārihūn* — "when you have no wish for it".** The root **ك-ر-ه**: **to find distasteful, not to want.**

**The refusal of compulsion, that is, is tied to the condition of the addressee's *unwillingness*.** The sentence does not say "nothing can be made acceptable to anyone"; it says **"it cannot be forced on one who does not want it."** This is a note about the wording.

### What this says to the present

**The sentence draws the line between persuasion and imposition by way of a question. And the one to whom the question is put is not the side that thinks its hand is strong — it is the messenger himself.**

**I record this as a reading:** believing that a view is true does not confer the authority to impose it. **Noah holds a *bayyina*; even so he asks the question.**

---

## 11/29-31 — وَيَٰقَوْمِ لَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مَالًا إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَى ٱللَّهِ وَمَآ أَنَا۠ بِطَارِدِ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ … وَيَٰقَوْمِ مَن يَنصُرُنِى مِنَ ٱللَّهِ إِن طَرَدتُّهُمْ أَفَلَا تَذَكَّرُونَ · وَلَآ أَقُولُ لَكُمْ عِندِى خَزَآئِنُ ٱللَّهِ وَلَآ أَعْلَمُ ٱلْغَيْبَ وَلَآ أَقُولُ إِنِّى مَلَكٌ وَلَآ أَقُولُ لِلَّذِينَ تَزْدَرِىٓ أَعْيُنُكُمْ لَن يُؤْتِيَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ خَيْرًا

*wa yā qawmi lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi mālā, in ajriya illā ʿalā'llāh, wa mā anā bi-ṭāridi'lladhīna āmanū, innahum mulāqū rabbihim wa lākinnī arākum qawman tajhalūn · wa yā qawmi man yanṣurunī mina'llāhi in ṭaradtuhum, a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn · wa lā aqūlu lakum ʿindī khazāʾinu'llāhi wa lā aʿlamu'l-ghayba wa lā aqūlu innī malakun wa lā aqūlu li'lladhīna tazdarī aʿyunukum lan yuʾtiyahumu'llāhu khayrā, Allāhu aʿlamu bimā fī anfusihim, innī idhan la-mina'l-ẓālimīn*

"'**My people! I ask you no wealth for this; my payment is due from God alone. And I am not going to drive away those who have believed; they will meet their Lord. But I see that you are a people who behave in ignorance.** · **My people! If I drove them away, who would help me against God? Will you not take heed?** · **I do not say to you that the treasuries of God are with me; nor do I know the unseen. I do not say "I am an angel". Nor do I say of those whom your eyes look down on that God will never give them any good. God knows best what is in their souls. If I did, I would indeed be one of the wrongdoers.**'"

### The sentence about payment — the difference from al-Shuʿarāʾ

**In Table 5 of `026-suara.md` it was tabulated that this sentence is word for word the same in five narratives:** *wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin, in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* (109, 127, 145, 164, 180).

**In Hūd the sentence occurs twice, and both differ from the one in al-Shuʿarāʾ:**

| Place | Expression | What is not asked for | Whose the payment is |
|---|---|---|---|
| **al-Shuʿarāʾ (5 times)** | *mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min **ajr*** | **Payment** | *rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* |
| **Hūd 11/29 (Noah)** | *lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi **mālā*** | **Wealth** | *Allāh* |
| **Hūd 11/51 (Hūd)** | *lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi **ajrā*** | **Payment** | *alladhī faṭaranī* |

**Three different words, three different attributions. This is a difference that can be verified from the text.**

**And the *mālā* in Noah's mouth is worth recording: the word is concrete.** *Ajr* is the return for a service; *māl* is **wealth outright.** **And the side making the objection was the *malaʾ*** — that is, those who had wealth and standing. The answer is given in the language of the objection.

### وَمَآ أَنَا۠ بِطَارِدِ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟

**This sentence was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/114 (*wa mā anā bi-ṭāridi'l-muʾminīn*), and I rely on that treatment.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> The root **ط-ر-د**: **to drive off, to expel, to banish.**
>
> **The word order of the sentence: the *mā … bi-* pattern.** In Arabic the *bāʾ* that comes onto the predicate of a negating *mā* **strengthens the negation**: "I am never going to drive them away."
>
> **And the object must be recorded: the people had called them *al-ardhalūn* ("the lowest sort"); Noah names the very same people *al-muʾminīn*.**
>
> **The Qurʾān takes this matter up elsewhere as well:** *wa lā taṭrudi'lladhīna yadʿūna rabbahum bi'l-ghadāti wa'l-ʿashiyy* (al-Anʿām 6/52) — the same root, the same prohibition. **And another face of the matter was treated in `080-abese.md`.**

**Hūd adds two things, and neither is present in al-Shuʿarāʾ:**

**One — the naming changes: in al-Shuʿarāʾ *al-muʾminīn*, in Hūd *alladhīna āmanū*.** The second is a verbal clause: **"those who have believed"** — not an attribute but a thing done.

**Two — Hūd adds a *ground*: *innahum mulāqū rabbihim*** — "they will meet their Lord." **Not driving them away, that is, is founded not on human delicacy but on a piece of knowledge about a meeting.**

**And the thirtieth verse turns this into a question: *man yanṣurunī mina'llāhi in ṭaradtuhum* — "if I drove them away, who would help me against God?"**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the two verses:** in al-Shuʿarāʾ not driving them away was given as an **attitude**; **in Hūd it is given as a *risk*.** The two verses do not conflict; Hūd opens up the ground of the same attitude.

### Four negative sentences — 11/31

**The thirty-first verse is the longest list of negations a prophet frames about himself in the Qurʾān. *lā aqūlu* / *lā aʿlamu* occurs four times.**

| # | Expression | The claim refused |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *lā aqūlu lakum ʿindī **khazāʾinu'llāh*** | **Wealth / ownership of the sources** |
| 2 | *wa lā **aʿlamu'l-ghayb*** | **Knowing the hidden** |
| 3 | *wa lā aqūlu innī **malak*** | **Standing above his own kind** |
| 4 | *wa lā aqūlu li'lladhīna tazdarī aʿyunukum **lan yuʾtiyahumu'llāhu khayrā*** | **Passing judgement on another's behalf** |

**And the first three of the list correspond exactly to the people's demands. This is an overlap that can be verified from the text:**

| The people's demand / objection | Noah's refusal |
|---|---|
| *law lā unzila ʿalayhi **kanz*** (12 — in the sūra's own section) | *ʿindī **khazāʾinu'llāh*** — I have none |
| *aw jāʾa maʿahū **malak*** (12) | *innī **malak*** — I am not |
| *mā narāka illā **basharan mithlanā*** (27) | Confirmed — not contested but conceded |

**Noah, that is, refuses one by one the attributes expected of him.**

**And the same threefold list occurs once more in the Qurʾān, in the mouth of the Prophet:** *qul lā aqūlu lakum ʿindī khazāʾinu'llāhi wa lā aʿlamu'l-ghayba wa lā aqūlu lakum innī malak* (al-Anʿām 6/50). **The same three sentences word for word, in the mouths of two different prophets.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

### تَزْدَرِىٓ أَعْيُنُكُمْ — the weight of the fourth sentence

The root **ز-ر-ي**: **to belittle, to look down on, to find fault with.** **The root occurs here for the first time in this series.** *Izdarā* (Form VIII) — **to hold in low regard.**

**And the choice of subject must be recorded: *tazdarī aʿyunukum* — "your eyes look down on".** Not the person who looks down, but **the eye.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and I tie it to the twenty-seventh verse:** the people had said *mā narā* ("we do not see") three times. **Noah puts the very same organ in the place of the subject.** **The sentence, that is, names the source of the objection: the gaze.**

### The subject of the fourth refusal — passing judgement on another

**And what the fourth sentence refuses is different from the first three: not an *authority* but a *judgement*.**

*lan yuʾtiyahumu'llāhu khayrā* — "God will never give them any good." **Noah refuses to say this.**

**And the ground is given at once: *Allāhu aʿlamu bimā fī anfusihim* — "God knows best what is in their souls".**

**This sentence is on the same line as the answer treated at `026-suara.md` 26/112 (*wa mā ʿilmī bimā kānū yaʿmalūn*), and I rely on that treatment.** The entry there ran: *"Noah does not defend those who believe. He does not say 'they are good people'. He says 'I do not know what they have been doing'. The answer refuses the ground the objection rests on."*

**In Hūd the same posture goes one step further, and I record this as my own reading:** in al-Shuʿarāʾ Noah said that he **did not know**; **in Hūd he refuses, because he does not know, to *pass judgement*.** The two are two faces of the same principle.

**And the sentence ends with a warning: *innī idhan la-mina'l-ẓālimīn* — "I would then indeed be one of the wrongdoers."** **To pass judgement on another's behalf about what God will give is thus named, in the language of the verse, *wrongdoing*.**

### What this says to the present

**The thirty-first verse lists four things a man who calls others will not allow to be attributed to him: ownership of wealth, knowledge of the hidden, standing above his own kind, and speaking about the final outcome of other people.**

**I record this as a reading, and its ground is the list itself:** all four are attributes that the one addressed might wish to load onto the caller, or that the caller might load onto himself. **And all four are refused.**

---

## 11/32-34 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰنُوحُ قَدْ جَٰدَلْتَنَا فَأَكْثَرْتَ جِدَٰلَنَا فَأْتِنَا بِمَا تَعِدُنَآ إِن كُنتَ مِنَ ٱلصَّٰدِقِينَ · قَالَ إِنَّمَا يَأْتِيكُم بِهِ ٱللَّهُ إِن شَآءَ وَمَآ أَنتُم بِمُعْجِزِينَ · وَلَا يَنفَعُكُمْ نُصْحِىٓ إِنْ أَرَدتُّ أَنْ أَنصَحَ لَكُمْ إِن كَانَ ٱللَّهُ يُرِيدُ أَن يُغْوِيَكُمْ هُوَ رَبُّكُمْ وَإِلَيْهِ تُرْجَعُونَ

*qālū yā Nūḥu qad jādaltanā fa-aktharta jidālanā fa'tinā bimā taʿidunā in kunta mina'l-ṣādiqīn · qāla innamā yaʾtīkum bihi'llāhu in shāʾa wa mā antum bi-muʿjizīn · wa lā yanfaʿukum nuṣḥī in aradtu an anṣaḥa lakum in kāna'llāhu yurīdu an yughwiyakum, huwa rabbukum wa ilayhi turjaʿūn*

"They said: '**Noah! You have disputed with us, and you have disputed with us at length. Bring us then what you promise us, if you are of the truthful!**' · He said: '**Only God will bring it to you, if He wills; and you are not going to frustrate Him.** · **If God wills to let you go astray, my counsel will not profit you, however much I wish to counsel you. He is your Lord, and to Him you are returned.**'"

### جَٰدَلْتَنَا فَأَكْثَرْتَ جِدَٰلَنَا — the root ج-د-ل, first occurrence

**The root was the backbone of `040-gafir.md`, and five occurrences were tabulated there.** The concrete sense of the root — **to twist a rope tight**, to throw an opponent in wrestling — **was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/197.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**The measure recorded in Ghāfir was this:** *"What is condemned is not disputation itself but its being carried on without any ground — bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum."*

**And the root occurs twice in Hūd, in the mouths of two different sides:**

| Verse | Who disputes | Who names it | The assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **32** | **Noah** | **His people** — as a complaint | The people count disputation a **fault** |
| **74** | **Abraham** | **The verse itself** | **Not condemned** — on the contrary, praised with three attributes |

**This is one of the most striking contrasts in the sūra, and it will be treated in detail at 11/74-76.**

**The entry that belongs here is this: in the thirty-second verse the side that counts disputation a *fault* is the people.** The verse does not join in that assessment; **it merely transmits it as having been said.**

**And the form of the verb must be recorded: *jādalta* — Form III (*mufāʿala*), which reports reciprocity.** The people thereby concede that they too are inside the dispute.

### فَأْتِنَا بِمَا تَعِدُنَآ — the demand that the punishment be hastened

**This demand has been treated in many places across this series** (`013-rad.md` 13/6, `029-ankebut.md` 29/53, `034-sebe.md` 34/29, `037-saffat.md` 37/176, `026-suara.md`). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded: the demand is tied to the *lengthening* of the dispute.** *qad jādaltanā fa-aktharta jidālanā* → *fa'tinā.* **Speech, that is, is counted finished, and the event is asked for.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the demand is not curiosity but **an attempt to close a dispute.**

### إِنَّمَا يَأْتِيكُم بِهِ ٱللَّهُ إِن شَآءَ — the limit of the answer

**Noah does not take to himself the authority to bring the punishment.** **The pattern of restriction (*innamā*) and the condition (*in shāʾa*) come together.**

**And this is the same limit as *innamā anta nadhīr* in the twelfth verse.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### لَا يَنفَعُكُمْ نُصْحِى — the weight of the thirty-fourth verse

The root **ن-ص-ح**. The concrete sense the lexicographers record is worth setting down: ***naṣaḥa* is used in two senses — the first is "to be pure, unmixed"** (*ʿasalun nāṣiḥ* — pure honey), **the second "to sew, to mend a tear"** (*naṣaḥa'l-thawb* — he sewed up the garment).

| Concrete sense | The side of it that bears on *naṣīḥa* |
|---|---|
| **To be pure** | That there is no admixture, no trick, no hidden intent in the counsel |
| **To sew up a tear** | That the counsel mends a place that has torn |

**I ascribe both explanations to the lexicographers.** And the word's use in the Qurʾān supports both: **when Noah, Hūd and Ṣāliḥ say to their peoples "I am counselling you", they are saying that they are doing a work of mending.**

**And the root occurs in Sūrat Hūd only in this verse** — twice in the thirty-fourth (*nuṣḥī* and *anṣaḥa*). This is a note about the text.

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: *lā yanfaʿukum nuṣḥī in aradtu an anṣaḥa lakum*** — "my counsel will not profit you **however much I wish** to counsel you". **Noah, that is, ties the effect of the counsel not to his own intention but to another condition.**

### إِن كَانَ ٱللَّهُ يُرِيدُ أَن يُغْوِيَكُمْ — a sentence that needs dwelling on

The root **غ-و-ي**: **to swerve from the right way, to go astray.** **The root was treated at `053-necm.md` 53/2 (*mā ḍalla ṣāḥibukum wa mā ghawā*) and at `026-suara.md` 26/224 (*al-ghāwūn*). I rely on that treatment.**

**And the sentence opens onto a question of divine decree. I enter a note per the method (`STYLE.md`) and I do not open the theological dispute.**

**In the classical sources two main directions are transmitted for reading the sentence:**

| Reading | What *yughwiyakum* reports |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The requital of going astray** — because a man goes astray, he is left in that straying |
| 2 | **Destruction / ruin** — the word's use in the sense of "to bring to ruin" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what is to be recorded is that the sentence is in the *conditional*: *in kāna*.** Noah is not announcing a ruling; **he is putting a possibility into words.** **And the end of the sentence is not a piece of information but a reminder: *huwa rabbukum wa ilayhi turjaʿūn*.**

---

## 11/35 — أَمْ يَقُولُونَ ٱفْتَرَىٰهُ قُلْ إِنِ ٱفْتَرَيْتُهُۥ فَعَلَىَّ إِجْرَامِى وَأَنَا۠ بَرِىٓءٌ مِّمَّا تُجْرِمُونَ

*am yaqūlūna'ftarāh, qul ini'ftaraytuhū fa-ʿalayya ijrāmī wa anā barīʾun mimmā tujrimūn*

"**Or do they say, 'He has invented it'? Say: 'If I have invented it, my crime is upon me; and I am free of the crimes you commit.'**"

### A verse that steps into the middle of the narrative

**This verse stands right in the middle of the Noah narrative, and it changes the addressee: the sentence is spoken not to Noah's people but to those the Prophet addresses.** The command *qul* (say) shows this plainly.

**And the verse opens with a word-for-word repetition of the thirteenth: *am yaqūlūna'ftarāh*.**

| Verse | Expression | The kind of answer |
|---|---|---|
| **13** | *am yaqūlūna'ftarāh* | **A challenge** — bring ten sūras |
| **35** | *am yaqūlūna'ftarāh* | **A division of responsibility** — my crime is mine |

**The same claim, two different answers.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

**And the placing of the verse in the middle of the narrative is worth recording. I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the position of the verse:** the Noah narrative was in the very midst of a scene of denial (32); **the verse steps out of it, turns to the present addressee and cites the same claim.** **The sūra, that is, breaks the impression that the narrative "belongs to the past".**

### فَعَلَىَّ إِجْرَامِى — an application of the principle of burden

The root **ج-ر-م**: **to cut, to sever; and from there, a crime.** *Ijrām* — to commit a crime.

**And the construction of the sentence is an application of the principle of burden treated across this series:**

| Side | Upon whom |
|---|---|
| *ʿalayya* | **My crime upon me** |
| *anā barīʾun mimmā tujrimūn* | **Your crime not upon me** |

**The principle *lā taziru wāziratun wizra ukhrā* was treated at `035-fatir.md` 35/18, `053-necm.md` 53/38, `039-zumer.md` 39/7 and `017-isra.md` 17/15. I rely on that treatment.**

**And this principle will come back in its hardest form in the forty-sixth verse of the sūra — in the matter of Noah's son.** That will be treated separately in its place.

---

## 11/36-37 — وَأُوحِىَ إِلَىٰ نُوحٍ أَنَّهُۥ لَن يُؤْمِنَ مِن قَوْمِكَ إِلَّا مَن قَدْ ءَامَنَ فَلَا تَبْتَئِسْ بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَفْعَلُونَ · وَٱصْنَعِ ٱلْفُلْكَ بِأَعْيُنِنَا وَوَحْيِنَا وَلَا تُخَٰطِبْنِى فِى ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوٓا۟ إِنَّهُم مُّغْرَقُونَ

*wa ūḥiya ilā Nūḥin annahū lan yuʾmina min qawmika illā man qad āmana fa-lā tabtaʾis bimā kānū yafʿalūn · wa'ṣnaʿi'l-fulka bi-aʿyuninā wa waḥyinā wa lā tukhāṭibnī fi'lladhīna ẓalamū, innahum mughraqūn*

"**It was revealed to Noah: 'None of your people will believe now except those who have already believed. So do not grieve at what they have been doing. · Build the ark under Our eyes and by Our revelation. And do not address Me about those who have done wrong; they are to be drowned.'**"

### The turning point of the narrative

**This verse changes the structure of the narrative: the section of dispute closes here and the section of work begins.**

**And the sentence that announces the turn is a piece of *knowledge*: *lan yuʾmina … illā man qad āman*.** **The outcome of the call, that is, is announced before the call has ended.**

**The particle *lan* reports a categorical negation directed at the future** — this was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/24.

### فَلَا تَبْتَئِسْ — root ب-أ-س

The root **ب-أ-س**: **severity, hardship, difficulty.** *Baʾs* — severity; *baʾsāʾ* — hardship; *bāʾis* — sorrowful.

**And the verb is Form VIII: *ibtaʾasa* — "to grieve, to be inwardly straitened".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and I tie it to the twelfth verse of the sūra:** there it was the Prophet's breast that was straitened (*ḍāʾiqun bihī ṣadruk*); **here Noah is commanded not to grieve.** **Two messengers, the same state, two separate sections of the sūra.**

**And the sentences *bākhiʿun nafsak* at `026-suara.md` 26/3 and `018-kehf.md` 18/6 are on the same line. I rely on that treatment.**

### وَٱصْنَعِ ٱلْفُلْكَ بِأَعْيُنِنَا وَوَحْيِنَا

The root **ص-ن-ع**: **to make a thing with skill, to manufacture.** **The root was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/129 (*maṣāniʿ*).**

**بِأَعْيُنِنَا** — "before Our eyes / under Our watch."

**I enter a note per the method (`STYLE.md`):** this construction is read within the same limit set for the sentences about the throne. **The two postures tabulated at `032-secde.md` 32/4 (consignment and interpretation) hold here as well, and the shared principle is al-Shūrā 42/11.** **I do not impose a preference; I build no description of modality.**

**The same construction is used in the Qurʾān for Moses as well: *wa li-tuṣnaʿa ʿalā ʿaynī* (Ṭāhā 20/39) — it occurs in `020-taha.md`.**

**And the naming of the two means together is worth recording: *bi-aʿyuninā wa waḥyinā*.**

| Means | What it gives |
|---|---|
| *bi-aʿyuninā* | **Watch** — being present at the work |
| *wa waḥyinā* | **Knowledge** — how it is to be made |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse does not say that Noah built the ark by his own knowledge; but **the one who builds it is Noah.** The two things stand together.

### وَلَا تُخَٰطِبْنِى فِى ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟

The root **خ-ط-ب**: **to utter speech, to address.** *Khiṭāb* — speech exchanged between two parties.

**And the subject of the prohibition must be recorded: Noah is forbidden to speak *about* those who have done wrong.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is the preparation for the forty-fifth verse:** in the forty-fifth verse Noah will speak about his son, and **in the forty-sixth he will receive a warning.** **The prohibition in the thirty-seventh verse, that is, will be tested nine verses later.** This is a link that can be verified from the text, and it will be treated in detail in its place.

---

## 11/38-39 — وَيَصْنَعُ ٱلْفُلْكَ وَكُلَّمَا مَرَّ عَلَيْهِ مَلَأٌ مِّن قَوْمِهِۦ سَخِرُوا۟ مِنْهُ قَالَ إِن تَسْخَرُوا۟ مِنَّا فَإِنَّا نَسْخَرُ مِنكُمْ كَمَا تَسْخَرُونَ · فَسَوْفَ تَعْلَمُونَ مَن يَأْتِيهِ عَذَابٌ يُخْزِيهِ وَيَحِلُّ عَلَيْهِ عَذَابٌ مُّقِيمٌ

*wa yaṣnaʿu'l-fulka wa kullamā marra ʿalayhi malaʾun min qawmihī sakhirū minh, qāla in taskharū minnā fa-innā naskharu minkum kamā taskharūn · fa-sawfa taʿlamūna man yaʾtīhi ʿadhābun yukhzīhi wa yaḥillu ʿalayhi ʿadhābun muqīm*

"**He was building the ark, and every time the leading men of his people passed by him they mocked him. He said: 'If you mock us, we shall mock you just as you mock. · You will come to know upon whom a punishment will come that disgraces him, and upon whom a lasting punishment will descend.'**"

### وَيَصْنَعُ — the change of mood

**In the thirty-seventh verse there was an imperative (*wa'ṣnaʿ*), in the thirty-eighth there is an imperfect (*wa yaṣnaʿu*).**

**This is the technique of narration called in Arabic *ḥikāyat al-ḥāl*: a past event is told in the present tense and the scene is brought to life.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse does not say "he built the ark", it says **"he was building it"** — and immediately after comes *kullamā* (every time). **What is described, that is, is not a single event but a scene that repeats.**

**That the particle *kullamā* reports repetition was treated at `071-nuh.md` 71/7, and I rely on that treatment.** There too the same particle described **a settled reflex.**

**And there is an overlap between the two verses, and it can be read from the text:**

| Place | What follows *kullamā* |
|---|---|
| **Nūḥ 71/7** | *daʿawtuhum* — **Noah calls**, and they flee |
| **Hūd 11/38** | *marra ʿalayhi malaʾun* — **they pass by**, and Noah works |

**The direction is reversed in the two sūras:** in one Noah goes, in the other they come. **And in both the outcome is the same.**

### سَخِرُوا۟ — the answer to mockery

The root **س-خ-ر**: **to mock, to belittle; and to make subservient** (*taskhīr*). **The root has been treated in many places across this series.**

**And Noah's answer must be recorded, because it is out of the ordinary: *in taskharū minnā fa-innā naskharu minkum*.** **The messenger says he will meet mockery with mockery.**

**But the continuation of the sentence defines what he means, and the definition matters:** *fa-sawfa taʿlamūna man yaʾtīhi ʿadhābun yukhzīh.* **What he calls "mockery", that is, is that what is laughed at today will be reversed tomorrow.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the adjacency of the two verses:** Noah frames no counter-insult; **he reports a difference in time.** **And the Qurʾān builds the same structure elsewhere** — the laughter of the believers in the hereafter (al-Muṭaffifīn 83/34, treated in `083-mutaffifin.md`). **I rely on that treatment.**

### يُخْزِيهِ / مُّقِيم — two adjectives

The root **خ-ز-ي**: **to be disgraced, to be brought low.** *Yukhzīh* — **that which shames him.**

The root **ق-و-م**: *muqīm* — **lasting, settled in place.**

**The two attributes of punishment look to two different times:**

| Attribute | Time |
|---|---|
| *yukhzīh* | **This world** — the one that disgraces |
| *muqīm* | **The hereafter** — the one that lasts |

**And this pair will return word for word in Shuʿayb's mouth: *sawfa taʿlamūna man yaʾtīhi ʿadhābun yukhzīh* (93).** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text, and it will be tabulated in its place.

---

## 11/40 — حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا وَفَارَ ٱلتَّنُّورُ قُلْنَا ٱحْمِلْ فِيهَا مِن كُلٍّ زَوْجَيْنِ ٱثْنَيْنِ وَأَهْلَكَ إِلَّا مَن سَبَقَ عَلَيْهِ ٱلْقَوْلُ وَمَنْ ءَامَنَ وَمَآ ءَامَنَ مَعَهُۥٓ إِلَّا قَلِيلٌ

*ḥattā idhā jāʾa amrunā wa fāra'l-tannūru qulnā'ḥmil fīhā min kullin zawjayni'thnayni wa ahlaka illā man sabaqa ʿalayhi'l-qawlu wa man āman, wa mā āmana maʿahū illā qalīl*

"**Until, when Our command came and the oven boiled over, We said: 'Load into it two of every kind, and your household — except those against whom the word has already gone forth — and those who have believed.' And only a few had believed with him.**"

### حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا — the closing pattern of the sūra

**This construction is the most repeated structural element in the sūra. It was tabulated in the section on the backbone:**

| Narrative | Verse | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah** | **40** | *ḥattā idhā jāʾa amrunā* |
| **Hūd** | **58** | *wa lammā jāʾa amrunā* |
| **Ṣāliḥ** | **66** | *fa-lammā jāʾa amrunā* |
| **Lot** | **82** | *fa-lammā jāʾa amrunā* |
| **Shuʿayb** | **94** | *wa lammā jāʾa amrunā* |

**Five occurrences.** And **Noah's is different: it begins with *ḥattā idhā*.**

***ḥattā* in Arabic reports the end of a process: "until".** In the other four there is *lammā* — "when".

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** in the Noah narrative a **process** had been told (the building of the ark, the repeated mockery); *ḥattā* marks the point at which that process ends. **In the other four narratives no such process is told.**

**And the word *amrunā* (Our command) must be recorded: the destruction is named not by the name of a natural event but by a *command*.** This is the same in all five narratives.

### وَفَارَ ٱلتَّنُّورُ — one of the most discussed expressions

The root **ف-و-ر**: **to boil, to gush, to overflow.** **The root was treated in `023-muminun.md` and at `067-mulk.md` 67/7 (*tafūr*)** — there it was used of the boiling of the Fire.

**تَنُّور** — the lexicographers record that the word may have come into Arabic from another language; the English **tandoor** is the same word. **Its common sense: the oven in which bread is baked.**

**Over what the expression describes there is disagreement in the classical sources, and I give it in a table:**

| Reading | What the *tannūr* is | What *fāra* is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A bread oven** — the familiar hearth | Water boiled up out of it; this was the sign that the flood had begun |
| 2 | **The face of the earth** — some of the lexicographers give the word this sense as well | Water gushed out of the ground |
| 3 | **A particular place** — a geographical point | Water boiled up there |
| 4 | **An idiom** — a figure meaning "things came to the boil, the oven grew hot" | The rising of anger and the intensifying of the event |

**All four readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**A note, per the rule:** in the classical commentaries there are detailed reports about **where this oven was, whose it was, from whom it had come down.** **These are not in the Qurʾān and they are not passed on in this commentary** (`STYLE.md` — the prohibition on *Isrāʾīliyyāt* and on reports without a ground).

**And what can be recorded is the *function* of the expression, and this can be read from the text: *fāra'l-tannūr* stands in the same sentence as the coming of the command, and serves as a sign.** The verse does not describe how the flood came about; **it says how it was known that it had begun.**

### مِن كُلٍّ زَوْجَيْنِ ٱثْنَيْنِ

**There is a variant reading, and it bears on the sense:**

| Reading | Sense |
|---|---|
| ***min kullin*** (with tanwīn) | "Two of **every [kind]**" — the second term of the construct is elided |
| ***min kulli*** (in construct) | "Two **of every pair**" |

**Both readings are transmitted in the sources for the variant readings; I do not impose a preference.**

The root **ز-و-ج**: **a pair, a mate.** **The root was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/7 (*min kulli zawjin karīm*).**

**And the construction itself is worth recording: *zawjayni'thnayni* — "two pairs" or "two of each as a pair".** Both words report duality; **the repetition reinforces the exactness of the number.**

**Per the rule: which creatures were taken, and how many, is not enumerated in the Qurʾān. I do not go into the detail.**

### وَأَهْلَكَ إِلَّا مَن سَبَقَ عَلَيْهِ ٱلْقَوْلُ — the key to the forty-fifth verse

**This exception is one of the most important sentences in the narrative, and it is usually passed over.**

**The command is this: "load your household — except *the one against whom the word has already gone forth*."**

**Noah, that is, is told *before he boards the ark* that one of his household will be left outside.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the verses:** in the forty-fifth verse Noah will say "my son is of my household"; **whereas in the fortieth it had been said "of your household, except the one against whom the word has gone forth".** **There is a gap in knowledge between the two verses, and the forty-sixth will close that gap.**

**This is a link that can be verified from the text. What it means will be treated in detail at 11/45-47.**

### وَمَآ ءَامَنَ مَعَهُۥٓ إِلَّا قَلِيلٌ

**And the verse gives no number: *illā qalīl* — "only a few".**

**Per the rule: in the classical sources figures are transmitted for the number of those on the ark, and they do not agree with one another. Since the Qurʾān gives no number, none is given here either.**

**And the choice of the word is worth recording, and it is read together with `071-nuh.md`:** in Sūrat Nūḥ it is told that the call went on for a long time and that the method was changed again and again. **The *illā qalīl* here is the outcome of that process.**

---

## 11/41 — وَقَالَ ٱرْكَبُوا۟ فِيهَا بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ مَجْر۪ىٰهَا وَمُرْسَىٰهَآ إِنَّ رَبِّى لَغَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*wa qāla'rkabū fīhā bismi'llāhi majrāhā wa mursāhā, inna rabbī la-ghafūrun raḥīm*

"**He said: 'Board it. In the name of God is its sailing and its mooring. My Lord is indeed much forgiving, most merciful.'**"

### ٱرْكَبُوا۟ فِيهَا — the choice of particle

**In Arabic the verb *rakiba* takes a direct object: *rakiba'l-safīna* (he boarded the ship).** **Here the particle *fī* comes between: *irkabū fīhā*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and I pass on the explanation the lexicographers record:** the particle *fī* reports that the boarding is **not onto but into**. **Not as one mounts an animal, that is, but as one steps inside a vessel.**

### بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ مَجْر۪ىٰهَا وَمُرْسَىٰهَا

The root **ج-ر-ي**: **to run, to flow.** The root **ر-س-و**: **to be fixed, to cast anchor.** *Mursā* — the place or moment of stopping; from the same root comes *rawāsī* (the firm mountains), treated across this series (`013-rad.md` 13/3, `031-lokman.md` 31/10).

**The two words form a pair:**

| Word | What it is |
|---|---|
| *majrāhā* | **Motion** — its sailing |
| *mursāhā* | **Rest** — its anchoring |

**And both are attached to *bismi'llāh*.**

**A variant reading is transmitted:** it is recorded that the words are read *majrāhā / mursāhā* (with fatḥa, as nouns of time and place) or *mujrāhā / mursāhā* (with ḍamma, as passive verbal nouns of Form IV). **On the second reading the sense becomes "its being made to sail and its being made to stop".** **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the place of *the basmala* here is worth recording.** **The basmala was treated in `001-fatiha.md`. What belongs here is this:** the places in the Qurʾān where the basmala is spoken inside an event, from a person's mouth, are few; **this is one of them.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence attaches both the motion of the ark and its rest to the same name. **Neither its sailing is attached to Noah's skill, that is, nor its stopping to his decision.**

### إِنَّ رَبِّى لَغَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ

**And the choice of the two names at the end of the sentence is worth recording.**

**The names cited at the moment the flood begins are *al-Ghafūr* and *al-Raḥīm*** — not names of punishment or of power.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the sentence is spoken to those boarding the ark; **the attributes cited for them are forgiveness and mercy.** This is an explanation.

---

## 11/42-43 — وَهِىَ تَجْرِى بِهِمْ فِى مَوْجٍ كَٱلْجِبَالِ وَنَادَىٰ نُوحٌ ٱبْنَهُۥ وَكَانَ فِى مَعْزِلٍ يَٰبُنَىَّ ٱرْكَب مَّعَنَا وَلَا تَكُن مَّعَ ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ · قَالَ سَـَٔاوِىٓ إِلَىٰ جَبَلٍ يَعْصِمُنِى مِنَ ٱلْمَآءِ قَالَ لَا عَاصِمَ ٱلْيَوْمَ مِنْ أَمْرِ ٱللَّهِ إِلَّا مَن رَّحِمَ وَحَالَ بَيْنَهُمَا ٱلْمَوْجُ فَكَانَ مِنَ ٱلْمُغْرَقِينَ

*wa hiya tajrī bihim fī mawjin ka'l-jibāli wa nādā Nūḥuni'bnahū wa kāna fī maʿzil, yā bunayya'rkab maʿanā wa lā takun maʿa'l-kāfirīn · qāla sa-āwī ilā jabalin yaʿṣimunī mina'l-māʾ, qāla lā ʿāṣima'l-yawma min amri'llāhi illā man raḥim, wa ḥāla baynahumā'l-mawju fa-kāna mina'l-mughraqīn*

"**The ark was carrying them through waves like mountains. Noah called out to his son, who was standing apart: 'My dear son! Board with us, and do not be with the disbelievers!'** · **He said: 'I shall take refuge on a mountain that will protect me from the water.' Noah said: 'Today there is nothing that protects from the command of God — except the one He has had mercy on.' And the wave came between them; and he was among the drowned.**"

### How the scene is built

**These two verses are among the most densely told scenes in the Qurʾān, and they are built out of four elements:**

| Element | Expression | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **The background** | *mawjin ka'l-jibāl* — **waves like mountains** | Scale |
| **The position** | *wa kāna fī maʿzil* — **standing apart** | Separation, distance still |
| **The call** | *yā bunayya'rkab maʿanā* | The father |
| **The answer** | *sa-āwī ilā jabal* | The son |

**And two words from the same root stand facing one another: *ka'l-jibāl* (the waves like mountains) and *ilā jabal* (I shall take refuge on a mountain).**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two words are present in the same scene:** the son wants to shelter himself on a **mountain**; **but the scene itself has already said that the water stands as high as the mountains.** **The invalidity of the answer, that is, is given by the description before it is given by any sentence.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### فِى مَعْزِلٍ — standing apart

The root **ع-ز-ل**: **to separate, to draw aside.** *Maʿzil* — **the place where one stands apart.** It has no connection with the English "isolate"; the word is Arabic.

**And note the mood of the sentence: *wa kāna fī maʿzil* — a past tense, reporting continuity.** **The son, that is, did not draw aside when the flood came; he was already apart.**

### يَٰبُنَىَّ — the pattern of the address

**This address was treated in `031-lokman.md` as the backbone of that sūra, and I rely on that treatment.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> **At the centre of the sūra there is a list of counsels, and the list passes between a father and a son.** … **The address each time is *yā bunayya* — "my dear son" — in the diminutive, reporting affection.**

**In Luqmān the address occurs three times (13, 16, 17). In Hūd once (42). And a comparison of the two places is worth recording:**

| | **Luqmān 31/13, 16, 17** | **Hūd 11/42** |
|---|---|---|
| **Who speaks** | **Luqmān** — a wise servant | **Noah** — a prophet |
| **What is said** | **A list of counsels** — associating partners, deeds being seen, the prayer, patience | **A single command** — *irkab maʿanā* |
| **The moment** | **Calm** — a conversation of instruction | **Crisis** — in the middle of the waves |
| **Is there an answer** | **No** — the son does not speak | **Yes** — the son refuses |
| **The outcome** | Not told | **Drowning** |

**All five rows are data of the text.**

**And the most striking difference is the second from last: Luqmān's son never speaks in the Qurʾān.** **Noah's son speaks — and refuses.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the Qurʾān attaches the same address to two different outcomes. **The warmth of the address, that is, does not determine the outcome.** This is consistent with a line repeated across the whole sūra: the messenger delivers, and does not carry the outcome.

**A variant reading is transmitted:** it is recorded that the word is read *yā bunayya* (with fatḥa) or *yā bunayyi* (with kasra). **The sense does not change; both mean "my dear son".**

### ٱرْكَب مَّعَنَا وَلَا تَكُن مَّعَ ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ — two occurrences of *maʿa*

**The call uses the same particle twice: *maʿanā* and *maʿa'l-kāfirīn*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** what is offered is not a **place** but a **being-together.** **And what is refused is likewise not a place but another being-together.**

**And the structure of the command must be recorded: a positive imperative plus a negative one.** Together they close off the alternative: **there is no third place.**

**And the son's answer is looking for exactly a third place: *sa-āwī ilā jabal*.**

### يَعْصِمُنِى — root ع-ص-م

The root **ع-ص-م**: **to hold, to protect, to prevent.** **The root was treated in `022-hac.md`, `033-ahzab.md` and `060-mumtehine.md`.** From the same root come *iʿtiṣām* (to hold fast) and *ʿiṣma* (being kept from harm).

**And the concrete sense of the word is recorded by the lexicographers thus: *ʿiṣām* is the cord that ties the mouth of a vessel, or the strap of a waterskin — that is, a tie that keeps a thing from falling.**

**And the two occurrences in the verse must be recorded:**

| Who | Expression | Form |
|---|---|---|
| **The son** | *yaʿṣim**unī*** — "it will protect me" | **A verb** — future |
| **Noah** | *lā **ʿāṣima**'l-yawm* | **A noun** — absolute negation |

**Noah meets his son's verb with a noun. In Arabic the *lā* of absolute negation of the genus (*lā ʿāṣima*) reports an unqualified negation: "there is no protector at all."**

**I record this as a grammatical observation:** the son was speaking of a **possibility**; the answer removes **the very category of the possibility.**

### إِلَّا مَن رَّحِمَ — the grammatical question of the exception

**Over the exception at the end of the sentence there is disagreement, and I give it in a table:**

| Reading | What *illā man raḥim* is an exception to |
|---|---|
| 1 | **An exception among the protected** — "except the one who has been shown mercy [he is protected]" |
| 2 | **An exception to the *ʿāṣim*** — "there is no protector but God's mercy" |
| 3 | **A disconnected exception (*istithnāʾ munqaṭiʿ*)** — "unless God shows mercy [that is another matter]" |

**All three readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The point all three arrive at is the same: protection is not the work of a place or of a precaution.**

### وَحَالَ بَيْنَهُمَا ٱلْمَوْجُ — the economy of the sentence

The root **ح-و-ل**: **to come between, to turn, to change.** From the same root come *ḥāl*, *ḥawl* and *iḥtiyāl*.

**And the ending of the verse is worth recording, and I give it as an observation about the word order:** the narration does not depict the drowning. **A single sentence: "and the wave came between them."** **After it there is only a verdict: *fa-kāna mina'l-mughraqīn*.**

**Per the rule: the details told in the classical sources about the sequel to this scene, the son's last words, Noah's reaction, are not in the Qurʾān, and they are not passed on here.**

---

## 11/44 — وَقِيلَ يَٰٓأَرْضُ ٱبْلَعِى مَآءَكِ وَيَٰسَمَآءُ أَقْلِعِى وَغِيضَ ٱلْمَآءُ وَقُضِىَ ٱلْأَمْرُ وَٱسْتَوَتْ عَلَى ٱلْجُودِىِّ وَقِيلَ بُعْدًا لِّلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

*wa qīla yā arḍu'blaʿī māʾaki wa yā samāʾu aqliʿī wa ghīḍa'l-māʾu wa quḍiya'l-amru wa'stawat ʿalā'l-Jūdiyyi wa qīla buʿdan li'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn*

"**And it was said: 'Earth, swallow your water! Sky, hold back!' The water subsided, the matter was ended, and the ark came to rest upon al-Jūdī. And it was said: 'Away with the wrongdoing people!'**"

### The word order of the verse — eight sentences, all short

**This verse is an example frequently cited in the sources of Arabic rhetoric. The reason is the shortness of the sentences and the way they are lined up. I set the verse out as a table:**

| # | Sentence | Form | Who / what |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | *wa **qīla*** | **Passive perfect** | The speaker is not named |
| 2 | *yā arḍu'blaʿī māʾaki* | **Imperative** | To the earth |
| 3 | *wa yā samāʾu aqliʿī* | **Imperative** | To the sky |
| 4 | *wa **ghīḍa**'l-māʾ* | **Passive perfect** | The water |
| 5 | *wa **quḍiya**'l-amr* | **Passive perfect** | The matter |
| 6 | *wa'stawat ʿalā'l-Jūdiyy* | Perfect | The ark |
| 7 | *wa **qīla*** | **Passive perfect** | The speaker is not named |
| 8 | *buʿdan li'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn* | The pattern of imprecation / verdict | The people |

**Four of the eight sentences are passive.** And this is a feature of the word order worth recording: **the agent is nowhere named.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the verse never fills in the distance between the one who gives the command and the one who receives it. **The command is spoken, the result is reported; there is no stage in between.**

### ٱبْلَعِى — to swallow

The root **ب-ل-ع**: **to swallow, to draw in.** **The root occurs here for the first time in this series.**

**The choice of word must be recorded: the verse does not say *jaffī* (dry up) or *inshafī* (soak it in).** **It says "swallow"** — the act of a living thing.

**And the command given to the sky is of the same kind: *aqliʿī*.** The root **ق-ل-ع**: **to pull up, to tear off; and to leave off a thing.** *Aqlaʿa ʿan* — he withdrew his hand from a thing, he stopped. **The sky, that is, is told "leave off raining".**

### وَغِيضَ ٱلْمَآءُ — root غ-ي-ض

The root **غ-ي-ض**: **the receding, the diminishing of water.**

**And this root was treated at `013-rad.md` 13/8 (*wa mā taghīḍu'l-arḥāmu wa mā tazdād*), and I rely on that treatment.** **The same root in both verses, with the same core sense: diminishing, receding.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the root occurs in two places in the Qurʾān, and in both a **diminishing that is not seen** is described.

### وَقُضِىَ ٱلْأَمْرُ

The root **ق-ض-ي**: **to complete a matter, to settle it by decree, to bring it to an end.**

**And the construction occurs elsewhere in the Qurʾān as well** (`019-meryem.md` 19/21, `002-bakara.md` 2/210). **Its work here is to report that the flood is over.**

### وَٱسْتَوَتْ عَلَى ٱلْجُودِىِّ

The root **س-و-ي**: **to be level, to be set right, to settle.** *Istawat* — **it came to rest, it settled.**

**ٱلْجُودِىّ** — **it is the name of a place, and it occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

**A note, per the rule:** as to which geographical point this name designates, differing views are transmitted in the classical sources and in the later literature. **The Qurʾān gives no description of a location.** **In this commentary no place is identified and the argument is not opened.**

### بُعْدًا لِّلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ — the fourth closing pattern of the sūra

**This construction occurs four times in the sūra, and it was tabulated in the section on the backbone:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **44** | *buʿdan li'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn* | **Noah's people** — no name is given |
| **60** | *alā buʿdan li-ʿĀdin qawmi Hūd* | **ʿĀd** |
| **68** | *alā buʿdan li-Thamūd* | **Thamūd** |
| **95** | *alā buʿdan li-Madyana kamā baʿidat Thamūd* | **Midian** |

**And Noah's is different: the name of the people is not given, and an *attribute* is put in its place — *al-ẓālimīn*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and I tie it to a principle of the method:** in this commentary no blanket ruling is issued about a community; **it is the attributes the verse describes that count** (`STYLE.md`). **The forty-fourth verse does this itself: it names not the name but the attribute.**

The root **ب-ع-د**: **to be far.** *Buʿdan* — **an expression of imprecation or verdict in the pattern of the absolute object (*mafʿūl muṭlaq*): "away with them".** And in the ninety-fifth verse the word will be reinforced with a verb: *kamā **baʿidat** Thamūd.*

---

## 11/45 — وَنَادَىٰ نُوحٌ رَّبَّهُۥ فَقَالَ رَبِّ إِنَّ ٱبْنِى مِنْ أَهْلِى وَإِنَّ وَعْدَكَ ٱلْحَقُّ وَأَنتَ أَحْكَمُ ٱلْحَٰكِمِينَ

*wa nādā Nūḥun rabbahū fa-qāla rabbi inna'bnī min ahlī wa inna waʿdaka'l-ḥaqqu wa anta aḥkamu'l-ḥākimīn*

"**Noah called out to his Lord: 'My Lord! My son is of my household. Your promise is the truth. And You are the most just of judges.'**"

### How the sentence is built — three occurrences of *inna/anta*

**Noah frames three sentences, and all three are statements; there is no *request* among them.**

| # | Sentence | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *inna'bnī min ahlī* | **A fact** — kinship |
| 2 | *wa inna waʿdaka'l-ḥaqq* | **An acknowledgement** — the truth of the promise |
| 3 | *wa anta aḥkamu'l-ḥākimīn* | **A surrender** — where judgement belongs |

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and it is the most striking side of the verse:** in the sentence there is **no verb of request at all.** Noah does not say "save him". **Three statements are set side by side and a question is left in between.**

**And the second sentence must be recorded: Noah himself confirms the truth of the promise.** He raises no objection, that is.

### إِنَّ ٱبْنِى مِنْ أَهْلِى — which promise is meant

**The promise Noah relies on is the command in the fortieth verse: *iḥmil fīhā … wa ahlaka* — "load your household".**

**And there was an exception there: *illā man sabaqa ʿalayhi'l-qawl* — "except the one against whom the word has gone forth".**

**This is the knot of the narrative, and I set it out as a table:**

| Verse | Expression | What it gives |
|---|---|---|
| **40** | *wa ahlaka **illā man sabaqa ʿalayhi'l-qawl*** | **Command + exception** — whom the exception covers is not said |
| **45** | *inna'bnī **min ahlī*** | Noah counts his son **within the scope of the command** |
| **46** | *innahū **laysa min ahlik*** | **The scope of the exception is announced** |

**In the forty-fifth verse, that is, Noah is asking about something he does not know:** whom the exception covers.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the arrangement of the three verses.** **And the warning in the forty-sixth verse (*fa-lā tasʾalni mā laysa laka bihī ʿilm*) supports this reading:** what is missing is, in the verse's own language, **knowledge.**

### أَحْكَمُ ٱلْحَٰكِمِينَ — the link with the first verse of the sūra

The root **ح-ك-م** occurred in the first verse as *uḥkimat* and *ḥakīm*; **here it occurs twice more: *aḥkam* and *al-ḥākimīn*.**

| Verse | Derivative |
|---|---|
| **1** | *uḥkimat* + *ḥakīm* |
| **45** | *aḥkamu'l-ḥākimīn* |

**I record this as an observation:** the root by which the sūra describes the Book comes back at the most critical moment of the narrative as **a form of address.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

---

## 11/46 — قَالَ يَٰنُوحُ إِنَّهُۥ لَيْسَ مِنْ أَهْلِكَ إِنَّهُۥ عَمَلٌ غَيْرُ صَٰلِحٍ فَلَا تَسْـَٔلْنِ مَا لَيْسَ لَكَ بِهِۦ عِلْمٌ إِنِّىٓ أَعِظُكَ أَن تَكُونَ مِنَ ٱلْجَٰهِلِينَ

*qāla yā Nūḥu innahū laysa min ahlik, innahū ʿamalun ghayru ṣāliḥ, fa-lā tasʾalni mā laysa laka bihī ʿilm, innī aʿiẓuka an takūna mina'l-jāhilīn*

"**He said: 'Noah! He is not of your household; he is a deed that is not righteous. So do not ask of Me what you have no knowledge of. I counsel you not to be among the ignorant.'**"

**This verse is one of the sentences in the sūra that most needs dwelling on, and it holds three separate questions: the variant reading, the definition of "household", and its link with the principle of burden.**

### إِنَّهُۥ عَمَلٌ غَيْرُ صَٰلِحٍ — the variant reading

**Two readings are transmitted for this sentence, and the reading changes the sense. I give it in a table:**

| # | Reading | Parsing | Sense |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***innahū ʿamaluN ghayru ṣāliḥ*** | *ʿamal* as the predicate of the subject (a noun in the nominative) | "**He is a deed that is not righteous**" — the person himself is identified with a deed |
| 2 | ***innahū ʿamiLA ghayra ṣāliḥ*** | *ʿamila* a perfect verb, *ghayra* its object (in the accusative) | "**He did a deed that was not righteous**" — the act and the agent are separate |

**The first reading is the widespread one (that of the majority).** **The second is also transmitted in the sources for the variant readings; it is widely attributed to al-Kisāʾī. I give this attribution with a hedge, with the note "this is how it is widely reported", and I claim no certainty.**

**The difference between the two readings must be recorded:**

| | **The first reading** | **The second reading** |
|---|---|---|
| **The structure of the sentence** | **A nominal sentence** — fixity (*thubūt*) | **A verbal sentence** — coming to be (*ḥudūth*) |
| **What is said** | The person = the deed | The person ≠ the deed; the person **did** the deed |
| **The ground of the separation** | **The deed done defines the person** | **The deed done is the cause of the separation** |

**And both readings arrive at the same conclusion: the separation is not by lineage but by deed.** **The difference is in how the separation is expressed.**

**I do not impose a preference.** **But I enter a note about the language, and it is my own reading:** the first reading is a structure used in Arabic for **intensification** — naming a person by a verbal noun or by a noun (as in *"he is justice itself"*). **The first reading, that is, can also be read as a more emphatic form of the second.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### لَيْسَ مِنْ أَهْلِكَ — "he is not of your household"

The root **أ-ه-ل**: **to belong to a place or a person; to settle.** *Ahl* — those who belong to a house, a work, a place.

**And over what the sentence says there is disagreement in the classical sources. I give it in a table:**

| Reading | What *laysa min ahlik* means | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **"He is not of the household whose deliverance was promised"** — that is, outside the scope of the promise | The exception in the fortieth verse (*illā man sabaqa ʿalayhi'l-qawl*) |
| 2 | **"He is not of your household in point of religion"** — the lineage is there, the bond is not | That *ahl* is used in the Qurʾān for a shared faith as well |
| 3 | **That he was not his son by lineage** | In the classical sources this is a minority view |

**The third view is transmitted but is counted weak, and it strains against the wording of the verse:** in the forty-second verse Noah called him *yā bunayya*, and in the forty-fifth said *ibnī* ("my son"); **the verse does not correct either expression.**

**I pass on the first and second readings without imposing a preference.** **And what they have in common must be recorded: in both, lineage does not determine the scope.**

**A note, per the rule:** on the basis of this verse various accounts about Noah's family have been produced in the classical sources and in the later literature. **These are not in the Qurʾān; they are not passed on in this commentary.**

### Its link with 35/18 — the principle of burden at its hardest

**The principle of burden was treated at `035-fatir.md` 35/18, and it was recorded there that the principle is *tested at its most demanding point*. I rely on that treatment.** The table there was this:

| Stage | Expression |
|---|---|
| The principle | *lā taziru wāziratun wizra ukhrā* |
| **Addition 1** | One whose load is heavy may ask for help |
| **Addition 2** | Even so, none of it is carried |
| **Addition 3** | ***wa law kāna dhā qurbā*** — **even if he be a near relative** |

**And in the Fāṭir section this entry was made:** *"The clause dhā qurbā (a near relative) names the most natural place in the world for a load to be shared. By saying that even there it is not shared, the verse builds the principle at its most durable point."*

**Hūd 11/46 is the application of that same principle inside an *event*. And a comparison of the two texts must be recorded:**

| | **Fāṭir 35/18** | **Hūd 11/46** |
|---|---|---|
| **Kind** | **A statement of principle** — a general ruling | **An event** — a single case |
| **Degree of nearness** | *dhā qurbā* — an unspecified relative | **Father and son** — the nearest degree |
| **What is asked to be carried** | *ḥiml* — a load | **Deliverance** — the right to board |
| **Who asks** | *muthqala* — the person whose load is heavy | **Noah** — the side with no load |
| **The answer** | *lā yuḥmalu minhu shayʾ* | *laysa min ahlik* |

**And the difference must be recorded: in Fāṭir the one who asks is the person carrying the load; in Hūd the one who asks is speaking *on another's behalf*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is how the two texts are built:** Fāṭir builds the principle from the side of **what cannot be carried**; **Hūd shows the same principle from the side of *what cannot be interceded for*.** **The principle works, that is, in both directions: no one can carry another's load, and no one can lighten that load for another.**

**And there is another entry on the same line across this series: in `066-tahrim.md` the wives of Noah and of Lot were treated as an example that nearness does not deliver. I rely on that treatment.** **Hūd 11/46 is the same principle shown by way of a son.**

### فَلَا تَسْـَٔلْنِ مَا لَيْسَ لَكَ بِهِۦ عِلْمٌ

**And the second half of the verse does work distinct from the first: it is a warning about *method*.**

**The subject of the prohibition must be recorded: what is forbidden is not *asking* but *asking without knowledge*.** The sentence does not end at *lā tasʾalnī*; **there is the qualification *mā laysa laka bihī ʿilm*.**

**And this ties back to the command in the thirty-seventh verse: *wa lā tukhāṭibnī fi'lladhīna ẓalamū*.**

| Verse | The prohibition | Its scope |
|---|---|---|
| **37** | *lā tukhāṭibnī fi'lladhīna ẓalamū* | **About those who do wrong** |
| **46** | *lā tasʾalni mā laysa laka bihī ʿilm* | **About what is not known** |

**The two prohibitions are different, and that difference must be recorded. I record this as my own reading:** in the forty-fifth verse Noah is not counted as having broken the prohibition of the thirty-seventh — **because he did not know that his son was among "those who do wrong".** **And the warning of the forty-sixth verse lands exactly here: a lack of knowledge.**

**And the same principle was treated at `018-kehf.md` 18/68** (*wa kayfa taṣbiru ʿalā mā lam tuḥiṭ bihī khubrā*) **and this entry was made there: "the withholding of judgement where knowledge is lacking."** **I rely on that treatment and record that Hūd 11/46 stands on the same line.**

### إِنِّىٓ أَعِظُكَ أَن تَكُونَ مِنَ ٱلْجَٰهِلِينَ

The root **و-ع-ظ**: **to counsel, to admonish.**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: *aʿiẓuka an takūna mina'l-jāhilīn* — "I counsel you not to be among the ignorant."**

In Arabic this structure reports **a warning against an outcome**; **it does not say that the person already bears that attribute.**

**I give this as a grammatical note and set a limit per the method (`STYLE.md`):** no ruling of fault regarding a prophet is to be drawn from this verse. **The wording of the verse is a caution, not a characterisation.**

**And the forty-seventh verse confirms this: Noah withdraws at once.**

---

## 11/47 — قَالَ رَبِّ إِنِّىٓ أَعُوذُ بِكَ أَنْ أَسْـَٔلَكَ مَا لَيْسَ لِى بِهِۦ عِلْمٌ وَإِلَّا تَغْفِرْ لِى وَتَرْحَمْنِىٓ أَكُن مِّنَ ٱلْخَٰسِرِينَ

*qāla rabbi innī aʿūdhu bika an asʾalaka mā laysa lī bihī ʿilm, wa illā taghfir lī wa tarḥamnī akun mina'l-khāsirīn*

"**He said: 'My Lord! I take refuge with You from asking of You what I have no knowledge of. If You do not forgive me and have mercy on me, I shall be among the losers.'**"

### The form of the answer

**Noah does not meet the warning of the forty-sixth verse with a defence.** He does not say "I did not know"; he does not say "I was right".

**The sentence consists of two parts:**

| # | Expression | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *aʿūdhu bika **an asʾalaka** mā laysa lī bihī ʿilm* | **Takes up the very words of the warning** |
| 2 | *wa illā taghfir lī wa tarḥamnī akun mina'l-khāsirīn* | **Takes the outcome upon himself** |

**And the words of the first part are the words of the forty-sixth verse:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **46** | *fa-lā tasʾalni **mā laysa laka bihī ʿilm*** |
| **47** | *an asʾalaka **mā laysa lī bihī ʿilm*** |

**The only difference is the pronoun: *laka* → *lī*.** **Noah, that is, repeats what was said to him, turning it into the first person.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the answer shows, by its very wording, that the warning **has been understood.**

### أَعُوذُ بِكَ — what refuge is taken from

The root **ع-و-ذ**: **to take refuge, to withdraw to a place for protection.** **The root was treated in `113-felak.md` and `114-nas.md`.**

**And what is taken refuge from must be recorded:** Noah takes refuge not from an enemy, a punishment or a danger, but **from a request he himself might make.**

**I record this as an observation and compare it with the uses of *aʿūdhu* across this series:** in Sūrat al-Falaq and Sūrat al-Nās what refuge is taken from is an evil coming from outside. **Here it is a behaviour coming from within.**

### وَإِلَّا تَغْفِرْ لِى وَتَرْحَمْنِىٓ

**The conditional clause: *wa in lā* → *wa illā*.**

**And the order of the two verbs is worth recording: *taghfir* (forgiveness) and *tarḥam* (mercy).** **And the same two names occurred in the forty-first verse: *inna rabbī la-ghafūrun raḥīm* — as Noah was boarding the ark.**

| Verse | Expression | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **41** | *inna rabbī la-**ghafūrun raḥīm*** | Said about **those boarding the ark** |
| **47** | *wa illā **taghfir** lī wa **tarḥamnī*** | Asked for **himself** |

**The same two roots, six verses apart, in two different directions.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And in `071-nuh.md` the order of the same structure within that sūra was tabulated:** forgiveness is first hoped for on another's behalf (71/7), then the asking of it is taught to others (71/10), and at the very end the man asks it for himself (71/28). **Hūd 11/41-47 repeats the same order over a shorter distance.** **I rely on that treatment and record this as an overlap.**

---

## 11/48 — قِيلَ يَٰنُوحُ ٱهْبِطْ بِسَلَٰمٍ مِّنَّا وَبَرَكَٰتٍ عَلَيْكَ وَعَلَىٰٓ أُمَمٍ مِّمَّن مَّعَكَ وَأُمَمٌ سَنُمَتِّعُهُمْ ثُمَّ يَمَسُّهُم مِّنَّا عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ

*qīla yā Nūḥu'hbiṭ bi-salāmin minnā wa barakātin ʿalayka wa ʿalā umamin mimman maʿak, wa umamun sa-numattiʿuhum thumma yamassuhum minnā ʿadhābun alīm*

"**It was said: 'Noah! Come down with peace from Us, and with blessings upon you and upon the peoples that will come from those with you. And there will be peoples We shall let enjoy life for a while, and then a painful punishment from Us will touch them.'**"

### The Noah narrative's closing of its own

**As was tabulated in the section on the backbone, not one of Hūd's five closing patterns is struck in full on the Noah narrative.** **Noah's closing is peculiar to itself and runs to two verses (48-49).**

**And the difference must be recorded: the other four peoples are sent off with *buʿdan*; Noah is met with *bi-salām*.**

| Narrative | What stands at the closing |
|---|---|
| **Noah** | ***bi-salāmin minnā wa barakāt*** — **peace and blessings** |
| **Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Shuʿayb** | *alā buʿdan li-…* — **distance** |

**This is the only positive closing among the six narratives of Sūrat Hūd.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

### ٱهْبِطْ — to come down

The root **ه-ب-ط**: **to go down.** **The root was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/36 and 2/38 (*ihbiṭū*).**

**And the concrete sense of the word is exactly in place here: the ark had come to rest on a height (*al-Jūdiyy*); the descent is from there.**

**And it can be compared with the use in `002-bakara.md`, and I record this as an observation:** there the same verb reported an **expulsion**; **here it comes together with a *greeting of peace*.** **The word carries no value on its own.**

### بِسَلَٰمٍ مِّنَّا وَبَرَكَٰتٍ

The root **س-ل-م** — **soundness, freedom from flaw, completeness** — was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/25, and it was recorded there: *"the greeting of peace is not a wish but a guarantee."* **I rely on that treatment.**

The root **ب-ر-ك**: **continuance, increase, a good that stays.** The lexicographers relate the root to **birka** (a pool): water gathering and remaining in one place.

**And the patterns of the two words differ, and this is worth recording:**

| Word | Pattern | To where |
|---|---|---|
| *salām* | **Singular** | *minnā* — **the source is named** |
| *barakāt* | **Plural** | *ʿalayka wa ʿalā umam* — **two addressees** |

**And the construction *umamin mimman maʿak* must be recorded: the blessing is given not to those on the ark but *to the peoples who will come from them*.** **The addressee of the promise, that is, is the future.**

### وَأُمَمٌ سَنُمَتِّعُهُمْ ثُمَّ يَمَسُّهُم مِّنَّا عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ

**And the sentence ends with a warning: deliverance is not a permanent guarantee.**

The root **م-ت-ع** occurred in the third verse of the sūra: *yumattiʿkum matāʿan ḥasanan ilā ajalin musammā.*

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **3** | *yumattiʿkum matāʿan ḥasanan* | **Those who turn back** — a promise |
| **48** | *sa-numattiʿuhum **thumma** yamassuhum … ʿadhābun alīm* | **Later peoples** — a warning |

**The same root, two different outcomes.** **And the difference lies in the particle *thumma*: in the third verse the enjoyment was tied to a term; here what comes after the enjoyment is stated.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the root the two verses share:** nowhere does the sūra count enjoyment (*matāʿ*) as an outcome. **And the same thing had been said in the fifteenth verse:** *nuwaffi ilayhim aʿmālahum fīhā* — but *laysa lahum fi'l-ākhirati illā'l-nār*.

---

## 11/49 — تِلْكَ مِنْ أَنۢبَآءِ ٱلْغَيْبِ نُوحِيهَآ إِلَيْكَ مَا كُنتَ تَعْلَمُهَآ أَنتَ وَلَا قَوْمُكَ مِن قَبْلِ هَٰذَا فَٱصْبِرْ إِنَّ ٱلْعَٰقِبَةَ لِلْمُتَّقِينَ

*tilka min anbāʾi'l-ghaybi nūḥīhā ilayk, mā kunta taʿlamuhā anta wa lā qawmuka min qabli hādhā, fa-ṣbir inna'l-ʿāqibata li'l-muttaqīn*

"**These are among the tidings of the unseen that We reveal to you. Neither you nor your people knew them before this. So be patient; the outcome belongs to those who are mindful.**"

### The turn from the narrative back to the one addressed

**The verse closes the Noah narrative and turns directly to the Prophet. And it does three things:**

| # | Expression | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *tilka min anbāʾi'l-ghayb* | **It reports the source of the tidings** |
| 2 | *mā kunta taʿlamuhā anta wa lā qawmuk* | **It closes off any prior knowledge** |
| 3 | *fa-ṣbir* | **It gives a command** |
