### تِلْكَ — the demonstrative that points to what is far

***tilka* is the demonstrative for what is distant. This use was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/2, and two explanations were recorded there: distance, or magnification (*taʿẓīm*).**

**The use here is worth recording: the narrative has only just ended, so it is "near"; even so it is referred to with the distant demonstrative.**

### أَنۢبَآءِ ٱلْغَيْب — the kind of tiding

The root **ن-ب-أ**: **news that is weighty, that has some use.** **The root was worked out in `078-nebe.md`, and I rely on that treatment.** The entry there: a *nabaʾ* is not just any piece of news; **it is news that has consequences.**

**And the construction occurs in a few places in the Qurʾān, and all of them stand at the end or the beginning of a narrative** (Āl ʿImrān 3/44; Yūsuf 12/102; Hūd 11/49; Hūd 11/100 — *min anbāʾi'l-qurā*).

### مَا كُنتَ تَعْلَمُهَآ أَنتَ وَلَا قَوْمُكَ

**There is a pronoun of emphasis in the sentence: *taʿlamuhā anta*.** In Arabic, when the pronoun inside the verb would suffice, the addition of *anta* reports **emphasis**.

**And the scope is widened: *wa lā qawmuk*.** **The claim, that is, is not about the Prophet alone but about the whole of his surroundings.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence frames a verifiable claim — **that the source of these tidings is not local knowledge.**

### فَٱصْبِرْ إِنَّ ٱلْعَٰقِبَةَ لِلْمُتَّقِينَ

The root **ص-ب-ر**: **to hold a thing in one place, to confine it; and from there, to endure.** **The root was treated in `103-asr.md` and `090-beled.md`.**

**And the place of the command must be recorded: at the end of a narrative twenty-five verses long, a command of a single word.**

**This is the first of the four commands the sūra gives the messenger, and it was tabulated in the section on the backbone:**

| Verse | Command |
|---|---|
| **49** | *fa-ṣbir* |
| **112** | *fa-staqim kamā umirt* |
| **115** | *wa'ṣbir* |
| **123** | *fa'ʿbudhu wa tawakkal ʿalayh* |

The root **ع-ق-ب**: **to come after, to follow a trace.** **The root was treated at `013-rad.md` 13/11 and 13/22.** *ʿĀqiba* — **what comes at the end.**

And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: *inna'l-ʿāqibata **li'l-muttaqīn*** — the lām of ownership (the lām of belonging). **The outcome, that is, is made to *belong* to those who are mindful.**

---

## 11/50-52 — وَإِلَىٰ عَادٍ أَخَاهُمْ هُودًا قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥٓ إِنْ أَنتُمْ إِلَّا مُفْتَرُونَ · يَٰقَوْمِ لَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ أَجْرًا إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَى ٱلَّذِى فَطَرَنِىٓ أَفَلَا تَعْقِلُونَ · وَيَٰقَوْمِ ٱسْتَغْفِرُوا۟ رَبَّكُمْ ثُمَّ تُوبُوٓا۟ إِلَيْهِ يُرْسِلِ ٱلسَّمَآءَ عَلَيْكُم مِّدْرَارًا وَيَزِدْكُمْ قُوَّةً إِلَىٰ قُوَّتِكُمْ وَلَا تَتَوَلَّوْا۟ مُجْرِمِينَ

*wa ilā ʿĀdin akhāhum Hūdā, qāla yā qawmi'ʿbudū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, in antum illā muftarūn · yā qawmi lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi ajran, in ajriya illā ʿalā'lladhī faṭaranī, a-fa-lā taʿqilūn · wa yā qawmi'staghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayhi yursili'l-samāʾa ʿalaykum midrāran wa yazidkum quwwatan ilā quwwatikum wa lā tatawallaw mujrimīn*

"**And to ʿĀd [We sent] their brother Hūd. He said: 'My people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. You are only inventing.** · **My people! I ask you no payment for this; my payment is due only from the One who brought me into being. Will you not use your minds?** · **My people! Ask forgiveness of your Lord, then turn back to Him, and He will send the sky upon you in abundant rain and add strength to your strength. And do not turn away as criminals.'**"

### The opening of the narrative — a new pattern

**The Noah narrative had opened with *wa laqad arsalnā*. The narratives of Hūd, Ṣāliḥ and Shuʿayb open with a different pattern:**

| Narrative | Opening | *akhāhum* |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah (25)** | *wa laqad arsalnā Nūḥan ilā qawmih* | **absent** |
| **Hūd (50)** | *wa **ilā ʿĀdin akhāhum** Hūdā* | **present** |
| **Ṣāliḥ (61)** | *wa **ilā Thamūda akhāhum** Ṣāliḥā* | **present** |
| **Shuʿayb (84)** | *wa **ilā Madyana akhāhum** Shuʿaybā* | **present** |
| **Abraham (69)** | *wa laqad jāʾat rusulunā Ibrāhīma bi'l-bushrā* | **absent** |
| **Moses (96)** | *wa laqad arsalnā Mūsā bi-āyātinā* | **absent** |

**Three narratives open with the same pattern, and in all three the verb is elided** (*arsalnā* is supplied). **This is a structure that can be verified from the text.**

**And the sense of the word *akhūhum* was treated at `026-suara.md` 26/106, and I rely on that treatment:** *"akh* here reports not brotherhood by lineage but **membership of the people**. In Arabic one of a community is called 'their brother' with reference to that community."

**And a datum recorded in al-Shuʿarāʾ holds here too:** in al-Shuʿarāʾ the people Shuʿayb addressed were named by a **place** (*aṣḥābu'l-Ayka*) and *akhūhum* had dropped out. **In Hūd, by contrast, those addressed are *Madyan* — again a place name; but here *akhāhum* is present.**

**This is a countable difference between the two sūras and must be recorded. In the classical sources it is disputed whether Madyan and al-Ayka are the same people or different ones; I do not impose a preference.**

### ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُ — a sentence repeated three times

**This sentence occurs three times in the sūra, word for word, in the mouths of three different prophets: 50 (Hūd), 61 (Ṣāliḥ), 84 (Shuʿayb).** This was tabulated in the section on the backbone.

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: command plus ground.**

| Part | Its work |
|---|---|
| *uʿbudū'llāh* | **The command** |
| *mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh* | **The ground** — negation + the redundant *min*: absolute negation |

**In Arabic the *min* in the structure *mā lakum min ilāhin* is a redundant particle and generalises the negation: "you have no god whatsoever."** This is a grammatical note.

### إِنْ أَنتُمْ إِلَّا مُفْتَرُونَ — an addition peculiar to Hūd

**And Hūd adds a sentence that the other two prophets do not say: "you are only inventing."**

The root **ف-ر-ي** was treated at the thirteenth and thirty-fifth verses (*iftarā*, *muftarayāt*).

**And the distribution of the word across the sūra is worth recording:**

| Verse | Who says it to whom |
|---|---|
| **13** | **The addressees → the Prophet**: *iftarāh* |
| **18** | **The verse → the wrongdoers**: *iftarā ʿalā'llāhi kadhibā* |
| **21** | **The verse → the wrongdoers**: *mā kānū yaftarūn* |
| **35** | **The addressees → the Prophet**: *iftarāh* |
| **50** | **Hūd → his people**: *in antum illā muftarūn* |

**Five occurrences, two directions.** **The charge is mutual: each side accuses the other of inventing.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### إِلَّا عَلَى ٱلَّذِى فَطَرَنِى — the change in the attribution

**The sentence about payment was *illā ʿalā'llāh* with Noah (29); here it is *illā ʿalā'lladhī faṭaranī* — "from the One who brought me into being".**

The root **ف-ط-ر**: **to split, to open for the first time.** **The root was the name of `035-fatir.md` and was worked out there. I rely on that treatment.** In summary: *faṭr* is **to bring a thing into being for the first time, with no model**; the concrete core of the word is **a splitting open**.

**And the placing of the attribution here is worth recording, and I give it as an observation about the word order:** in saying "my payment is due from God", the sentence names God **by the attribute of creating**. **Payment, that is, is asked of the one who gave the work.**

### يُرْسِلِ ٱلسَّمَآءَ عَلَيْكُم مِّدْرَارًا وَيَزِدْكُمْ قُوَّةً

**And the fifty-second verse gives concrete shape to the promise of the sūra's third verse.**

| Verse | The promise |
|---|---|
| **3** | *yumattiʿkum **matāʿan ḥasanan** ilā ajalin musammā* — **general** |
| **52** | *yursili'l-samāʾa **midrārā** wa yazidkum **quwwatan** ilā quwwatikum* — **two concrete items** |

The root **د-ر-ر**: **to flow abundantly** (it is used of milk flowing). *Midrār* — an intensive form: **falling in abundance.**

**And *al-samāʾ* here means "rain"** — in Arabic the sky, being the place rain comes from, is used figuratively for rain. **This is a note about the language.**

**And the construction *quwwatan ilā quwwatikum* must be recorded: an existing strength is conceded, and an addition to it is promised.**

**I record this as an observation:** the strength of the people of ʿĀd is mentioned elsewhere in the Qurʾān (`089-fecr.md` 89/8; the monuments and strongholds at `026-suara.md` 26/128-130; *man ashaddu minnā quwwa* at `041-fussilet.md` 41/15). **The verse does not deny that strength; it says it can be increased.**

**A limit, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** no rule such as "those who believe will gain political or economic power" is to be drawn from this. **The wording of the verse is a promise spoken to a particular people inside a particular call.**

---

## 11/53-55 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰهُودُ مَا جِئْتَنَا بِبَيِّنَةٍ وَمَا نَحْنُ بِتَارِكِىٓ ءَالِهَتِنَا عَن قَوْلِكَ وَمَا نَحْنُ لَكَ بِمُؤْمِنِينَ · إِن نَّقُولُ إِلَّا ٱعْتَرَىٰكَ بَعْضُ ءَالِهَتِنَا بِسُوٓءٍ قَالَ إِنِّىٓ أُشْهِدُ ٱللَّهَ وَٱشْهَدُوٓا۟ أَنِّى بَرِىٓءٌ مِّمَّا تُشْرِكُونَ · مِن دُونِهِۦ فَكِيدُونِى جَمِيعًا ثُمَّ لَا تُنظِرُونِ

*qālū yā Hūdu mā jiʾtanā bi-bayyinatin wa mā naḥnu bi-tārikī ālihatinā ʿan qawlika wa mā naḥnu laka bi-muʾminīn · in naqūlu illā'ʿtarāka baʿḍu ālihatinā bi-sūʾ, qāla innī ushhidu'llāha wa'shhadū annī barīʾun mimmā tushrikūn · min dūnihī fa-kīdūnī jamīʿan thumma lā tunẓirūn*

"**They said: 'Hūd! You have brought us no clear proof. We are not going to leave our gods at your word, nor are we going to believe you.** · **We only say that one of our gods has struck you with some harm.' He said: 'I call God to witness, and you bear witness too, that I am free of what you associate with Him** · **besides Him. So plot against me, all of you together, and give me no respite!'**"

### Three occurrences of *mā* — the people's refusal

**The fifty-third verse answers to the threefold negation of the twenty-seventh. I set the two verses side by side:**

| | **Noah's people (27)** | **Hūd's people (53)** |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *mā narāka illā basharan mithlanā* | *mā jiʾtanā bi-**bayyina*** |
| 2 | *mā narāka'ttabaʿaka illā'l-arādhil* | *mā naḥnu bi-**tārikī ālihatinā*** |
| 3 | *mā narā lakum ʿalaynā min faḍl* | *mā naḥnu laka bi-**muʾminīn*** |

**Both peoples frame three negative sentences each.** **The difference: Noah's people were saying what they *saw*; Hūd's people are saying what they *will not do*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the first is a **claim**, the second a **decision**.

### مَا جِئْتَنَا بِبَيِّنَةٍ — the fifth occurrence of *bayyina*

**As was tabulated above (11/17), *bayyina* occurs five times in the sūra, and four of them report that it is in the hands of the prophets or the believers.** **This is the one negative occurrence.**

**And what must be recorded is this: three verses after this one (56) Hūd will bring a proof** — *mā min dābbatin illā huwa ākhidhun bi-nāṣiyatihā.*

### ٱعْتَرَىٰكَ بَعْضُ ءَالِهَتِنَا بِسُوٓءٍ — the kind of accusation

The root **ع-ر-و**: **to come upon a thing, to strike it suddenly.** *Iʿtarā* (Form VIII) — **to come upon someone, to fasten on him.**

**And the content of the accusation must be recorded: Hūd is not called "a liar"; he is called "struck".**

**This belongs to a family of accusations repeated in the Qurʾān** — the charge of *majnūn* (possessed) was treated at `068-kalem.md` 68/2, `037-saffat.md` 37/36 and `052-tur.md` 52/29. **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded, and it is an observation about the word order: the accusation ascribes an *act* to their own gods.** **The people, that is, claim that their gods can do something.** **And Hūd's answer will land exactly there: "so plot against me, all of you together."**

### إِنِّىٓ أُشْهِدُ ٱللَّهَ وَٱشْهَدُوٓا۟ — two witnessings

**The same root twice in the sentence, in two different directions:**

| Expression | Who witnesses |
|---|---|
| *ushhidu'llāh* | **God** — called to witness |
| *wa'shhadū* | **The people** — asked to bear witness |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** Hūd makes the very side that denies him **a witness to his own declaration.** This is an address out of the ordinary.

### فَكِيدُونِى جَمِيعًا ثُمَّ لَا تُنظِرُونِ — the challenge

The root **ك-ي-د**: **a hidden plot, a covert plan.** **The root was treated at `086-tarik.md` 86/15-16 and `105-fil.md` 105/2.**

The root **ن-ظ-ر**: here in Form IV — *anẓara*: **to grant time, to allow a respite.**

**And the structure of the sentence must be recorded: a prophet invites everyone who is against him, all at once and without limit of time.**

**The same pattern occurs elsewhere in the Qurʾān:** *thumma kīdūnī fa-lā tunẓirūn* (al-Aʿrāf 7/195); *fa-ajmiʿū amrakum wa shurakāʾakum* (Yūnus 10/71). **These are of the same family.**

**And the ground for this challenge will come in the next verse: *innī tawakkaltu ʿalā'llāh*.**

---

## 11/56-57 — إِنِّى تَوَكَّلْتُ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ رَبِّى وَرَبِّكُم مَّا مِن دَآبَّةٍ إِلَّا هُوَ ءَاخِذٌۢ بِنَاصِيَتِهَآ إِنَّ رَبِّى عَلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ · فَإِن تَوَلَّوْا۟ فَقَدْ أَبْلَغْتُكُم مَّآ أُرْسِلْتُ بِهِۦٓ إِلَيْكُمْ وَيَسْتَخْلِفُ رَبِّى قَوْمًا غَيْرَكُمْ وَلَا تَضُرُّونَهُۥ شَيْـًٔا إِنَّ رَبِّى عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ حَفِيظٌ

*innī tawakkaltu ʿalā'llāhi rabbī wa rabbikum, mā min dābbatin illā huwa ākhidhun bi-nāṣiyatihā, inna rabbī ʿalā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm · fa-in tawallaw fa-qad ablaghtukum mā ursiltu bihī ilaykum, wa yastakhlifu rabbī qawman ghayrakum wa lā taḍurrūnahū shayʾā, inna rabbī ʿalā kulli shayʾin ḥafīẓ*

"'**I have put my trust in God, my Lord and your Lord. There is no creature that moves but He holds it by its forelock. My Lord is indeed on a straight path.**' · '**If you turn away, I have delivered to you what I was sent with. My Lord will put another people in your place, and you will do Him no harm at all. My Lord is indeed the Keeper of everything.**'"

### مَا مِن دَآبَّةٍ إِلَّا … — the link with the sixth verse

**And the fifty-sixth verse uses the same pattern as the sixth verse of the sūra. I set the two sentences side by side:**

| Verse | Expression | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| **6** | *wa **mā min dābbatin** fi'l-arḍi **illā** ʿalā'llāhi rizquhā* | **Provision** |
| **56** | ***mā min dābbatin illā** huwa ākhidhun bi-nāṣiyatihā* | **Being held** |

**The same pattern (*mā min … illā*), the same word (*dābba*), two different predicates.** **This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verbal overlap between the two verses:** the sūra says two things with the same sentence pattern — **the feeding is His, and so is the holding.** **And the second comes in Hūd's mouth immediately after a challenge:** it is the ground of the sentence "plot against me".

### ءَاخِذٌۢ بِنَاصِيَتِهَا — to take by the forelock

The root **ن-ص-و**: *nāṣiya* — **the hair over the forehead, the forelock.** **The word was treated at `096-alak.md` 96/15-16 (*la-nasfaʿan bi'l-nāṣiya · nāṣiyatin kādhibatin khāṭiʾa*), and I rely on that treatment.**

**And the concrete background of the idiom must be recorded:** in Arabic, to take someone by the forelock means **to bring him wholly under one's command**; in war a captive is held by the forelock, and so is a mount. **The idiom, that is, reports an absolute mastery.**

**And the work of the idiom here: to the people's claim that their gods can do something to him, Hūd answers by saying that *every living thing is held by its forelock*.** This is a link that can be read from the text.

### إِنَّ رَبِّى عَلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ

**The sentence occurs in the Qurʾān only here in this form, and it is striking.**

**Several explanations are transmitted in the classical sources, and I give them in a table:**

| Explanation | What it says |
|---|---|
| 1 | **"My Lord is upon justice"** — there is no swerving in His judgement |
| 2 | **"My Lord stands at the head of the road that is travelled"** — everyone comes to Him; no one escapes |
| 3 | **"My Lord's work is upon rightness"** — there is no departure from wisdom in His acts |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`): no description of place is to be drawn from this; the principle of `042-sura.md` 42/11 holds.**

**The construction *ṣirāṭin mustaqīm* was worked out in `001-fatiha.md`.**

### فَقَدْ أَبْلَغْتُكُم — the limit of the messenger

The root **ب-ل-غ**: **to reach a place, to arrive.** *Iblāgh* — **to convey.**

**And the drawing of the messenger's limit with the word *balāgh* was treated at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/35, and I rely on that treatment.**

**And the mood of the verb must be recorded: *qad ablaghtukum* — perfect plus *qad*.** In Arabic *qad* plus a perfect reinforces **the completion of the act**. **Hūd, that is, is announcing that his task is done.**

### وَيَسْتَخْلِفُ رَبِّى قَوْمًا غَيْرَكُمْ

The root **خ-ل-ف**: **to come after, to take the place of.** **The root was treated at `038-sad.md` 38/26 and `024-nur.md` 24/55.**

**And the side of the sentence that must be recorded is this: the replacement is given not as a threat but as a *way things work*.** **And the same line was treated at `047-muhammed.md` 47/38 (*wa in tatawallaw yastabdil qawman ghayrakum*). I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 11/58-60 — وَلَمَّا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا نَجَّيْنَا هُودًا وَٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ مَعَهُۥ بِرَحْمَةٍ مِّنَّا وَنَجَّيْنَٰهُم مِّنْ عَذَابٍ غَلِيظٍ · وَتِلْكَ عَادٌ جَحَدُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِ رَبِّهِمْ وَعَصَوْا۟ رُسُلَهُۥ وَٱتَّبَعُوٓا۟ أَمْرَ كُلِّ جَبَّارٍ عَنِيدٍ · وَأُتْبِعُوا۟ فِى هَٰذِهِ ٱلدُّنْيَا لَعْنَةً وَيَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ أَلَآ إِنَّ عَادًا كَفَرُوا۟ رَبَّهُمْ أَلَا بُعْدًا لِّعَادٍ قَوْمِ هُودٍ

*wa lammā jāʾa amrunā najjaynā Hūdan wa'lladhīna āmanū maʿahū bi-raḥmatin minnā wa najjaynāhum min ʿadhābin ghalīẓ · wa tilka ʿĀdun jaḥadū bi-āyāti rabbihim wa ʿaṣaw rusulahū wa'ttabaʿū amra kulli jabbārin ʿanīd · wa utbiʿū fī hādhihi'l-dunyā laʿnatan wa yawma'l-qiyāma, alā inna ʿĀdan kafarū rabbahum, alā buʿdan li-ʿĀdin qawmi Hūd*

"**When Our command came, We delivered Hūd and those who believed with him by a mercy from Us; We delivered them from a heavy punishment.** · **Such was ʿĀd: they denied the signs of their Lord knowing them for what they were, disobeyed His messengers, and followed the command of every obstinate tyrant.** · **They were pursued by a curse in this world and on the Day of Resurrection. Take note: ʿĀd denied their Lord. Take note: away with ʿĀd, the people of Hūd!**"

### The closing pattern — the sharpest departure from al-Shuʿarāʾ

**In al-Shuʿarāʾ the ʿĀd narrative closed thus:** *fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-ahlaknāhum · inna fī dhālika la-āya…* (26/139-140) — **the destruction in one verb, the seal in two verses.**

**In Hūd the closing runs to three verses and its structure is different:**

| Verse | Expression | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **58** | *najjaynā Hūdan wa'lladhīna āmanū maʿah* | **Deliverance** — before the destruction |
| **59** | *jaḥadū · ʿaṣaw · ittabaʿū* | **The counting of the offence** — three verbs |
| **60** | *utbiʿū … laʿna · alā buʿdan li-ʿĀd* | **The outcome** |

**And the most striking difference is in the first row: in Sūrat Hūd the *deliverance* is told before the destruction is told.**

**This holds in three narratives at once (58, 66, 94) and was tabulated in the section on the backbone.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the shared construction of the three verses:** the seal of al-Shuʿarāʾ said *"most of them did not believe"* — **it looked at number.** **The closing of Hūd, by contrast, names first those who were delivered** — their number is not given, but **their existence** is reported. This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### عَذَابٍ غَلِيظٍ — the concreteness of the adjective

The root **غ-ل-ظ**: **thickness, hardness, coarseness.** The concrete sense of the word is the thickness of a fabric.

**And the construction occurs in a few places in the Qurʾān** (Ibrāhīm 14/17; Fuṣṣilat 41/50; Luqmān 31/24 — *ʿadhābin ghalīẓ*). **It has occurred in `031-lokman.md` and `041-fussilet.md`.**

### جَحَدُوا۟ — root ج-ح-د

The root **ج-ح-د**. The sense the lexicographers record is worth setting down: ***jaḥd* is to deny what one knows** — not, that is, a failure to know, but **a refusal to accept while knowing.**

**And the lexicographers record another use of the root as well: *arḍun jaḥdatun* — land of poor yield, dry ground.** What the two senses have in common is **withholding**.

**The root was treated at `027-neml.md` 27/14 (*wa jaḥadū bihā wa'stayqanathā anfusuhum*), and I rely on that treatment** — there the same thing was said outright: **while their own souls were certain of it.**

### وَٱتَّبَعُوٓا۟ أَمْرَ كُلِّ جَبَّارٍ عَنِيدٍ

The root **ج-ب-ر**: **to set a thing right by force, to bind a fracture; and from there, to compel.** *Jabbār* — **a tyrant.** **The word was treated at `050-kaf.md` 50/45 (*wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-jabbār*), and I rely on that treatment.**

The root **ع-ن-د**: **to swerve from the truth, to be obstinate.** *ʿAnīd* — **one who holds out knowingly.**

**And the side of the sentence that must be recorded is the third verb. I give this as an observation about the word order:**

| # | Verb | Its direction |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *jaḥadū bi-āyāti rabbihim* | **Upward** — rejecting the signs |
| 2 | *ʿaṣaw rusulah* | **Against the messenger** |
| 3 | ***ittabaʿū** amra kulli jabbārin ʿanīd* | **Downward** — **following** someone |

**The third of the three verbs is positive: they followed.** **The list, that is, counts not only the rejection but what was put in its place.**

**And the word *rusulah* is plural: "His messengers".** One messenger had come to ʿĀd (Hūd); **the plural shows that rejecting one messenger is counted as rejecting the institution of messengership.** **The same structure was tabulated in `026-suara.md`** — there each people denied *al-mursalīn* (plural). **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 11/61 — وَإِلَىٰ ثَمُودَ أَخَاهُمْ صَٰلِحًا قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥ هُوَ أَنشَأَكُم مِّنَ ٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱسْتَعْمَرَكُمْ فِيهَا فَٱسْتَغْفِرُوهُ ثُمَّ تُوبُوٓا۟ إِلَيْهِ إِنَّ رَبِّى قَرِيبٌ مُّجِيبٌ

*wa ilā Thamūda akhāhum Ṣāliḥā, qāla yā qawmi'ʿbudū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, huwa anshaʾakum mina'l-arḍi wa'staʿmarakum fīhā fa'staghfirūhu thumma tūbū ilayh, inna rabbī qarībun mujīb*

"**And to Thamūd [We sent] their brother Ṣāliḥ. He said: 'My people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. It is He who brought you into being from the earth and set you to work its cultivation. So ask forgiveness of Him, then turn back to Him. My Lord is indeed near, and He answers.'**"

### وَٱسْتَعْمَرَكُمْ فِيهَا — the word worked out in detail

**This word occurs in the Qurʾān only here, and it needs treating in detail.**

The root **ع-م-ر**. **The root has occurred in several places across this series** (`030-rum.md`, `036-yasin.md`, `052-tur.md`) **but this derivative is being worked out here for the first time.**

**The concrete core of the root is given by the lexicographers in two directions, and the two come out at the same place:**

| Direction | Sense |
|---|---|
| **Time** | **To live long, to have a span of life** — *ʿumur*, *ʿumr* |
| **Place** | **To make a place thrive, to build it up** — *ʿimāra*, *maʿmūr* |

**The lexicographers join the two directions thus: for a place to be *maʿmūr* is for life to *continue* there.** An empty place is *kharāb*; a place lived in is *ʿāmir*. **The sense of place, that is, is a consequence of the sense of time.**

**The derivatives of the root:**

| Derivative | Sense |
|---|---|
| **عُمْر / عَمْر** | A lifetime, a span of life |
| **عِمَارَة** | Making a place thrive; a building |
| **مَعْمُور** | Made to thrive, lived in |
| **عَمَّرَ** | He gave long life; or he built up |
| **ٱعْتَمَرَ** | He visited — *ʿumra* comes from here |
| **ٱسْتَعْمَرَ** | **The verb here** — Form X |

**And that the word *ʿumra* is from the same root is worth recording:** the lexicographers attach it to the sense of **making a place thrive by visiting it.**

### The pattern itself: ٱسْتِعْمَار — the work of Form X

**The verb is in Form X (*istifʿāl*), and in Arabic this form has three main functions:**

| Function | Example | Its fit here |
|---|---|---|
| **1. To seek** | *istaghfara* — he sought forgiveness | *"He asked you to cultivate it"* |
| **2. To find or count a thing so** | *istaḥsana* — he found it beautiful | Does not fit here |
| **3. To set someone to a task, to appoint him** | *istaʿmala* — he set someone to a work | *"He set you to its cultivation"* |

**The renderings given in the classical sources for the verb *istaʿmara* in the verse rest on the first and the third of these functions. I give them in a table:**

| Reading | Sense |
|---|---|
| 1 | **"He appointed you to cultivate it"** — the cultivation is given as a task |
| 2 | **"He settled you there, He made you dwell in it"** — the sense of settlement |
| 3 | **"He gave you long life there"** — the sense of time |
| 4 | **"He asked you to cultivate it"** — the sense of request |

**All four readings are transmitted in the classical sources; I do not impose a preference.** **But what the four have in common must be recorded: in every one of them a *lasting* relationship is set up between the human being and the ground.**

### The two halves of the sentence — creation and task

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: two verbs come one after the other, and in both the subject is God and the object is the human being.**

| Verb | What it gives | From where / where |
|---|---|---|
| *anshaʾakum* | **Existence** | *mina'l-arḍ* — **from the earth** |
| *istaʿmarakum* | **A task / a span** | *fīhā* — **in it** |

**The two prepositions differ, and the difference carries meaning: *min* ("from") and *fī* ("in").**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the opposition of the two particles:** the human being is named both as the **product** of the same thing (*mina'l-arḍ*) and as the one **charged with it** (*fīhā*). **The sentence, that is, does not leave the relation with the ground running one way only: the place taken from is the place worked.**

The root **ن-ش-أ**: **the gradual coming into being of a thing, its rising.** **The root was treated at `053-necm.md` 53/32 and `029-ankebut.md` 29/20 (*thumma'llāhu yunshiʾu'l-nashʾata'l-ākhira*).**

### What this says to the present

**The word has acquired a political sense in present-day Arabic, and I cite it as a note about the language:** in modern Arabic *istiʿmār* is used for **colonialism**. **This is a use the word acquired later and it has nothing to do with the meaning of the verse.**

**I record it explicitly, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** the verse is not speaking of a political concept; **and this commentary does not enter into current politics.** What is recorded is the idea in the root of the word: **making a place fit to be lived in.**

**And the relation the verse sets up is, as my own reading, this:** the earth is given to the human being not as a **possession** but as a **work.** **And the ground for this is that the verb is in Form X** — the third function of the form (setting someone to a task) supports this reading. **This is an explanation and it is not binding.**

### إِنَّ رَبِّى قَرِيبٌ مُّجِيبٌ

The root **ق-ر-ب**: nearness. The root **م-ج-ب / ج-و-ب**: **to answer, to respond.**

**And the coming together of the two names is worth recording: *qarīb* reports distance, *mujīb* reports response.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence comes immediately after **a command to ask forgiveness.** **The nearness, that is, is placed there as the ground for the call's not going unanswered.** **At `002-bakara.md` 2/186 (*fa-innī qarīb, ujību daʿwata'l-dāʿ*) the same two roots occur together.**

---

## 11/62-63 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰصَٰلِحُ قَدْ كُنتَ فِينَا مَرْجُوًّا قَبْلَ هَٰذَآ أَتَنْهَىٰنَآ أَن نَّعْبُدَ مَا يَعْبُدُ ءَابَآؤُنَا وَإِنَّنَا لَفِى شَكٍّ مِّمَّا تَدْعُونَآ إِلَيْهِ مُرِيبٍ · قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ أَرَءَيْتُمْ إِن كُنتُ عَلَىٰ بَيِّنَةٍ مِّن رَّبِّى وَءَاتَىٰنِى مِنْهُ رَحْمَةً فَمَن يَنصُرُنِى مِنَ ٱللَّهِ إِنْ عَصَيْتُهُۥ فَمَا تَزِيدُونَنِى غَيْرَ تَخْسِيرٍ

*qālū yā Ṣāliḥu qad kunta fīnā marjuwwan qabla hādhā, a-tanhānā an naʿbuda mā yaʿbudu ābāʾunā wa innanā la-fī shakkin mimmā tadʿūnā ilayhi murīb · qāla yā qawmi a-raʾaytum in kuntu ʿalā bayyinatin min rabbī wa ātānī minhu raḥmatan fa-man yanṣurunī mina'llāhi in ʿaṣaytuh, fa-mā tazīdūnanī ghayra takhsīr*

"**They said: 'Ṣāliḥ! Before this you were one among us in whom hope was placed. Are you now forbidding us to serve what our fathers served? We are in disquieting doubt about what you call us to.'** · **He said: 'My people! Tell me: what if I stand on a clear proof from my Lord, and He has given me a mercy from Himself? If I disobeyed Him, who would help me against God? You would add nothing to me but loss.'**"

### قَدْ كُنتَ فِينَا مَرْجُوًّا — the only entry of its kind in the Qurʾān

The root **ر-ج-و**: **to hope, to expect.** *Marjuww* — a passive participle: **one in whom hope is placed, one who is looked to.**

**This expression occurs in the Qurʾān only here, and it is a rare entry reporting a prophet's social standing before his call.**

**I record this as an observation:** the people saw Ṣāliḥ, before his call, in **a high place**, and they admit it. **The objection, that is, does not arise from a mistrust of the man but from what he says.**

**And this is the reverse of the objection in the twenty-seventh verse:** there Noah was told "you are a human being like us"; **here Ṣāliḥ is told "you were one we set our hopes on".** **Two peoples, two different grounds, the same outcome.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

### أَتَنْهَىٰنَآ أَن نَّعْبُدَ مَا يَعْبُدُ ءَابَآؤُنَا — the argument from the forefathers

**This objection is one of the most repeated objections in the Qurʾān, and it has been treated across this series:**

- **`043-zuhruf.md` 43/22-23** — *innā wajadnā ābāʾanā ʿalā ummatin wa innā ʿalā āthārihim muhtadūn*; there the sense of *umma* as "the line followed" was tabulated and the structure of the argument worked out. **I rely on that treatment.**
- **`031-lokman.md` 31/21** — *bal nattabiʿu mā wajadnā ʿalayhi ābāʾanā*. **I rely on that treatment.**
- **`026-suara.md`** — the answer of Abraham's people (26/74).

**What belongs here is that the objection comes in the form of a *question*: *a-tanhānā*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** in al-Zukhruf and in Luqmān the argument from the forefathers was a **statement** ("we found our fathers thus"). **In Hūd it takes the form of an *accusation*: "are you forbidding us?"**

**And the same form will recur with Shuʿayb's people: *a-ṣalātuka taʾmuruka an natruka mā yaʿbudu ābāʾunā* (87).** **Two peoples, the same structure: a question plus the forefathers plus a verb.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it will be tabulated in its place.

### فِى شَكٍّ … مُّرِيبٍ — two roots, one semantic field

The root **ش-ك-ك**: **doubt.** The lexicographers attach the root to the idea of "piercing through a thing" — two possibilities running into one another.

The root **ر-ي-ب**: **misgiving, a doubt that leaves one uneasy.** **The root was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/2 (*lā rayba fīh*).**

**And the coming together of the two words is worth recording: *shakkin murīb* — "a doubt that disquiets".**

**The lexicographers count this construction an intensification.** **And the distinction recorded in the al-Baqara section is useful here:** *rayb* is not merely a lack of knowledge but a doubt that **produces unease**.

**And the construction occurs three times in the sūra:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **62** | *la-fī **shakkin** mimmā tadʿūnā ilayhi **murīb*** | **Thamūd** |
| **110** | *la-fī **shakkin** minhu **murīb*** | **Those who differ over the Book of Moses** |
| **17** | *fa-lā taku fī **miryatin** minh* | **A command to the Prophet** — the same field |

**This is a repetition that can be verified from the text, and it will be treated separately at 11/110.**

### فَمَا تَزِيدُونَنِى غَيْرَ تَخْسِيرٍ

The root **خ-س-ر** was treated above (11/22). **The pattern here is the verbal noun of Form II: *takhsīr* — "to bring to loss".**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: *mā tazīdūnanī ghayra takhsīr* — "you would add nothing to me but loss".**

**The verb is a verb of *ziyāda* (increase), and its object is *takhsīr* (diminution).** **The sentence, that is, brings two opposed words together: what you add is a subtraction.**

**I record this as an observation about the language; it is a stylistic subtlety well known in Arabic, and it gives the sentence an edge.**

---

## 11/64-65 — وَيَٰقَوْمِ هَٰذِهِۦ نَاقَةُ ٱللَّهِ لَكُمْ ءَايَةً فَذَرُوهَا تَأْكُلْ فِىٓ أَرْضِ ٱللَّهِ وَلَا تَمَسُّوهَا بِسُوٓءٍ فَيَأْخُذَكُمْ عَذَابٌ قَرِيبٌ · فَعَقَرُوهَا فَقَالَ تَمَتَّعُوا۟ فِى دَارِكُمْ ثَلَٰثَةَ أَيَّامٍ ذَٰلِكَ وَعْدٌ غَيْرُ مَكْذُوبٍ

*wa yā qawmi hādhihī nāqatu'llāhi lakum āyatan fa-dharūhā taʾkul fī arḍi'llāhi wa lā tamassūhā bi-sūʾin fa-yaʾkhudhakum ʿadhābun qarīb · fa-ʿaqarūhā fa-qāla tamattaʿū fī dārikum thalāthata ayyām, dhālika waʿdun ghayru makdhūb*

"'**My people! This is the she-camel of God, a sign for you. Let her be, to eat in the earth of God, and do not touch her with harm, or a punishment close at hand will seize you.**' · **But they hamstrung her. So he said: 'Enjoy yourselves in your homes for three days. That is a promise that will not be belied.'**"

### نَاقَةُ ٱللَّهِ — the work of the genitive construction

**The she-camel is mentioned in several sūras of the Qurʾān** (`026-suara.md` 26/155, `007-araf.md` — not yet in this series, `054-kamer.md` 54/27, `091-sems.md` 91/13). **It was treated in `026-suara.md` and `091-sems.md`; I rely on that treatment.**

**And the construction must be recorded: *nāqatu'llāh* — "the she-camel of God".**

**This is what in Arabic is called the genitive of honour (*iḍāfat al-tashrīf*): attributing a thing to God in order to raise it** (*baytu'llāh*, *nāqatu'llāh*). **The she-camel, that is, is attributed not in point of ownership but in point of *standing*.**

**And immediately afterwards comes a second genitive construction: *fī arḍi'llāh* — "in the earth of God".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the two genitives stand one after the other. **Both the camel and the ground are attributed to the same place** — that is, the people are left with no claim of right either to the camel or to the ground she grazes on.

### لَكُمْ ءَايَةً — what the sign is

***Āya* here means "sign, proof".** **And why the she-camel is a sign — the Qurʾān does not explain this in Hūd.**

**A note, per the rule:** in the classical commentaries detailed reports are transmitted about how the she-camel appeared, how much water she drank, which rock she came out of. **None of this is in the Qurʾān; it is not passed on in this commentary.**

**What the Qurʾān gives is three items, and all of them are here:** the she-camel is a **sign**, she is to be **left free** in the earth of God, and she is **not to be touched.** **A fourth item is given at al-Qamar 54/28: the turns at the water** (*al-māʾu qismatun baynahum*) — **treated in `054-kamer.md`.**

### فَذَرُوهَا تَأْكُلْ — the bareness of the command

**The command is not a command to *do* but a command to *leave alone*: *dharūhā* — "let her be".**

**I record this as an observation:** what is asked of the people is not an act of worship, a building or a payment; **it is not to interfere.** **And the test lies exactly there.**

### فَعَقَرُوهَا — a single verb

The root **ع-ق-ر**: **to wound, to cut the legs; to bring an animal down by cutting its legs before slaughtering it.** From the same root come *ʿāqir* (barren) and *ʿuqr* (the root, the base of a thing).

**And the economy of the verse is worth recording:** after a long sentence of command, the verse answers with a single verb — *fa-ʿaqarūhā.* **In between there is nothing at all: no argument, no reason, no interval.**

**And the verb is plural: "they hamstrung her".** **In another place the Qurʾān reports that the act was done by a single person** (*fa-nādaw ṣāḥibahum fa-taʿāṭā fa-ʿaqar* — al-Qamar 54/29; *idhi'nbaʿatha ashqāhā* — al-Shams 91/12). **These two verses were treated in `054-kamer.md` and `091-sems.md`.**

**And the difference between the two tellings is attached to a principle, and I record it relying on `091-sems.md`:** one person committed the act and the responsibility is ascribed to the community — **because the community did not prevent him.** **And Sūrat al-Shams says this outright: *fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-ʿaqarūhā*.**

### ثَلَٰثَةَ أَيَّامٍ — a numbered span

**And the span given must be recorded: three days.**

**This is on the same line as *ummatin maʿdūda* ("a numbered span") in the eighth verse of the sūra.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**ذَٰلِكَ وَعْدٌ غَيْرُ مَكْذُوبٍ** — **and the expression is built with a passive participle: *ghayru makdhūb* — "not belied / not to be belied".**

**I record this as a grammatical observation:** the sentence does not say *ghayru kādhib* ("not lying"). **The passive form reports that the promise cannot be belied *from the other side*.** **The truth of the word, that is, does not depend on the addressee's acceptance.**

---

## 11/66-68 — فَلَمَّا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا نَجَّيْنَا صَٰلِحًا وَٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ مَعَهُۥ بِرَحْمَةٍ مِّنَّا وَمِنْ خِزْىِ يَوْمِئِذٍ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ هُوَ ٱلْقَوِىُّ ٱلْعَزِيزُ · وَأَخَذَ ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟ ٱلصَّيْحَةُ فَأَصْبَحُوا۟ فِى دِيَٰرِهِمْ جَٰثِمِينَ · كَأَن لَّمْ يَغْنَوْا۟ فِيهَآ أَلَآ إِنَّ ثَمُودَا۟ كَفَرُوا۟ رَبَّهُمْ أَلَا بُعْدًا لِّثَمُودَ

*fa-lammā jāʾa amrunā najjaynā Ṣāliḥan wa'lladhīna āmanū maʿahū bi-raḥmatin minnā wa min khizyi yawmiʾidh, inna rabbaka huwa'l-qawiyyu'l-ʿazīz · wa akhadha'lladhīna ẓalamū'l-ṣayḥatu fa-aṣbaḥū fī diyārihim jāthimīn · ka-an lam yaghnaw fīhā, alā inna Thamūda kafarū rabbahum, alā buʿdan li-Thamūd*

"**When Our command came, We delivered Ṣāliḥ and those who believed with him by a mercy from Us, and from the disgrace of that day. Your Lord is indeed the Strong, the Mighty.** · **And the Cry seized those who had done wrong, and morning found them fallen prone in their homes** · **— as though they had never dwelt there. Take note: Thamūd denied their Lord. Take note: away with Thamūd!**"

### ٱلصَّيْحَة — root ص-ي-ح

Root: **ص-ي-ح** — **to shout aloud.** *Ṣayḥa* — **a single cry.**

**The word occurs in many places in the Qurʾān as the instrument of destruction and has been treated across this series** (`036-yasin.md` 36/29 and 36/49, `015-hicr.md` — not in this series, `054-kamer.md` 54/31 — there *ṣayḥatan wāḥida*). **I rely on `036-yasin.md`.**

**And in Hūd the word occurs twice: for Thamūd (67) and for Midian (94) — in word-for-word the same sentence.**

| Verse | People | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **67** | **Thamūd** | *wa akhadha'lladhīna ẓalamū'l-ṣayḥatu fa-aṣbaḥū fī diyārihim jāthimīn* |
| **94** | **Midian** | *wa akhadhati'lladhīna ẓalamū'l-ṣayḥatu fa-aṣbaḥū fī diyārihim jāthimīn* |

**The two sentences are identical but for a single letter (the feminine *tāʾ* of the verb).** **This is the longest word-for-word repetition in Sūrat Hūd, and it can be verified from the text.**

**I record this as an observation and compare it with the seal technique of `026-suara.md`:** in al-Shuʿarāʾ the repetition was **the closing formula**; in Hūd the repetition is **the depiction of the destruction.** **What is repeated in Hūd, that is, is not the outcome but the event itself.**

### جَٰثِمِين — the picture in the word

The root **ج-ث-م**: **to sink down in a place and stay there, to collapse onto the knees.** The lexicographers record that the word is used of **a bird crouching to the ground** as well.

**And the choice of word must be recorded: the verse does not say "they died".** **It says "they were left fallen prone in their homes"** — the body in its place, and no life in it.

### كَأَن لَّمْ يَغْنَوْا۟ فِيهَا

The root **غ-ن-ي**: **to have no need of a thing; and to remain in a place, to dwell.** *Maghnā* — **the place one lives in.**

**And the sentence occurs twice in Hūd: 68 (Thamūd) and 95 (Midian).** **Both in the same position: immediately after the depiction of the destruction.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence is one of **following a trace** — that when one looks back, nothing at all is left. **And at `026-suara.md` 26/128-129 the monuments and strongholds of ʿĀd were mentioned.** **The two depictions can be read together: the things built were thought to be lasting.**

### إِنَّ رَبَّكَ هُوَ ٱلْقَوِىُّ ٱلْعَزِيزُ — the change of addressee

**And at the end of the sixty-sixth verse the address changes: *inna rabbak* — "your Lord".**

**Inside the narrative, in the middle of a third-person telling, the text turns suddenly to the Prophet.** This is the technique called *iltifāt* in Arabic (a shift in the direction of address), and it has been treated in many places across this series.

**And the choice of names must be recorded: *al-Qawiyyu'l-ʿAzīz*.** **The attribute that stood out in Thamūd was strength** (carving houses out of the mountains — `026-suara.md` 26/149). **The name cited at the closing is likewise a name of strength.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the sūra closes each narrative with a name suited to that narrative's subject. **This is a choice that departs from the fixed seal of al-Shuʿarāʾ (each time *al-ʿAzīzu'l-Raḥīm*).**

**And it can be verified as a table:**

| Narrative | The name(s) at the closing | The subject of the narrative |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah** | *ghafūrun raḥīm* (41), *bi-salām* (48) | **Deliverance** |
| **Hūd** | *ḥafīẓ* (57) | **Protection** — "plot against me" |
| **Ṣāliḥ** | ***al-Qawiyyu'l-ʿAzīz*** (66) | **Strength** |
| **Shuʿayb** | *raḥīmun wadūd* (90) | **The call to return** |

**Four narratives, four different pairs of names.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

---

## 11/69 — وَلَقَدْ جَآءَتْ رُسُلُنَآ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ بِٱلْبُشْرَىٰ قَالُوا۟ سَلَٰمًا قَالَ سَلَٰمٌ فَمَا لَبِثَ أَن جَآءَ بِعِجْلٍ حَنِيذٍ

*wa laqad jāʾat rusulunā Ibrāhīma bi'l-bushrā, qālū salāman, qāla salām, fa-mā labitha an jāʾa bi-ʿijlin ḥanīdh*

"**Our messengers came to Abraham with the good news. They said 'Peace'; he said 'Peace', and without delay he brought a roasted calf.**"

### Two tellings of the scene — a comparison with al-Dhāriyāt

**This scene was treated in detail at `051-zariyat.md` 51/24-30, and I rely on that treatment without reservation.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> The concrete sense of the root **ض-ي-ف**: **to incline to one side, to draw near, to join.** *Ḍayf* — "one who draws near, who joins". **A guest is not one who enters the house from outside but one who is *added* to the house.**
>
> **In the exchange of greetings the two sides do not say the same word:** the guests say *salāman* (accusative — a verbal clause, coming to be), Abraham says *salāmun* (nominative — a nominal sentence, fixity). **Abraham returns the greeting given to him in a more lasting pattern.**
>
> **رَاغَ** — "to withdraw to one side quietly, without being noticed". The verse does not say "he went".
>
> **سَمِين** — "fat". **Abraham slaughters the best of the flock for three travellers he does not know.**
>
> **The question *a-lā taʾkulūn*** and **the gravity of refusing food as a sign in the Arab custom of hospitality.**

**And in `051-zariyat.md` this verse (Hūd 11/69) was already cited:** *"The telling in another sūra of the Qurʾān adds one detail: 'without delay he brought a roasted calf' — the note *mā labitha* (he did not delay) there is consistent with the speed the *fāʾ* conjunctions give here."*

**I now compare the two tellings as a table. All the rows are data of the text:**

| Element | **al-Dhāriyāt 51/24-30** | **Hūd 11/69-73** |
|---|---|---|
| **Opening** | *hal atāka ḥadīthu **ḍayfi** Ibrāhīma'l-mukramīn* — the form of a question | *wa laqad jāʾat **rusulunā** Ibrāhīma bi'l-bushrā* — a statement |
| **What the comers are called** | ***ḍayf*** — "guests" | ***rusulunā*** — "Our messengers" |
| **The reason for the visit** | **Not stated at the outset** — disclosed in verse 32 | ***bi'l-bushrā*** — **stated from the start** |
| **The greeting** | *qālū salāman · qāla salām* | *qālū salāman · qāla salām* — **word for word the same** |
| **The first reaction** | *qawmun munkarūn* — "a people not known" | **Absent** |
| **The verb of withdrawing** | ***fa-rāgha** ilā ahlih* — he slipped away quietly | **Absent** |
| **The adjective of the calf** | ***samīn*** — fat | ***ḥanīdh*** — roasted |
| **The note of speed** | The *fa-* conjunctions | ***fa-mā labitha*** — "he did not delay" |
| **The verb of offering** | *fa-qarrabahū ilayhim* + *a-lā taʾkulūn* | **Absent** — straight to the noticing |
| **The noticing** | *fa-awjasa minhum khīfa* | *nakirahum wa **awjasa** minhum khīfa* |
| **The wife's reaction** | *fa-aqbalati'mraʾatuhū fī ṣarratin fa-ṣakkat wajhahā* — she came with a cry and struck her face | ***fa-ḍaḥikat*** — she laughed |
| **The content of the good news** | *bi-ghulāmin **ʿalīm*** | *bi-**Isḥāq** wa min warāʾi Isḥāqa **Yaʿqūb*** |
| **The going to Lot** | 32-37 — in detail | 74-83 — **in greater detail** |

**Three data points emerge from the table and must be recorded:**

**One — the two sūras open up different parts of the same scene.** al-Dhāriyāt gives detail on **the manners of hospitality** (slipping out quietly, the fatness, the offering, "will you not eat?"). **Hūd compresses the hospitality into a single sentence and moves on to the *good news* and to *Lot*.**

**Two — Hūd names the comers *rusulunā* from the start; al-Dhāriyāt says *ḍayf*.** **Hūd, that is, gives the reader the information from the outset, and al-Dhāriyāt does not.**

**Three — the content of the good news is given differently.** al-Dhāriyāt states **an attribute** (*ʿalīm* — knowing); **Hūd states two *names*: Isaac (Isḥāq) and Jacob (Yaʿqūb).**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the two tellings do not conflict — **they are two different frames on the same scene.** The frame of al-Dhāriyāt is **the receiving of guests**, the frame of Hūd is **the continuation of the line and the deliverance of Lot.** This also suits the whole of each sūra: Hūd is a sequence of narratives, and the links of the sequence are being tied to one another.

### حَنِيذ — the word worked out

The root **ح-ن-ذ**. **The root occurs here for the first time in this series, and in the Qurʾān it is found only in this verse.**

**The sense the lexicographers give: *ḥanīdh* — meat cooked on hot stones, or in a pit with fire beneath it.** Some of the lexicographers explain it as **"cooked so well that it drips"**; others say **"roasted on stone"**.

| Explanation | What it says |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Meat cooked in a pit with heated stones** |
| 2 | **Well roasted meat, dripping with its own fat** |
| 3 | **Roasted whole** |

**All three are passed on as ascribed to the lexicographers; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what the word adds to the verse must be recorded:** *samīn* (al-Dhāriyāt) reported **the quality of the animal**; ***ḥanīdh* reports the manner of the cooking.** **The two sūras give two different details of the same act of hospitality.**

### فَمَا لَبِثَ — not delaying

The root **ل-ب-ث**: **to stay in a place, to linger, to wait.** **The root was treated at `018-kehf.md` 18/12 and 18/25 (*labithū*).**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: *fa-mā labitha an jāʾa* — "he did not delay in bringing it".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse makes the measure not **the size** of the hospitality but **its speed**. **And in al-Dhāriyāt this was conveyed by three *fāʾ* conjunctions.** The two sūras say the same thing by two different means of language.

---

## 11/70 — فَلَمَّا رَءَآ أَيْدِيَهُمْ لَا تَصِلُ إِلَيْهِ نَكِرَهُمْ وَأَوْجَسَ مِنْهُمْ خِيفَةً قَالُوا۟ لَا تَخَفْ إِنَّآ أُرْسِلْنَآ إِلَىٰ قَوْمِ لُوطٍ

*fa-lammā raʾā aydiyahum lā taṣilu ilayhi nakirahum wa awjasa minhum khīfa, qālū lā takhaf innā ursilnā ilā qawmi Lūṭ*

"**When he saw that their hands did not reach it, he found them strange, and a fear of them entered him. 'Do not be afraid,' they said, 'we have been sent to the people of Lot.'**"

### The concreteness of the scene

**This moment was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/27-28, and I rely on that treatment.** In summary, the entries there were these:

> **What Abraham notices, that is, is concrete: the hands do not reach out.**
>
> **In Arab custom a guest's refusal of food is a grave sign: to eat is to make a bond with the host.** A man who refuses the food is saying that he does not want that bond. **Abraham's fear, that is, is not an idle fear but a correct inference.**
>
> **أَوْجَسَ** — root **و-ج-س**: *wajs* is **a faint, barely perceptible sound**; *awjasa* — he felt it within. **The fear here is not terror but an intimation.**
>
> **The fear of the prophets is not presented in the Qurʾān as a fault; it is recorded as a human reaction.** And the angels' answer does not rebuke the fear.

**And in `051-zariyat.md` this verse (Hūd 11/70) was already cited.**

**Two differences belong here:**

**One — in Hūd there is one more verb: *nakirahum*.** The root **ن-ك-ر** — **not to recognise, to find strange** — was treated at `051-zariyat.md` 51/25 (*qawmun munkarūn*).

**And the place of the root differs in the two sūras, and this must be recorded:**

| | **al-Dhāriyāt 51/25** | **Hūd 11/70** |
|---|---|---|
| **Where the root stands** | **At the start of the scene** — at the door | **In the middle of the scene** — at the table |
| **What is found strange** | **Their identity** — *qawmun munkarūn* | **Their behaviour** — *nakirahum* |

In al-Dhāriyāt, that is, the strangeness is noticed **at the entrance**; in Hūd **at the table.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

**Two — in Hūd the angels' answer contains a *ground*: *innā ursilnā ilā qawmi Lūṭ*.** **In al-Dhāriyāt this information is given later (51/32).**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** Hūd gives the information at once — because this section of the sūra is moving towards **the narrative of Lot.** **al-Dhāriyāt delays the scene, because there the subject is the receiving of guests itself.**

---

## 11/71-73 — وَٱمْرَأَتُهُۥ قَآئِمَةٌ فَضَحِكَتْ فَبَشَّرْنَٰهَا بِإِسْحَٰقَ وَمِن وَرَآءِ إِسْحَٰقَ يَعْقُوبَ · قَالَتْ يَٰوَيْلَتَىٰٓ ءَأَلِدُ وَأَنَا۠ عَجُوزٌ وَهَٰذَا بَعْلِى شَيْخًا إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَشَىْءٌ عَجِيبٌ · قَالُوٓا۟ أَتَعْجَبِينَ مِنْ أَمْرِ ٱللَّهِ رَحْمَتُ ٱللَّهِ وَبَرَكَٰتُهُۥ عَلَيْكُمْ أَهْلَ ٱلْبَيْتِ إِنَّهُۥ حَمِيدٌ مَّجِيدٌ

*wa'mraʾatuhū qāʾimatun fa-ḍaḥikat fa-bashsharnāhā bi-Isḥāqa wa min warāʾi Isḥāqa Yaʿqūb · qālat yā waylatā a-alidu wa anā ʿajūzun wa hādhā baʿlī shaykhā, inna hādhā la-shayʾun ʿajīb · qālū a-taʿjabīna min amri'llāh, raḥmatu'llāhi wa barakātuhū ʿalaykum ahla'l-bayt, innahū ḥamīdun majīd*

"**His wife was standing there, and she laughed. So We gave her the good news of Isaac, and after Isaac of Jacob.** · **She said: 'Alas for me! Shall I bear a child when I am an old woman and this husband of mine an old man? This is indeed an astonishing thing!'** · **They said: 'Do you wonder at the affair of God? The mercy of God and His blessings are upon you, people of this house. He is worthy of all praise, full of glory.'**"

### فَضَحِكَتْ — the verb that is disputed

The root **ض-ح-ك**: **to laugh.** **The root was treated at `080-abese.md` 80/39 (*ḍāḥikatun mustabshira*) and `053-necm.md` 53/43 (*wa annahū huwa aḍḥaka wa abkā*).**

**The sense of the verb here is disputed. I give it in a table:**

| Reading | What *ḍaḥikat* is | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **She laughed** — the common sense of the word | The primary sense of the root |
| 2 | **She was glad / relieved** — on hearing that the guests were angels and were going to the people of Lot | The content of the preceding verse |
| 3 | **She menstruated** — some of the lexicographers record this sense of the word too | Its link with the news of the birth in the next verse |

**The third explanation is transmitted as ascribed to the lexicographers; some of them reject this sense.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**And there is a question about the order of the laughing as well:**

| Reading | The order |
|---|---|
| A | **She laughed first, then the good news came** — the literal order of the verse |
| B | **The good news came first, then she laughed** — counted as fronting and postponement (*taqdīm–taʾkhīr*) |

**Both readings are transmitted.** **The literal order of the verse is A; B has been proposed in order to explain the reason for the laughter.** **I do not impose a preference.**

### وَٱمْرَأَتُهُۥ قَآئِمَةٌ — her position in the scene

**The verse gives one detail: the woman is *standing*.** **In al-Dhāriyāt, by contrast, *fa-aqbalati'mraʾatuhū fī ṣarra* — "she came forward with a cry".**

**I record this as an observation and record the difference between the two sūras:** in al-Dhāriyāt there is movement (she came), in Hūd a stance (standing). **The two depictions may be showing two moments of the same scene; I impose no preference.**

### يَٰوَيْلَتَىٰٓ — the pattern itself

***yā waylatā* is a vocative pattern in Arabic reporting astonishment or amazement.** Word for word it means "woe to me", **but in use it is less an imprecation than an *exclamation of astonishment*.**

**The pattern occurs in a few places in the Qurʾān** (`025-furkan.md` 25/28 — *yā waylatā laytanī lam attakhidh fulānan khalīlā*; al-Kahf 18/49; al-Māʾida 5/31). **It was treated in `025-furkan.md`.**

**And the difference of the use here must be recorded, and this is an observation about the language:** in the other places the pattern reported **regret**; **here it reports *amazement*.** **The pattern in itself carries no value.**

### ءَأَلِدُ وَأَنَا۠ عَجُوزٌ وَهَٰذَا بَعْلِى شَيْخًا

**And the construction of the objection must be recorded: the age of both sides is named.**

The root **ع-ج-ز**: **to be without strength, to be unable to do a thing.** *ʿAjūz* — **an old woman.** The idea of "inability" in the root of the word is worth recording.

The root **ش-ي-خ**: *shaykh* — **an old man.**

The root **ب-ع-ل**: *baʿl* — **husband, spouse.** The lexicographers record the sense "owner, master" for the word as well.

**And the parsing of the word *shaykhan* is a grammatical question: it is in the accusative (a circumstantial accusative), whereas the predicate of the nominal sentence *hādhā baʿlī* would be in the nominative.** **The grammarians explain it as a circumstantial accusative: "this is my husband, being an old man."** This is a grammatical note.

### أَتَعْجَبِينَ مِنْ أَمْرِ ٱللَّهِ — the kind of answer

The root **ع-ج-ب**: **to wonder.** **The root was treated at `050-kaf.md` 50/2 (*bal ʿajibū*) and `013-rad.md` 13/5 (*wa in taʿjab fa-ʿajabun qawluhum*). I rely on that treatment.**

**And the two occurrences of the root are inside the same exchange:**

| Who | Expression |
|---|---|
| **The woman** | *inna hādhā la-shayʾun **ʿajīb*** |
| **The messengers** | *a-**taʿjabīna** min amri'llāh* |

**The answer hands back the word of the question.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And the finding recorded in `013-rad.md` works here too:** *"In al-Raʿd the wonder is returned to the one addressed."* **The same technique is present in Hūd: the verb of wondering is turned back on the one who wonders.**

**And the content of the answer must be recorded: the answer gives not an *explanation* but a *frame*** — *min amri'llāh.* **How the thing will happen, that is, is not told; whose affair it is, is.**

### أَهْلَ ٱلْبَيْت — the scope of the address

**The construction *ahla'l-bayt* occurs here as a vocative: "people of this house".**

**A note, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** this construction occurs in one other place in the Qurʾān (al-Aḥzāb 33/33) **and sectarian arguments have been conducted throughout history over whom it covers there.** **That verse was treated in `033-ahzab.md`.** **In this commentary no side is taken.**

**What belongs here is this: at Hūd 11/73 the address is plain — Abraham's house is meant.** **And the pronoun is masculine plural: *ʿalaykum*** — although the person addressed is a woman. **The address, that is, covers not one person but a *house*.** This is a grammatical note.

### حَمِيدٌ مَّجِيد

The root **ح-م-د**: praise. The root **م-ج-د**: **honour, elevation, amplitude.** The lexicographers explain *majd* as "a thing's being abundant and high".

**The two names come here after a piece of good news.** This is consistent with the sūra's line of closing each narrative with names suited to its subject (tabulated above).

---

## 11/74-76 — فَلَمَّا ذَهَبَ عَنْ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ ٱلرَّوْعُ وَجَآءَتْهُ ٱلْبُشْرَىٰ يُجَٰدِلُنَا فِى قَوْمِ لُوطٍ · إِنَّ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ لَحَلِيمٌ أَوَّٰهٌ مُّنِيبٌ · يَٰٓإِبْرَٰهِيمُ أَعْرِضْ عَنْ هَٰذَآ إِنَّهُۥ قَدْ جَآءَ أَمْرُ رَبِّكَ وَإِنَّهُمْ ءَاتِيهِمْ عَذَابٌ غَيْرُ مَرْدُودٍ

*fa-lammā dhahaba ʿan Ibrāhīma'l-rawʿu wa jāʾathu'l-bushrā yujādilunā fī qawmi Lūṭ · inna Ibrāhīma la-ḥalīmun awwāhun munīb · yā Ibrāhīmu aʿriḍ ʿan hādhā, innahū qad jāʾa amru rabbik, wa innahum ātīhim ʿadhābun ghayru mardūd*

"**When the alarm had gone from Abraham and the good news had come to him, he began to dispute with Us about the people of Lot.** · **Abraham was indeed forbearing, given to sighing, ever turning back.** · '**Abraham! Turn away from this. The command of your Lord has already come. A punishment that will not be turned back is coming to them.**'"

**These three verses set up one of the most striking comparisons in the sūra — and probably in this series. They need treating in detail.**

### يُجَٰدِلُنَا — the root ج-د-ل, a disputation not condemned

**The root was the backbone of `040-gafir.md`, and five occurrences were tabulated there.** The concrete sense of the root — **to twist a rope tight**, to throw an opponent in wrestling — **was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/197.** **And at `018-kehf.md` 18/54 it was named as the human being's most marked characteristic** (*wa kāna'l-insānu akthara shayʾin jadalā*). **The root had occurred at `013-rad.md` 13/13 as well** (*wa hum yujādilūna fi'llāh*).

**The measure recorded in Ghāfir was this, and I bring it here as it stands:**

> **I record this as my own reading:** the sūra takes denial not as a thought but as **a posture in argument.** And in the fourth verse the measure of it is given: ***bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum*** — **with no proof that has come into their hands.** What is condemned, that is, is not disputation itself but **its being carried on without a ground.**

**And Hūd 11/74 is the strongest confirmation of that measure. For here a prophet disputes *with God* and is *not condemned*.**

**I set the two texts side by side. All the rows are data of the text:**

| | **Ghāfir 40/4, 5, 35, 56, 69** | **Hūd 11/74** |
|---|---|---|
| **The verb** | *yujādilu* / *jādalū* | ***yujādilu*** — **the same verb, the same form** |
| **Who** | *alladhīna kafarū* — those who disbelieve | ***Abraham*** — a prophet |
| **About what** | *fī āyāti'llāh* — about the signs of God | *fī qawmi Lūṭ* — **about the fate of a people** |
| **Against whom** | Against the messengers / against the truth | ***-nā*** — **directly with God** |
| **Its ground** | *bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum* — **without proof** | **Not stated** — but three attributes of praise follow at once |
| **The assessment** | **Condemned** — *kabura maqtan ʿinda'llāh* (40/35) | ***Not condemned*** — *inna Ibrāhīma la-ḥalīmun awwāhun munīb* |
| **The outcome** | Punishment | **A gentle halt** — *aʿriḍ ʿan hādhā* |

**This is one of the strongest comparisons across this series, and it is read wholly from the text.**

**And I record the conclusion that follows as my own reading, its ground being the table above:**

**The Qurʾān does not condemn disputation as an *act*.** What is condemned in Ghāfir is disputation carried on **without a ground** and **in order to bring down the truth** (*wa jādalū bi'l-bāṭili li-yudḥiḍū bihi'l-ḥaqq* — 40/5). **In Hūd the same act is a man's intervening on behalf of a people about to be destroyed — and the verse places three attributes of praise directly after the act.**

**The measure, that is, lies not in the act but in *what it is for* and *what it rests on*.** **Ghāfir had given that measure itself; Hūd shows it in an example.**

### The subject and the limit of the disputation

**And *when* the disputation begins must be recorded: *fa-lammā dhahaba ʿan Ibrāhīma'l-rawʿu wa jāʾathu'l-bushrā*.**

| Condition | What happened |
|---|---|
| 1 | *dhahaba'l-rawʿ* — **the alarm went** |
| 2 | *jāʾathu'l-bushrā* — **the good news came** |

**Abraham, that is, begins to speak for others only *after* his own anxiety has passed and his own good news has arrived.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the position of the two conditions in the sentence.** **The verse makes the link and leaves it; it does not explain it.**

The root **ر-و-ع**: **fear, a start of alarm.** *Rawʿ* — **sudden fear.** **The word occurs in the Qurʾān only here.**

### إِنَّ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ لَحَلِيمٌ أَوَّٰهٌ مُّنِيبٌ — three attributes

**The place of the verse must be recorded: the sentence comes *immediately after* the verb of disputation.** **The three attributes, that is, are placed there as the ground of the disputation.**

#### حَلِيم — root ح-ل-م

**The root has been treated in many places across this series** (`017-isra.md`, `022-hac.md`, `037-saffat.md`, `052-tur.md`, `089-fecr.md`).

**The sense of the root: to hold back anger, not to hurry, to be patient when one has the power to punish.** **The lexicographers count the word the opposite of *jahl* (angry haste).**

**And the placing of the attribute *ḥalīm* here is worth recording:** Abraham's disputation is **a request that the punishment be delayed** — and *ḥilm* is exactly that.

**The word is used of Abraham once more in the Qurʾān: *inna Ibrāhīma la-awwāhun ḥalīm* (al-Tawba 9/114).** **And at al-Ṣāffāt 37/101 the good news of Isaac is given with *bi-ghulāmin ḥalīm* — treated in `037-saffat.md`.**

#### أَوَّٰه — root أ-و-ه

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series. The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān: here and al-Tawba 9/114.**

**The source of the root is an *interjection*: أَوْه / آه — "ah".** The word, that is, is derived from **a sound**. The same sound, and the same field of meaning, as the English "ah".

**And *awwāh* is in the intensive form (*faʿʿāl*): "one who sighs much".**

**More than one rendering is given for the word in the classical sources, and I give them in a table:**

| Rendering | What it says |
|---|---|
| 1 | **One who feels deeply, who sighs much** — the literal sense of the word |
| 2 | **One who prays much** |
| 3 | **One full of mercy, who aches at another's condition** |
| 4 | **One who remembers God much / glorifies Him much** |
| 5 | **One who possesses sure knowledge** — a sense recorded by some of the lexicographers |

**All five are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But there is something to record, and this is an observation about the language: the root of the word is a sound, and that sound is the sound of pain.** **The first three renderings are consistent with that sound.**

**And the placing of the word here supports my own reading, and I give it as an explanation:** Abraham is intervening for a people about to be destroyed. **The verse calls him "one who sighs".** **The source of the disputation, that is, is not a proof but a *pain*.**

#### مُنِيب — root ن-و-ب

The root **ن-و-ب**: **to return to a place again and again.** **The root was treated in `030-rum.md`, `034-sebe.md`, `038-sad.md`, `042-sura.md`, `050-kaf.md` and `060-mumtehine.md`.**

***Munīb* — "one who returns constantly".** **And the lexicographers record the root's link with *nawba* (a turn):** a thing's coming round **by repetition**.

**And the word will come back in Shuʿayb's mouth before the ninety-seventh verse of the sūra: *wa ilayhi unīb* (88).** **Two occurrences, two prophets.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### The arrangement of the three attributes

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the three attributes:**

| Attribute | Its direction |
|---|---|
| *ḥalīm* | **Towards the other** — not to hurry |
| *awwāh* | **Inward** — to feel the pain |
| *munīb* | **Upward** — to turn back |

**The three attributes look in three different directions, and together they explain a man's intervening on another's behalf.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### يَٰٓإِبْرَٰهِيمُ أَعْرِضْ عَنْ هَٰذَا — the form of the halting

**And the disputation is ended with a command. The construction of the command must be recorded, and it is the finest point in the verse.**

**The sentence is not a rebuke. It has three parts:**

| # | Expression | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *yā Ibrāhīm* | **Address by name** |
| 2 | *aʿriḍ ʿan hādhā* | **A command** — "turn away from this" |
| 3 | *innahū qad jāʾa amru rabbik…* | **A ground** — the decision has been made |

**And the giving of the ground must be recorded: the command is not left without a reason.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order and compare it with Noah's case:**

| | **Noah 11/46** | **Abraham 11/76** |
|---|---|---|
| **The address** | *yā Nūḥ* | *yā Ibrāhīm* |
| **The command** | *fa-lā tasʾalni mā laysa laka bihī ʿilm* | *aʿriḍ ʿan hādhā* |
| **The ground** | **A lack of knowledge** | **That the decision has been made** |
| **The addition** | *innī aʿiẓuka an takūna mina'l-jāhilīn* — **a warning** | **None** |
| **What precedes** | **None** | **Three attributes of praise (75)** |

**The two scenes are alike, but the grounds differ, and the difference can be read from the text:** Noah had asked for something he **did not know**; **Abraham had intervened against something he did know.** **In the one what is lacking is knowledge, in the other the decision is already closed.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding.**

### عَذَابٌ غَيْرُ مَرْدُودٍ

The root **ر-د-د**: **to send back, to turn back.** *Mardūd* — a passive participle: **turned back.**

**And it is the same pattern as *ghayru makdhūb* in the sixty-fifth verse:** *ghayru* plus a passive participle.

| Verse | Expression | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| **65** | *waʿdun **ghayru makdhūb*** | **The word cannot be belied** |
| **76** | *ʿadhābun **ghayru mardūd*** | **The punishment cannot be turned back** |

**The same pattern, in two narratives.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### What this says to the present

**The Qurʾān shows a prophet intervening on behalf of others and does not name it a fault. On the contrary, it places three attributes of praise directly after the attempt.**

**And when the attempt is stopped, there is no rebuke either; there is a piece of information: the decision has been made.**

**I record this as a reading:** the verses separate the fact that a judgement is settled from the legitimacy of the pain felt at that judgement. **The second is not condemned, even though it does not change the first.**

---

## 11/77 — وَلَمَّا جَآءَتْ رُسُلُنَا لُوطًا سِىٓءَ بِهِمْ وَضَاقَ بِهِمْ ذَرْعًا وَقَالَ هَٰذَا يَوْمٌ عَصِيبٌ

*wa lammā jāʾat rusulunā Lūṭan sīʾa bihim wa ḍāqa bihim dharʿan wa qāla hādhā yawmun ʿaṣīb*

"**When Our messengers came to Lot he was distressed on their account, his powers were straitened by them, and he said: 'This is a hard day.'**"

### Two receptions, two reactions

**And the difference between the two scenes must be recorded: the same messengers go to two houses and meet two different responses.**

| | **Abraham (69-70)** | **Lot (77)** |
|---|---|---|
| **The first reaction** | *qāla **salām*** and a calf | ***sīʾa** bihim wa **ḍāqa** bihim dharʿā* |
| **When the fear comes** | **Afterwards** — when the hands do not reach out | **At once** — when they arrive |
| **Fear of what** | **The identity of the guests** | **What will happen to the guests** |
| **What is said** | — | *hādhā yawmun ʿaṣīb* |

**And the difference is explained in the next verse: Lot's people come running.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** both prophets are receiving people they do not know. **What makes the difference is not the guests but the *surroundings*.**

### ضَاقَ بِهِمْ ذَرْعًا — the concrete source of the idiom

The root **ذ-ر-ع**: **the arm; and a measure of the length of an arm** (*dhirāʿ* — a cubit). The Turkish unit of length *arşın* is attached to this root.

**And the concrete picture in the idiom must be recorded, and this is an observation about the language:** in Arabic *ḍāqa dharʿuhū* — "his arm was straitened" — means that a person **has not the power** to do a thing. Its source is the situation of a man who cannot get his arms around a load.

| Idiom | The concrete picture | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| *ḍāqa dharʿan* | **The arms do not reach** | He has not the power, he is at a loss |

**And *dharʿan* is in the accusative as a specifier (*tamyīz*): "he was straitened as to his arm".** This is a grammatical note.

**And the root ض-ي-ق occurs for the second time in the sūra:**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **12** | **The Prophet** | *wa **ḍāʾiq**un bihī ṣadruk* |
| **77** | **Lot** | *wa **ḍāqa** bihim dharʿā* |

**The same root, two messengers.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it ties the sūra's opening sentence to the narrative.

### يَوْمٌ عَصِيبٌ

The root **ع-ص-ب**: **to bind, to wrap, to knot.** From the same root come *ʿiṣāba* (a headband), *ʿaṣaba* (a person's kin on the father's side — those bound to one another) and *ʿaṣab* (a nerve, a sinew).

***ʿAṣīb* — "tight as a knot, hard".** **The word occurs in the Qurʾān only here.**

**And the picture in the word is worth recording:** for a day to be "knotted" is for **the way out to be closed.**

---

## 11/78-80 — وَجَآءَهُۥ قَوْمُهُۥ يُهْرَعُونَ إِلَيْهِ وَمِن قَبْلُ كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتِ قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ بَنَاتِى هُنَّ أَطْهَرُ لَكُمْ فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَلَا تُخْزُونِ فِى ضَيْفِىٓ أَلَيْسَ مِنكُمْ رَجُلٌ رَّشِيدٌ · قَالُوا۟ لَقَدْ عَلِمْتَ مَا لَنَا فِى بَنَاتِكَ مِنْ حَقٍّ وَإِنَّكَ لَتَعْلَمُ مَا نُرِيدُ · قَالَ لَوْ أَنَّ لِى بِكُمْ قُوَّةً أَوْ ءَاوِىٓ إِلَىٰ رُكْنٍ شَدِيدٍ

*wa jāʾahū qawmuhū yuhraʿūna ilayhi wa min qablu kānū yaʿmalūna'l-sayyiʾāt, qāla yā qawmi hāʾulāʾi banātī hunna aṭharu lakum fa'ttaqū'llāha wa lā tukhzūni fī ḍayfī, a-laysa minkum rajulun rashīd · qālū laqad ʿalimta mā lanā fī banātika min ḥaqqin wa innaka la-taʿlamu mā nurīd · qāla law anna lī bikum quwwatan aw āwī ilā ruknin shadīd*

"**His people came rushing to him — and before this they had been doing evil deeds. He said: 'My people! Here are my daughters; they are purer for you. So be mindful of God and do not disgrace me before my guests. Is there not one right-minded man among you?'** · **They said: 'You know that we have no right to your daughters; and you know well what we want.'** · **He said: 'If only I had the strength to face you, or could take shelter with some strong support!'**"

### يُهْرَعُونَ — the pattern of the word

The root **ه-ر-ع**: **to walk fast and unsteadily, to run as though dragged along.** **The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān: here and al-Ṣāffāt 37/70** (*fa-hum ʿalā āthārihim yuhraʿūn* — treated in `037-saffat.md`).

**And the verb is in the passive pattern: *yuhraʿūna* — "they are made to rush".**

**I record this as a grammatical observation:** the pattern gives the impression that the movement is **not of their own will** — a being dragged. **The lexicographers explain it as "as though someone were pushing them".**

**And the use in `037-saffat.md` is in the same pattern, and there too a being dragged along in the footsteps of the forefathers was described.** **Two occurrences, the same passive pattern.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### وَمِن قَبْلُ كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتِ

**And the verse inserts a parenthesis: this is not something happening for the first time.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence takes the event out of being **a single excess** and records it as **a settled condition.** **And the ground of the sentence of destruction is here: continuance.**

### هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ بَنَاتِى هُنَّ أَطْهَرُ لَكُمْ — the sentence that is disputed

**I record it explicitly, per the method (`STYLE.md`): there is disagreement in the classical sources over this sentence, I give the disagreement in a table, and I issue no legal ruling.**

| Reading | Who *banātī* are | What the offer is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Lot's own daughters** | **An offer of marriage** — a call to the lawful way |
| 2 | **The women of the people** | A prophet stands in the position of **father** to his people; in saying "my daughters" he means the women of the people |
| 3 | **Not an offer but a redirection** | The sentence is not a bargain but **a pointing to what is right** |

**All three readings are transmitted in the classical sources; I do not impose a preference.**

**And these entries must be made:**

**One — the sentence itself sets up a *comparison*: *hunna aṭharu lakum* — "purer".** An elative compares two things. **The sentence, that is, is not a permission but a pointing of direction.**

**Two — the people's answer supports this reading: *mā lanā fī banātika min ḥaqq* — "we have no right to your daughters".** **The other side too, that is, understood the offer as a matter of *right*, and refused it.**

**Three — per the rule: no reports absent from the Qurʾān are entered into concerning the details of this scene, the description of the people's act, or the accounts about Lot's household.**

**Four — per the method (`STYLE.md`):** no legal ruling is to be drawn from this verse. **The verse transmits a moment of crisis; it does not lay down a rule.**

### وَلَا تُخْزُونِ فِى ضَيْفِى — the law of guests

The root **خ-ز-ي**: **to be disgraced, to be brought low.** **The root occurred in the thirty-ninth verse** (*ʿadhābun yukhzīh*).

The root **ض-ي-ف** — **was worked out at `051-zariyat.md` 51/24, and I rely on that treatment:** *"A guest is not one who enters the house from outside but one who is added to it. The legal weight of the Arab custom of hospitality rests in this word: from the moment he arrives the guest is a part of the house and under the host's protection."*

**And Lot's sentence rests exactly on that law: *fī ḍayfī* — "in the matter of my guests".** **The one to be disgraced, that is, is not the guests but the host.**

**This is directly connected with the historical background and is an application of the custom recorded in `051-zariyat.md`.**

### أَلَيْسَ مِنكُمْ رَجُلٌ رَّشِيدٌ — the weight of the question

The root **ر-ش-د**: **to find the right way, to come to maturity.** *Rashīd* — **right-minded, able to see what is right.**

**And the word occurs twice more in the sūra, and the three occurrences build a table:**

| Verse | Expression | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *a-laysa minkum rajulun **rashīd*** | **Lot's people** — a question |
| **87** | *innaka la-anta'l-ḥalīmu'l-**rashīd*** | **Shuʿayb** — spoken in mockery |
| **97** | *wa mā amru Firʿawna bi-**rashīd*** | **Pharaoh** — a verdict |

**Three occurrences, three different offices: a question, a mockery, a verdict.** **This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the distribution of the three verses:** the sūra uses the same word as a measure — **and in none of the three occurrences is the word granted to the one addressed.** With Lot the question goes unanswered, with Shuʿayb it is spoken in mockery, with Pharaoh it is plainly denied.

### لَوْ أَنَّ لِى بِكُمْ قُوَّةً أَوْ ءَاوِىٓ إِلَىٰ رُكْنٍ شَدِيدٍ

The root **ر-ك-ن**: **the firmest corner of a thing, a point of support.** *Rukn* — **a corner, a pillar, a support.**

**The root occurred in `017-isra.md` and `051-zariyat.md`** — at al-Dhāriyāt 51/39 *fa-tawallā bi-**ruknihī*** is used of Pharaoh. **The root will be worked out in detail at 11/113, because that is where the sūra really treats it.**

**What belongs here is this: the word occurs twice in the sūra, and the two occurrences are the reverse of one another.**

| Verse | Expression | Who | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| **80** | *aw **āwī ilā ruknin shadīd*** | **Lot** | **He looks for a support** — and finds none |
| **113** | *wa lā **tarkanū** ilā'lladhīna ẓalamū* | **The one addressed** | **Leaning is forbidden** |

**The same root, in two different directions.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it will be treated separately at 11/113.

**And note the mood of the sentence: *law* — a wish that has not come about.** **Lot, that is, has neither strength nor support in hand.**

**I record this as an observation:** the verse shows a prophet **entirely helpless**, and does not conceal it. **And in the next verse the support comes from outside: *innā rusulu rabbik*.**

---

## 11/81 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰلُوطُ إِنَّا رُسُلُ رَبِّكَ لَن يَصِلُوٓا۟ إِلَيْكَ فَأَسْرِ بِأَهْلِكَ بِقِطْعٍ مِّنَ ٱلَّيْلِ وَلَا يَلْتَفِتْ مِنكُمْ أَحَدٌ إِلَّا ٱمْرَأَتَكَ إِنَّهُۥ مُصِيبُهَا مَآ أَصَابَهُمْ إِنَّ مَوْعِدَهُمُ ٱلصُّبْحُ أَلَيْسَ ٱلصُّبْحُ بِقَرِيبٍ

*qālū yā Lūṭu innā rusulu rabbika lan yaṣilū ilayk, fa-asri bi-ahlika bi-qiṭʿin mina'l-layli wa lā yaltafit minkum aḥadun illā'mraʾatak, innahū muṣībuhā mā aṣābahum, inna mawʿidahumu'l-ṣubḥ, a-laysa'l-ṣubḥu bi-qarīb*

"**They said: 'Lot! We are the messengers of your Lord; they will never reach you. So set out with your household in a part of the night, and let none of you look back — except your wife; what befalls them will befall her. Their appointed time is the morning. Is the morning not near?**'"

### The support arrives

**The eighty-first verse is the answer to the eightieth, and I record this as an observation about the word order:**

| Verse | What Lot lacked | The answer that comes |
|---|---|---|
| **80** | *quwwatan* — **strength** | *lan yaṣilū ilayk* — **they will not reach you** |
| **80** | *ruknin shadīd* — **a support** | *innā rusulu **rabbik*** — **the messengers of your Lord** |

**Two lacks, two answers, in the same order.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### فَأَسْرِ بِأَهْلِكَ بِقِطْعٍ مِّنَ ٱلَّيْلِ

The root **س-ر-ي**: **to travel by night.** **The root occurred at `017-isra.md` 17/1 (*subḥāna'lladhī asrā bi-ʿabdihī laylā*), on the occasion of that sūra's name.**

The root **ق-ط-ع**: **to cut.** *Qiṭʿ* — **a piece, a cut-off portion.** **So *bi-qiṭʿin mina'l-layl* — "in a portion of the night".**

**I record this as an observation:** the command names no hour; **it says a part of the night.** **And the limit of time comes at the end of the verse from another quarter: *inna mawʿidahumu'l-ṣubḥ*.**
