### وَلَا يَلْتَفِتْ مِنكُمْ أَحَدٌ — not looking back

The root **ل-ف-ت**: **to turn a thing from its direction, to twist it round.** *Iltifāt* (Form VIII) — **to turn the head and look back.** **And in Arabic rhetoric *iltifāt* is also the source of the term used for a shift in the direction of address** — it has occurred in many places across this series.

**And the scope of the prohibition must be recorded: *minkum aḥad* — "not one of you".**

**Per the rule: why looking back is forbidden is not said in the Qurʾān. The explanations given in the classical sources are conjecture; I do not enter into them.**

### إِلَّا ٱمْرَأَتَكَ — the variant reading and its sense

**There is a variant reading in this exception, and the difference changes *what* the exception attaches to. I give it in a table:**

| Reading | Parsing | What the exception attaches to | Sense |
|---|---|---|---|
| ***illā'mraʾatAka*** (accusative) | An exception from *ahl* | ***fa-asri bi-ahlik*** | "Set out with your household — **except your wife**" |
| ***illā'mraʾatUka*** (nominative) | An exception from *aḥad* | ***wa lā yaltafit minkum aḥad*** | "Let none of you look back — **except your wife; she did look**" |

**Both readings are transmitted in the sources for the variant readings; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the difference between the two readings must be recorded:**

| | **The first reading** | **The second reading** |
|---|---|---|
| **Did the woman set out** | **No** — outside from the start | **Yes** — but she looked back |
| **Its fit with the rest of the sentence** | *innahū muṣībuhā mā aṣābahum* — consistent with both | The same |

**And both readings arrive at the same conclusion: *muṣībuhā mā aṣābahum* — "what befell them will befall her."**

**And this connects with the principle treated at `066-tahrim.md` 66/10** (the wives of Noah and of Lot). **I rely on that treatment.** **And it is on the same line as the forty-sixth verse of the sūra: nearness does not determine the scope.**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **46** | **Noah's son** | *laysa min ahlik* |
| **81** | **Lot's wife** | *illā'mraʾataka innahū muṣībuhā mā aṣābahum* |

**Two narratives, the same principle, the same sūra.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### أَلَيْسَ ٱلصُّبْحُ بِقَرِيبٍ — the work of the sentence

**And the verse ends with a question: "is the morning not near?"**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence gives no information; **it gives a consolation.** **And the one addressed is Lot** — that is, the side being asked to wait.

**And this is in the same field as *wa'ntaẓirū innā muntaẓirūn* (122) in the closing verses of the sūra.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

---

## 11/82-83 — فَلَمَّا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا جَعَلْنَا عَٰلِيَهَا سَافِلَهَا وَأَمْطَرْنَا عَلَيْهَا حِجَارَةً مِّن سِجِّيلٍ مَّنضُودٍ · مُّسَوَّمَةً عِندَ رَبِّكَ وَمَا هِىَ مِنَ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ بِبَعِيدٍ

*fa-lammā jāʾa amrunā jaʿalnā ʿāliyahā sāfilahā wa amṭarnā ʿalayhā ḥijāratan min sijjīlin manḍūd · musawwamatan ʿinda rabbik, wa mā hiya mina'l-ẓālimīna bi-baʿīd*

"**When Our command came, We turned the place upside down and rained upon it stones of baked clay, layer upon layer** · **— marked with your Lord. And it is not far from the wrongdoers.**"

### جَعَلْنَا عَٰلِيَهَا سَافِلَهَا

The root **ع-ل-و**: height. The root **س-ف-ل**: lowness. **Two opposed words in one sentence.**

**And the construction occurs once more in the Qurʾān: al-Ḥijr 15/74 — that sūra has not yet been treated in this series.**

### حِجَارَةً مِّن سِجِّيلٍ مَّنضُودٍ

**سِجِّيل** — **the lexicographers differ over the origin of the word.** Some count it an Arabic derivative; some record that it is **a word that came from Persian** (*seng* + *gil* — stone and clay). **Both views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**The word occurs in three places in the Qurʾān: here, al-Ḥijr 15/74 and al-Fīl 105/4.** **It was treated in `105-fil.md`, and I rely on that treatment.**

The root **ن-ض-د**: **to lay one thing on another, to stack.** *Manḍūd* — **laid in layers, stacked.** **The word occurred at `050-kaf.md` 50/10 (*ṭalʿun naḍīd*).**

### مُّسَوَّمَةً عِندَ رَبِّكَ — marked

The root **س-و-م**: **to put a mark on a thing, to brand it; and to turn an animal loose to graze.** *Musawwama* — **marked.**

**And the work of the word here must be recorded, and I give it as an observation about the word order:** the stones are not at random; **they are marked.** **The destruction, that is, is told not as an indiscriminate ruin but as a *targeted* work.**

**And the same word occurs, besides `003-al-i-imran.md` — not yet in this series — at `051-zariyat.md` 51/34 as well** (*musawwamatan ʿinda rabbika li'l-musrifīn*) **and the same scene is told there.** **The identical construction in two sūras: *musawwamatan ʿinda rabbik*.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

### وَمَا هِىَ مِنَ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ بِبَعِيدٍ — the closing of the Lot narrative

**As was tabulated in the section on the backbone, the Lot narrative takes none of the sūra's closing patterns** — neither *alā buʿdan*, nor *ka-an lam yaghnaw*, nor *najjaynā*.

**In their place comes a sentence of its own, and the addressee of the sentence changes.**

**Over the referent of the pronoun (*hiya*) there is disagreement:**

| Reading | What *hiya* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The stones** — those stones are not far from the wrongdoers |
| 2 | **The towns / those settlements** — geographically not far, they lie on the road |
| 3 | **That punishment / that event** — its like is always possible |

**All three readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what is striking is this: at the closing, in place of *buʿdan* (away with them) comes *bi-baʿīd* (not far) — the same root, in the opposite direction.**

| Narrative | The closing | How the root ب-ع-د is used |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah (44)** | *buʿdan li'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn* | **Putting far off** |
| **Hūd (60)** | *buʿdan li-ʿĀd* | **Putting far off** |
| **Ṣāliḥ (68)** | *buʿdan li-Thamūd* | **Putting far off** |
| **Lot (83)** | *wa mā hiya mina'l-ẓālimīna bi-**baʿīd*** | **Bringing near** |
| **Shuʿayb (95)** | *buʿdan li-Madyana kamā **baʿidat** Thamūd* | **Putting far off** |

**In four of the five narratives the root reports a putting far off; in Lot's it reports a nearness.** **This is one of the finest structural data in the sūra, and it is read wholly from the text.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** four of the closings look to the past (*"away with them"*), **the closing of Lot looks to the present** (*"it is not far"*). **And immediately after this sentence the sūra moves to Shuʿayb — and Shuʿayb will say exactly this to his people: *wa mā qawmu Lūṭin minkum bi-baʿīd* (89).**

**The two sentences are in word-for-word the same pattern, and they stand eight verses apart.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

---

## 11/84-86 — وَإِلَىٰ مَدْيَنَ أَخَاهُمْ شُعَيْبًا قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥ وَلَا تَنقُصُوا۟ ٱلْمِكْيَالَ وَٱلْمِيزَانَ

*wa ilā Madyana akhāhum Shuʿaybā · qāla yā qawmi'ʿbudū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh · wa lā tanquṣū'l-mikyāla wa'l-mīzān · innī arākum bi-khayrin wa innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin muḥīṭ*

"**And to Midian We sent their brother Shuʿayb. He said: 'My people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. Do not give short measure and short weight. I see you in prosperity, and I fear for you the punishment of an encompassing day.'**"

### The same sentence, a fifth time

**The fifth messenger comes in the sūra, and the opening sentence is again the same:** *uʿbudū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh* — **Noah (at 26, in different wording), Hūd (50), Ṣāliḥ (61) and now Shuʿayb (84).** **Identical, letter for letter, in three places.**

**But what follows changes each time**, and I record this as a structure of the sūra itself:

| Messenger | The second command that follows the call to God's oneness |
|---|---|
| Hūd (52) | *wa lā tatawallaw mujrimīn* |
| Ṣāliḥ (64) | *fa-dharūhā taʾkul fī arḍi'llāh* — **the inviolability of the she-camel** |
| **Shuʿayb (84-85)** | *lā tanquṣū'l-mikyāla wa'l-mīzān* — **measure and weight** |

**The sūra, that is, attaches the same root in each society to that society's own disease.** **And the disease of Midian is trade.**

### إِنِّىٓ أَرَىٰكُم بِخَيْرٍ — the abundance is itself a warning

**The order of the sentences must be recorded:** first *"I see you in prosperity"*, and immediately after *"I fear for you the punishment of an encompassing day"*.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** between the two sentences there is a *wa* (a *wāw*), not a *lākin*. **The prosperity, that is, is named not as the ground of the punishment but as set over against it** — a man with means in hand has no need to give short measure.

**مُحِيط** — root **ح-و-ط**: **to encircle completely.** **The word will come a second time shortly, in the same sūra** — at 92, *inna rabbī bimā taʿmalūna muḥīṭ.*

| Place | What *muḥīṭ* encompasses |
|---|---|
| 84 | **The day** — the day of punishment encompasses |
| 92 | **Knowledge** — the Lord has encompassed what they do |

**The two uses stand eight verses apart and are from the same root;** this is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

### وَلَا تَبْخَسُوا۟ ٱلنَّاسَ أَشْيَآءَهُمْ وَلَا تَعْثَوْا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مُفْسِدِينَ (85)

The root **ب-خ-س**: **to give a thing less than its due, to lower its value.** ***Thamanin bakhs* — a low price.** **The root was treated across the whole of `083-mutaffifin.md`;** I rely on that treatment.

**And the object of the word is worth recording: *al-nāsa ashyāʾahum* — "people of *their things*".** **Not the instrument of measure, but the other party's property.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the verse begins by speaking of the calibration of a balance and ends at **the right of a human being.** **And at the third step the sentence widens further: *wa lā taʿthaw fi'l-arḍi mufsidīn* — corruption on the earth.**

| Step | Scope |
|---|---|
| *lā tanquṣū'l-mikyāla* | **The instrument** |
| *lā tabkhasū'l-nāsa ashyāʾahum* | **The person** |
| *lā taʿthaw fi'l-arḍ* | **The earth** |

**Three steps: the consequence of the same act on three different scales** — and this ordering is the text's own.

### بَقِيَّتُ ٱللَّهِ خَيْرٌ لَّكُمْ إِن كُنتُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ (86)

The root **ب-ق-ي**: **to remain, what is left over.** ***Baqiyya* — the remaining share.**

**The exegetes differ over this construction, and the disagreement is not concealed:**

| View | What *baqiyyatu'llāh* means |
|---|---|
| A | **The lawful earning God has left you** — set against what is got by short measure |
| B | **The recompense with God, the reward** — set against what remains in this world |
| C | **Obedience to God's command itself** |

**No preference is adopted.** **But it must be recorded that the sentence is built with the pattern *khayrun lakum* (better for you):** a **comparison** is being made — that is, there is something standing over against it, and in this context that something is the gain got by giving short measure.

**وَمَآ أَنَا۠ عَلَيْكُم بِحَفِيظٍ** — "**I am not a keeper over you.**" **The same limit was treated at `006-enam.md` 6/107 (*wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-wakīl*) and `042-sura.md` 42/48.**

---

## 11/87-88 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰشُعَيْبُ أَصَلَوٰتُكَ تَأْمُرُكَ أَن نَّتْرُكَ مَا يَعْبُدُ ءَابَآؤُنَآ أَوْ أَن نَّفْعَلَ فِىٓ أَمْوَٰلِنَا مَا نَشَٰٓؤُا۟

"**They said: 'Shuʿayb! Does your prayer command you that we should abandon what our fathers served, or that we should give up doing as we please with our own property? You are indeed the forbearing, the right-minded one!'**"

### The objection is itself a finding

**The people name two things together** — and it is they who join them, not the messenger:

| The first | *mā yaʿbudu ābāʾunā* — **the religion of the forefathers** |
|---|---|
| The second | *an nafʿala fī amwālinā mā nashāʾ* — **doing as one pleases with property** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the sentence itself:** those who object have **noticed that a link is being made between the prayer and the ordering of property**, and they object to it. **The verse, that is, shows the side making that link to be not the messenger but his opponents** — and the objection itself certifies that the link exists.

***amwālinā* — "*our* property".** **And the eighty-fifth verse had named the same subject as *al-nāsa ashyāʾahum* — "*people's* things".** **Two possessive pronouns stand facing one another.**

**إِنَّكَ لَأَنتَ ٱلْحَلِيمُ ٱلرَّشِيدُ** — **two attributes of praise, in a context of mockery.** **And the word *ḥalīm* was used in this sūra of Abraham as well** (11/75: *inna Ibrāhīma la-ḥalīmun awwāhun munīb*, cited also at `009-tevbe.md` 9/114). **The same word occurs twelve verses apart in two places, one in earnest and one in derision.**

### قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ أَرَءَيْتُمْ إِن كُنتُ عَلَىٰ بَيِّنَةٍ مِّن رَّبِّى وَرَزَقَنِى مِنْهُ رِزْقًا حَسَنًا (88)

**The first move of the answer is worth recording: the messenger names his own means of living** — *wa razaqanī minhu rizqan ḥasanā.* **What is said, that is, is "I have no need of my own in making this call".**

**وَمَآ أُرِيدُ أَنْ أُخَالِفَكُمْ إِلَىٰ مَآ أَنْهَىٰكُمْ عَنْهُ** — "**I do not wish to do myself what I forbid you.**"

**The construction needs care: *ukhālifakum ilā* — "to go against you *towards* a thing".** **That is, "to forbid you a thing and then run to it myself behind your backs".** **The lexicographers record that the verb *khilāf* takes this sense with *ilā*.**

**إِنْ أُرِيدُ إِلَّا ٱلْإِصْلَٰحَ مَا ٱسْتَطَعْتُ** — ***iṣlāḥ* is the exact opposite of the *ifsād* forbidden to the people at 85** (ص-ل-ح / ف-س-د). **And the qualification *mā'staṭaʿt* is added: "as far as I am able".**

**I record this as an observation:** the messenger undertakes not the outcome but **the effort** — and the sentence ends with *wa mā tawfīqī illā bi'llāh*, that is, the outcome of the effort is not his either.

---

## 11/89-90 — وَيَٰقَوْمِ لَا يَجْرِمَنَّكُمْ شِقَاقِىٓ أَن يُصِيبَكُم مِّثْلُ مَآ أَصَابَ قَوْمَ نُوحٍ أَوْ قَوْمَ هُودٍ أَوْ قَوْمَ صَٰلِحٍ وَمَا قَوْمُ لُوطٍ مِّنكُم بِبَعِيدٍ

"**My people! Do not let your enmity towards me bring upon you the like of what came upon the people of Noah, or the people of Hūd, or the people of Ṣāliḥ. And the people of Lot are not far from you.**"

### The sūra turning back on itself

**This verse gathers into one sentence the four narratives the sūra has told up to that point.**

**And the order is the sūra's own order:** Noah (25-49) → Hūd (50-60) → Ṣāliḥ (61-68) → Lot (77-83). **The fifth messenger counts off four of those who came before him.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the sūra does not tell its narratives as independent of one another; **inside the fifth narrative the first four are used as evidence.**

**وَمَا قَوْمُ لُوطٍ مِّنكُم بِبَعِيدٍ** — **and this sentence is in word-for-word the same pattern as *wa mā hiya mina'l-ẓālimīna bi-baʿīd*, which came eight verses earlier (11/83).**

| Place | Sentence | What is "not far" |
|---|---|---|
| 11/83 | *wa mā hiya mina'l-ẓālimīna bi-baʿīd* | **The stones** — from the wrongdoers |
| **11/89** | *wa mā qawmu Lūṭin minkum bi-baʿīd* | **The people of Lot** — from you |

**The two sentences carry the same pattern in a way that can be verified from the text** — and this repetition was already recorded at the closing of the Lot section (in the note written on 11/83).

**The exegetes differ over what *bi-baʿīd* means:** nearness **in time**, nearness **in place** (the distance between Midian and the land of the people of Lot), or likeness **in deed**. **No preference is adopted;** all three readings are compatible with the wording of the sentence.

### إِنَّ رَبِّى رَحِيمٌ وَدُودٌ (90)

The root **و-د-د**: **to love, to be attached with affection.** ***Wudd* — love; *wadūd* is the intensive form.**

**The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān as a name of God:** here and **at `085-buruc.md` 85/14 (*wa huwa'l-ghafūru'l-wadūd*).** **Both were treated;** I rely on that treatment.

**And its placement is worth recording:** the word comes **immediately after four reminders of destruction.** **The sentence following the harshest reminder, that is, is the gentlest name.**

---

## 11/91-93 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰشُعَيْبُ مَا نَفْقَهُ كَثِيرًا مِّمَّا تَقُولُ وَإِنَّا لَنَرَىٰكَ فِينَا ضَعِيفًا وَلَوْلَا رَهْطُكَ لَرَجَمْنَٰكَ وَمَآ أَنتَ عَلَيْنَا بِعَزِيزٍ

"**They said: 'Shuʿayb! We do not understand much of what you say. We see you as weak among us. Were it not for your clan we would surely have stoned you. You are not mighty over us.'**"

### The admission itself

**The sentence says three things, and all three must be recorded:**

| Expression | What it admits |
|---|---|
| *mā nafqahu kathīran mimmā taqūl* | **We do not understand** — but we have decided |
| *innā la-narāka fīnā ḍaʿīfā* | **Our measure is strength** |
| *wa law lā rahṭuka la-rajamnāk* | **And what holds us back from you is a clan, not an argument** |

**I record this as my own reading:** the third sentence refutes the second out of their own mouths — **the reason they cannot touch a man they call "weak" is a balance of power they themselves acknowledge.**

**The root *fiqh* (ف-ق-ه) was treated at `009-tevbe.md` 9/122 (*li-yatafaqqahū fi'l-dīn*).** **There a journey was undertaken in order to understand; here judgement is passed without understanding.**

### قَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ أَرَهْطِىٓ أَعَزُّ عَلَيْكُم مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ وَٱتَّخَذْتُمُوهُ وَرَآءَكُمْ ظِهْرِيًّا (92)

**The answer takes up the word they used and puts it back in place:** they had said *mā anta ʿalaynā bi-**ʿazīz***; the messenger asks *a-rahṭī **aʿazzu** ʿalaykum mina'llāh*. **The same root (ع-ز-ز), in the same context, two verses apart.**

**وَرَآءَكُمْ ظِهْرِيًّا** — ***ẓihriyy*: root ظ-ه-ر — "back". The idiom means to throw a thing behind one's back, to pay it no heed.** **The lexicographers record it as an expression used of what is abandoned, what is not needed.**

**إِنَّ رَبِّى بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ مُحِيطٌ** — **and *ʿadhāba yawmin muḥīṭ* from eight verses earlier (84) finds its counterpart here.** **What is thrown behind is what encompasses.**

### وَيَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْمَلُوا۟ عَلَىٰ مَكَانَتِكُمْ إِنِّى عَٰمِلٌ (93)

**The same sentence comes a second time in the sūra** — **at 11/121, where it will this time be spoken to the Prophet:** *wa qul li'lladhīna lā yuʾminūna'ʿmalū ʿalā makānatikum innā ʿāmilūn.*

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the sūra gives the sentence Shuʿayb speaks to his people, twenty-eight verses later, directly to the one addressed. **The narrative ceases to be a counsel and becomes an instruction.**

**وَٱرْتَقِبُوٓا۟ إِنِّى مَعَكُمْ رَقِيبٌ** — **the root *raqaba* (ر-ق-ب): to watch, to wait.** **The imperative and the noun come one after the other from the same root: "watch, and I am watching with you".**

---

## 11/94-95 — وَلَمَّا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا نَجَّيْنَا شُعَيْبًا وَٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ مَعَهُۥ بِرَحْمَةٍ مِّنَّا وَأَخَذَتِ ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟ ٱلصَّيْحَةُ فَأَصْبَحُوا۟ فِى دِيَٰرِهِمْ جَٰثِمِينَ

"**When Our command came, We delivered Shuʿayb and those who believed with him by a mercy from Us. And the Cry seized those who had done wrong; morning found them fallen prone in their homes, as though they had never dwelt there. Take note: as Thamūd was put far away, so was Midian.**"

### The fifth repetition of the closing pattern

**The sūra closes each narrative with the same three elements**, and this is a structure that can be verified from the text:

| Narrative | The deliverance | What seizes | The closing |
|---|---|---|---|
| Noah (11/48-49) | *ihbiṭ bi-salām* | The flood | — |
| Hūd (11/58-60) | *najjaynā Hūdan* | *rīḥun ṣarṣar* (41/16, 54/19, 69/6) | *alā buʿdan li-ʿĀd* |
| Ṣāliḥ (11/66-68) | *najjaynā Ṣāliḥan* | *al-ṣayḥa* | *alā buʿdan li-Thamūd* |
| Lot (11/81-83) | *fa-asri bi-ahlik* | *ḥijāratun min sijjīl* | *wa mā hiya … bi-baʿīd* |
| **Shuʿayb (11/94-95)** | *najjaynā Shuʿayban* | ***al-ṣayḥa*** | ***alā buʿdan li-Madyana kamā baʿidat Thamūd*** |

**And the last closing refers back to an earlier one: *kamā baʿidat Thamūd*.** **The fifth seal, that is, cites the third.**

***Jāthimīn* (ج-ث-م) and *ka-an lam yaghnaw fīhā* (غ-ن-ي) were treated in the Ṣāliḥ narrative (11/67-68);** **here they are repeated letter for letter.**

**The root ب-ع-د occurs in this sūra in two opposed senses, and I record this as my own reading:**

| Place | Pattern | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| 11/89 | *wa mā … **bi-baʿīd*** | **Not far** — nearness |
| 11/95 | *alā **buʿdan** li-Madyan* | **Away with them** — expulsion |

**The same root, six verses apart, once as a warning and once as a verdict.** **The warning said "not far"; the verdict says "away with them".**

---

## 11/96-99 — وَلَقَدْ أَرْسَلْنَا مُوسَىٰ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَسُلْطَٰنٍ مُّبِينٍ · إِلَىٰ فِرْعَوْنَ وَمَلَإِي۟هِۦ فَٱتَّبَعُوٓا۟ أَمْرَ فِرْعَوْنَ وَمَآ أَمْرُ فِرْعَوْنَ بِرَشِيدٍ

"**And We sent Moses with Our signs and a clear warrant to Pharaoh and his leading men. They followed Pharaoh's command; and Pharaoh's command did not lead to what is right.**"

### The sixth narrative — and the shortened one

**Five of the sūra's narratives were told at length; the sixth fits into four verses.** **I record this as an observation about the structure:** the text leaves the Moses narrative to other sūras and takes only **one point** here: **whom the people followed.**

***fa'ttabaʿū amra Firʿawn* — and immediately the verdict: *wa mā amru Firʿawna bi-rashīd.*** **The verse, that is, weighs not the followers but the one followed.**

The root **ر-ش-د**: **to find the right way, maturity.** **And the word has already occurred twice in this sūra:**

| Place | About whom |
|---|---|
| 11/78 | *a-laysa minkum rajulun **rashīd*** — Lot's question |
| 11/87 | *innaka la-anta'l-ḥalīmu'l-**rashīd*** — said to Shuʿayb in mockery |
| **11/97** | *wa mā amru Firʿawna bi-**rashīd*** — **a verdict** |

**Three uses stand in the same sūra, and in all three the word is the object of a *search* or of a *claim*.** **In the third it is negated outright.**

### يَقْدُمُ قَوْمَهُۥ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ فَأَوْرَدَهُمُ ٱلنَّارَ وَبِئْسَ ٱلْوِرْدُ ٱلْمَوْرُودُ (98)

The root **ق-د-م**: **to go in front, to lead the way.** **The one who goes in front in this world goes in front in the hereafter too** — **but the place he arrives at changes.**

The root **و-ر-د**: **to come to water, to go down to the watering place.** ***Wird* — the place one goes to drink; the point at which a caravan comes down to water.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the word describes the most looked-for moment of life in the desert — **the man who leads the caravan to water.** **The verse sets up the same scene and makes the place arrived at the Fire:** *fa-awradahumu'l-nār.* **And it goes on: *biʾsa'l-wirdu'l-mawrūd* — "an evil arrival, that place arrived at".**

**The same root was treated at `019-meryem.md` 19/86 (*wa nasūqu'l-mujrimīna ilā jahannama wirdā*);** I rely on that treatment.

**وَأُتْبِعُوا۟ فِى هَٰذِهِۦ لَعْنَةً وَيَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ بِئْسَ ٱلرِّفْدُ ٱلْمَرْفُودُ** (99) — **and the same pattern is built a second time:**

| 98 | *biʾsa'l-**wirdu'l-mawrūd*** | **The arrival arrived at** |
|---|---|---|
| 99 | *biʾsa'l-**rifdu'l-marfūd*** | **The gift given** |

***Rifd* (ر-ف-د): help, a gift, support.** **The two sentences are in the same pattern — a noun plus a passive participle — and both put a positive word in a negative place.** **This is a matching of sound and structure that can be verified from the text.**

---

## 11/100-104 — ذَٰلِكَ مِنْ أَنۢبَآءِ ٱلْقُرَىٰ نَقُصُّهُۥ عَلَيْكَ مِنْهَا قَآئِمٌ وَحَصِيدٌ

"**These are among the tidings of the towns that We relate to you. Of them some are still standing and some are cut down.**"

### Six narratives in two words

***qāʾimun wa ḥaṣīd* — "standing and reaped".**

The root **ح-ص-د**: **to reap a crop.** ***Ḥaṣīd* — a reaped crop.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the agricultural sense of the word:** the verse sums up the peoples that were destroyed with the image of **a field** — **some still standing, some cut off at the root.** **And the image of the field implies that a reaping is not an *end* but a *harvest*; what is sown is reaped.**

**The same root was treated at `018-kehf.md` 18/45 (*fa-aṣbaḥa hashīman tadhrūhu'l-riyāḥ*) in a similar scene;** I rely on that treatment.

### وَمَا ظَلَمْنَٰهُمْ وَلَٰكِن ظَلَمُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُمْ (101)

**The same sentence is repeated in the Qurʾān and was treated at `016-nahl.md` 16/33 and `043-zuhruf.md` 43/76.**

**فَمَآ أَغْنَتْ عَنْهُمْ ءَالِهَتُهُمُ ٱلَّتِى يَدْعُونَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ مِن شَىْءٍ** — **and the verb *aghnat* is worth recording: at 68 and 95 the sūra had said *ka-an lam yaghnaw fīhā* ("as though they had never dwelt there").** **The same root (غ-ن-ي), here in the sense of "to avail".**

***Tatbīb* (ت-ب-ب): destruction, ruin.** **The root was treated at `111-tebbet.md` 111/1 (*tabbat yadā Abī Lahab*).** ***wa mā zādūhum ghayra tatbīb* — "they added nothing to them but ruin".**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the verse says *zādūhum* (they added). **What increases, that is, is not the expected benefit but the harm** — the verb is positive, its object negative.

### وَكَذَٰلِكَ أَخْذُ رَبِّكَ إِذَآ أَخَذَ ٱلْقُرَىٰ وَهِىَ ظَٰلِمَةٌ (102)

**The root *akhdh* (أ-خ-ذ) comes twice in the sentence: as a noun and as a verb.** **And a qualification is set down: *wa hiya ẓālima* — "while it is doing wrong".** **The seizing, that is, is attached to a condition; not to an identity.**

**This qualification is the textual counterpart of the method's clause "it is the attributes the verse describes"**, and the sūra will say it once more fifteen verses later (11/117: *wa mā kāna rabbuka li-yuhlika'l-qurā bi-ẓulmin wa ahluhā muṣliḥūn*).

### إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً لِّمَنْ خَافَ عَذَابَ ٱلْءَاخِرَةِ (103)

**The condition must be recorded: *li-man khāfa* — "for one who fears".** **What is told, that is, becomes a sign not for everyone but for one who has a certain turning.**

**ذَٰلِكَ يَوْمٌ مَّجْمُوعٌ لَّهُ ٱلنَّاسُ وَذَٰلِكَ يَوْمٌ مَّشْهُودٌ** — **two passive participles one after the other:** *majmūʿ* (gathered for) and *mashhūd* (witnessed). **And at 98-99 too two passive participles had come one after the other (*mawrūd*, *marfūd*).** **This section of the sūra keeps up the pattern.**

**وَمَا نُؤَخِّرُهُۥٓ إِلَّا لِأَجَلٍ مَّعْدُودٍ** (104) — ***ajalun maʿdūd*: a numbered term.** **The deferral is not indefiniteness; the number is known — only the side that knows it changes.**

---

## 11/105-108 — يَوْمَ يَأْتِ لَا تَكَلَّمُ نَفْسٌ إِلَّا بِإِذْنِهِۦ فَمِنْهُمْ شَقِىٌّ وَسَعِيدٌ

"**On the day it comes no soul will speak except by His leave. Among them some are wretched and some are happy.**"

### Two words

The root **ش-ق-و**: **to suffer hardship, to grow weary, to be wretched.** **The root was treated at `020-taha.md` 20/2 (*mā anzalnā ʿalayka'l-qurʾāna li-tashqā*) and 20/117 (*fa-tashqā*);** I rely on that treatment.

The root **س-ع-د**: **to be happy, to be fortunate.** ***Saʿīd* — happy.**

**The two words are set facing one another in this verse of the Qurʾān**, and the sūra gives them first as **nouns** (*shaqiyyun wa saʿīd*), then repeats them in the next two verses as **verbs** (*alladhīna shaqū* / *alladhīna suʿidū*).

### فَأَمَّا ٱلَّذِينَ شَقُوا۟ فَفِى ٱلنَّارِ لَهُمْ فِيهَا زَفِيرٌ وَشَهِيقٌ (106)

***Zafīr* (ز-ف-ر) and *shahīq* (ش-ه-ق):** the lexicographers explain the two as **the breath going out** and **the breath being drawn in**; the description used for the braying of a donkey is given with these same two words.

**I pass this on as the lexicographers give it, and I record this as my own reading:** the verse describes not a speech but a **sound** — and this stands beside the qualification in the preceding verse, *lā takallamu nafsun illā bi-idhnih* (none speaks without leave). **Speech has been taken away, and breath is left.**

***Shahīq*'s root was treated at `067-mulk.md` 67/7 (*samiʿū lahā shahīqan wa hiya tafūr*);** there the sound belongs to the Fire, here to those inside it.

### خَٰلِدِينَ فِيهَا مَا دَامَتِ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتُ وَٱلْأَرْضُ إِلَّا مَا شَآءَ رَبُّكَ

**This construction occurs in the Qurʾān only here — and twice, at 107 and at 108.** **And it is one of the places where the exegetes differ most.** **The disagreement is not concealed:**

| Question | Views |
|---|---|
| *mā dāmati'l-samāwātu wa'l-arḍ* | (a) **The Arabs' idiom "as long as the heavens and the earth endure"** — an expression of continuance; (b) **the heavens and earth of the hereafter itself** are meant (at `014-ibrahim.md` 14/48 *yawma tubaddalu'l-arḍu ghayra'l-arḍ* was treated) |
| *illā mā shāʾa rabbuk* | (a) **The exception is not a real one; it reports that the power is unrestricted** — "had He willed, He could have done otherwise"; (b) **the exception concerns those who will enter the Fire and come out**; (c) **the exception concerns the waiting periods before entry into the Fire** |
| The difference between the two verses | **At 107 the sentence ends with *inna rabbaka faʿʿālun limā yurīd*, at 108 with *ʿaṭāʾan ghayra majdhūdh*** — the second closing states unbrokenness outright |

**In this commentary no preference is adopted and no ruling is issued.**

**But that the *closings of the two verses differ* is a datum that can be verified from the text**, and I record it:

| Verse | The sentence after the exception |
|---|---|
| 107 (the Fire) | *inna rabbaka **faʿʿālun limā yurīd*** — **who does what He wills** |
| 108 (the Garden) | ***ʿaṭāʾan ghayra majdhūdh*** — **a gift without a break** |

***Majdhūdh* (ج-ذ-ذ): cut, broken off.** **The root was treated at `021-enbiya.md` 21/58 (*fa-jaʿalahum judhādhan*).** ***ghayra majdhūdh* — not cut off.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the same exception is placed in both, but **only in the second is unbrokenness stated in addition.** **The asymmetry is in the wording; its interpretation is disputed among the exegetes.**

---

## 11/109-112 — فَلَا تَكُ فِى مِرْيَةٍ مِّمَّا يَعْبُدُ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ … فَٱسْتَقِمْ كَمَآ أُمِرْتَ وَمَن تَابَ مَعَكَ وَلَا تَطْغَوْا۟

"**So be not in doubt about what these people serve. They serve only as their fathers served before them. And We shall pay them their share in full, without diminishing it.**"

**This section of the sūra turns from the narratives back to the one addressed.** **And the first sentence of the turn is a *prohibition of doubt*: *fa-lā taku fī miryatin*.**

***Mirya* (م-ر-ي): doubt, hesitation, wrangling.**

### وَلَقَدْ ءَاتَيْنَا مُوسَى ٱلْكِتَٰبَ فَٱخْتُلِفَ فِيهِ (110)

**And a ground is given: it happened this way before too.** ***fa'khtulifa fīh* — a passive pattern: "and there was disagreement over it".**

**وَلَوْلَا كَلِمَةٌ سَبَقَتْ مِن رَّبِّكَ لَقُضِىَ بَيْنَهُمْ** — **the same sentence was treated at `010-yunus.md` 10/19 and `041-fussilet.md` 41/45;** I rely on that treatment. **In the sūra the pattern *kalimatun sabaqat* stands as the reason for the deferral of the judgement.**

### فَٱسْتَقِمْ كَمَآ أُمِرْتَ وَمَن تَابَ مَعَكَ وَلَا تَطْغَوْا۟ (112)

The root **ق-و-م**, in Form X (*istiqāma*): **to be upright, to stand straight.** **The Form X pattern here carries not a seeking but a sense of steadfastness — the lexicographers explain it as "to remain continuously in a state".**

**And the command has three qualifications:**

| Qualification | Expression |
|---|---|
| **The measure** | *kamā **umirt*** — not as you determine but as you were commanded |
| **The scope** | *wa man tāba **maʿak*** — not you alone |
| **The limit** | *wa lā **taṭghaw*** — plural; do not overstep |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the inflection of the verbs:** the command begins in the singular (*istaqim*) and ends with a prohibition in the plural (*lā taṭghaw*). **The command to be upright, that is, is addressed to the individual, and the prohibition of overstepping to the community.**

The root **ط-غ-ي**: **to exceed the limit, to overflow the bound.** **The root was treated at `069-hakka.md` 69/11 (*lammā ṭaghā'l-māʾ*) of water overflowing, and at `096-alak.md` 96/6 of the human being.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** *istiqāma* and *ṭughyān* are two directions on the same axis — the one is to stand on the line, the other to cross it. **The verse sets them side by side in a single sentence.**

**إِنَّهُۥ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ بَصِيرٌ** — **and the sentence of watchfulness is repeated in this region of the sūra for the fourth time** (92: *muḥīṭ*, 111: *khabīr*, 112: *baṣīr*, 123: *wa mā rabbuka bi-ghāfilin ʿammā taʿmalūn*).

---

## 11/113-115 — وَلَا تَرْكَنُوٓا۟ إِلَى ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟ فَتَمَسَّكُمُ ٱلنَّارُ

"**Do not lean towards those who do wrong, or the Fire will touch you. You have no protectors besides God; and then you will not be helped.**"

### The root ر-ك-ن

***Rukn*: the corner of a building, its support, its firmest part.** ***Rukūn* — to rest on a thing, to lean on it, to incline towards it.**

**And the word had occurred earlier in this sūra:** **at 11/80 Lot said *aw āwī ilā ruknin shadīd* — "or could take shelter with some strong support".**

| Place | *Rukn* |
|---|---|
| 11/80 | **A support sought** — Lot's wish |
| **11/113** | **A support forbidden** — those who do wrong |

**The two uses stand in the same sūra, thirty-three verses apart, and are from the same root.** **I record this as my own reading:** the sūra first shows a messenger looking for a support, and then says **which support cannot be had.**

**The force of the verb must be recorded: what is forbidden is *rukūn* — that is, not "to become" but "to lean".** **The lexicographers explain it as the slightest inclination.** **And its counterpart is *fa-tamassakumu'l-nār* — "it will touch you", which is likewise the lightest verb of contact.**

**I record this as an observation, and it is not binding:** the sentence chooses **the least** at both ends — the lightest inclination, the lightest touch. **A measure is met with a measure.**

### وَأَقِمِ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ طَرَفَىِ ٱلنَّهَارِ وَزُلَفًا مِّنَ ٱلَّيْلِ (114)

***ṭarafayi'l-nahār* — the two ends of the day; *zulafan mina'l-layl* — the near hours of the night (ز-ل-ف: to draw near).**

**The exegetes differ over which prayers these times correspond to; no legal identification is made in this commentary.** **But similar descriptions of times were treated at `017-isra.md` 17/78 (*aqimi'l-ṣalāta li-dulūki'l-shamsi ilā ghasaqi'l-layl*) and `020-taha.md` 20/130 (*wa sabbiḥ … qabla ṭulūʿi'l-shamsi wa qabla ghurūbihā*).**

**إِنَّ ٱلْحَسَنَٰتِ يُذْهِبْنَ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتِ** — "**Good deeds take away bad ones.**"

**The exegetes differ over the scope of the sentence** — **which bad deeds, on what conditions.** **No legal ruling is issued.** **But the word order must be recorded: the sentence comes immediately after the command about *prayer* and immediately after the prohibition of *leaning towards those who do wrong*.** **A possibility, that is, is placed after a prohibition.**

**ذَٰلِكَ ذِكْرَىٰ لِلذَّٰكِرِينَ** — **the same root twice: *dhikrā* and *dhākirīn*.** **A reminder is a reminder to one who remembers.**

**وَٱصْبِرْ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يُضِيعُ أَجْرَ ٱلْمُحْسِنِينَ** (115) — **and the command to *patience* is the name of the thing shown again and again in the narrative section of the sūra.** **The pattern was treated at `012-yusuf.md` 12/90 (*innahū man yattaqi wa yaṣbir fa-inna'llāha lā yuḍīʿu ajra'l-muḥsinīn*).**

---

## 11/116-117 — فَلَوْلَا كَانَ مِنَ ٱلْقُرُونِ مِن قَبْلِكُمْ أُو۟لُوا۟ بَقِيَّةٍ يَنْهَوْنَ عَنِ ٱلْفَسَادِ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ إِلَّا قَلِيلًا مِّمَّنْ أَنجَيْنَا مِنْهُمْ

"**Were there not, among the generations before you, people of remaining worth who would forbid corruption on the earth? Only a few of those We delivered from among them. And those who did wrong pursued the ease they had been pampered with, and became criminals.**"

### أُو۟لُوا۟ بَقِيَّةٍ — the sūra's own word comes back

**Thirty verses earlier, Shuʿayb had said to his people *baqiyyatu'llāhi khayrun lakum* (11/86).** **Here the same root (ب-ق-ي) becomes an attribute of human beings: *ūlū baqiyya*.**

| Place | Construction | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| 11/86 | *baqiyyatu'llāh* | **What God has left** |
| **11/116** | *ūlū baqiyya* | **What remains in a human being** |

**This repetition can be verified from the text and lies inside the same sūra.**

**The lexicographers explain *baqiyya* here in two ways:** **(a) a store of reason, of good and of virtue** — "the sound part that remains"; **(b) the good section left within a community.** **Both follow from the word's sense of "remaining"; no preference is adopted.**

### The real subject of the sentence

***fa-law lā kāna* — "were there not?"** **The lexicographers record this pattern for reproach and regret.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the subject of the sentence:** the verse questions the peoples that were destroyed not by way of **those who did wrong** but by way of **those who did not prevent it.** **And the qualification *illā qalīlan mimman anjaynā minhum* completes it: those delivered are precisely those who did prevent.**

**The verb *najjaynā* (We delivered), repeated five times across the sūra, is here attached to a *cause*.**

**وَٱتَّبَعَ ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟ مَآ أُتْرِفُوا۟ فِيهِ** — ***itrāf* (ت-ر-ف): to be pampered in abundance.** **The root was treated at `023-muminun.md` 23/33 and `034-sebe.md` 34/34 (*mutrafūhā*);** I rely on that treatment.

**And the pattern of the verb is passive: *utrifū* — "they were pampered".** **I record this as an observation about the language: they do not build the ease, the ease is given to them; what they do is to *pursue* it (*ittabaʿa*).**

### وَمَا كَانَ رَبُّكَ لِيُهْلِكَ ٱلْقُرَىٰ بِظُلْمٍ وَأَهْلُهَا مُصْلِحُونَ (117)

**The qualification in the sentence must be recorded: *wa ahluhā muṣliḥūn*.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the verse gives the condition of destruction not with the word **belief** but with the word **setting right.** **And *muṣliḥ* was Shuʿayb's own description of himself in this sūra: *in urīdu illā'l-iṣlāḥa mā'staṭaʿt* (11/88).**

**Three verses thus form a chain:**

| 11/88 | The messenger: *in urīdu illā'l-iṣlāḥ* — **the intention** |
|---|---|
| 11/116 | The reproach: *ūlū baqiyya **yanhawna ʿani'l-fasād*** — **what was missing** |
| 11/117 | The rule: *wa ahluhā **muṣliḥūn*** — **the protecting condition** |

**All three links of the chain lie inside the same sūra and can be verified from the text.**

---

## 11/118-119 — وَلَوْ شَآءَ رَبُّكَ لَجَعَلَ ٱلنَّاسَ أُمَّةً وَٰحِدَةً وَلَا يَزَالُونَ مُخْتَلِفِينَ · إِلَّا مَن رَّحِمَ رَبُّكَ وَلِذَٰلِكَ خَلَقَهُمْ

"**Had your Lord willed, He would have made people a single community. But they will go on differing — except those on whom your Lord has had mercy. And for this He created them.**"

### وَلِذَٰلِكَ خَلَقَهُمْ — what the demonstrative refers to

**The exegetes differ at this point, and the disagreement is not concealed:**

| View | What *dhālika* points to |
|---|---|
| A | **To the differing** — people were created so as to diverge |
| B | **To the mercy** — the nearest word named is *raḥima*; the purpose of the creation is mercy |
| C | **To both at once** — the possibility of divergence and the possibility of mercy together |

**No preference is adopted.** **But grammatically this much can be recorded:** the nearest referent of a demonstrative is the element **immediately preceding it in the sentence**, and that element is *man raḥima rabbuk*. **This is a ground, not a definite ruling;** in the classical exegesis all three views have their defenders.

**The same structure was treated at `010-yunus.md` 10/19 and 10/99 (*wa law shāʾa rabbuka la-āmana man fi'l-arḍi kulluhum jamīʿā*);** I rely on that treatment.

**And I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the first half of the sentence attaches the possibility of **a single community** to **God's willing** and reports that it did not come about. **Plurality, that is, is named not as a defect but as an order** — and at `049-hucurat.md` 49/13 (*wa jaʿalnākum shuʿūban wa qabāʾila li-taʿārafū*) this was treated explicitly.

**وَتَمَّتْ كَلِمَةُ رَبِّكَ لَأَمْلَأَنَّ جَهَنَّمَ مِنَ ٱلْجِنَّةِ وَٱلنَّاسِ أَجْمَعِينَ** (119) — ***tammat kalimatu rabbik*: and nine verses earlier it had been said *wa law lā kalimatun sabaqat min rabbik* (110).** **The same word (كلمة), once as "gone before" and once as "completed", twice in the same region.**

**The sentence occurs in the same wording at `032-secde.md` 32/13 and `038-sad.md` 38/85 and was treated there.**

---

## 11/120-123 — The closing of the sūra

*wa kullan naquṣṣu ʿalayka min anbāʾi'l-rusuli mā nuthabbitu bihī fuʾādak · wa jāʾaka fī hādhihi'l-ḥaqqu wa mawʿiẓatun wa dhikrā li'l-muʾminīn*

"**Of the tidings of the messengers We relate to you all with which We make your heart firm. In this there has come to you the truth, and an admonition and a reminder for the believers.**"

### The stated purpose of the narratives

**After telling six narratives, the sūra says why it told them** — and this is the text's own statement, not an interpretation:

| To whom | For what |
|---|---|
| To the one addressed | *mā nuthabbitu bihī **fuʾādak*** — **to make the heart firm** |
| To everyone | *al-**ḥaqq*** — the truth itself |
| To the believers | ***mawʿiẓa** and **dhikrā*** — admonition and reminder |

The root **ث-ب-ت**: **to stand in place, to become firm.** **The root was treated at `014-ibrahim.md` 14/27 (*yuthabbitu'llāhu'lladhīna āmanū bi'l-qawli'l-thābit*) and `008-enfal.md` 8/11-12 (*wa li-yarbiṭa ʿalā qulūbikum* / *fa-thabbitū'lladhīna āmanū*);** I rely on that treatment.

**فُؤَاد** — root **ف-أ-د**: the lexicographers explain the word as the **moving, kindling** side of the heart; *faʾada'l-laḥma* is to roast meat over a fire. **What is asked to be made firm, that is, is the thing that by its nature stirs.**

**I record this as an observation about the language and pass it on as the lexicographers give it.**

### وَقُل لِّلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ ٱعْمَلُوا۟ عَلَىٰ مَكَانَتِكُمْ إِنَّا عَٰمِلُونَ · وَٱنتَظِرُوٓا۟ إِنَّا مُنتَظِرُونَ (121-122)

**And the sentence Shuʿayb spoke to his people twenty-eight verses earlier (11/93) is here given to the one addressed in word-for-word the same pattern:**

| 11/93 (Shuʿayb) | *iʿmalū ʿalā makānatikum **innī ʿāmil** … wa'rtaqibū innī maʿakum raqīb* |
|---|---|
| **11/121-122** | *iʿmalū ʿalā makānatikum **innā ʿāmilūn** … wa'ntaẓirū innā muntaẓirūn* |

**The difference is singular versus plural** (*innī* → *innā*) **and the verb of waiting has changed** (*irtiqāb* → *intiẓār*). **This is a correspondence that can be verified from the text.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the sūra hands the narrative it has told over to the one addressed as **speech.** **The narrative does not end; its sentence changes hands.**

### وَلِلَّهِ غَيْبُ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَإِلَيْهِ يُرْجَعُ ٱلْأَمْرُ كُلُّهُۥ فَٱعْبُدْهُ وَتَوَكَّلْ عَلَيْهِ (123)

**And the last verse of the sūra returns to its opening.**

| 11/1-2 | *kitābun uḥkimat āyātuhū … **allā taʿbudū illā'llāh*** |
|---|---|
| **11/123** | ***fa'ʿbudhu** wa tawakkal ʿalayh* |

**The same root (ع-ب-د), in the sūra's first command and in its last.** **And in between six messengers, all of whom began with the same sentence: *uʿbudū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh.***

***yurjaʿu'l-amru kulluh* — and the word *amr* had been used again and again in this sūra for the coming of the destruction** (11/40 *jāʾa amrunā*, 58, 66, 76, 82, 94; and at 11/97 *amra Firʿawn*). **The last verse turns that word entirely back to a single place.**

**وَمَا رَبُّكَ بِغَٰفِلٍ عَمَّا تَعْمَلُونَ** — **and it is the last of the sentences of watchfulness.** **The sūra closes the sequence *muḥīṭ* (84, 92), *khabīr* (111), *baṣīr* (112) here with *ghayru ghāfil*** — **four doing the same work with four different words.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

| Word / root | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **أحكمت / فصلت** | 11/1 | The sūra's two methods: firmness and detail |
| **اعبدوا الله ما لكم من إله غيره** | 50, 61, 84 (and 26) | **The same sentence, with every messenger** |
| **جاء أمرنا** | 40, 58, 66, 76, 82, 94 | The shared threshold of the narratives |
| **نجينا** | 58, 66, 94 (+ 40, 81) | The verb of deliverance — at 116 attached to its **cause** |
| **ألا بعدا** | 60 (ʿĀd), 68 (Thamūd), 95 (Midian) | **Three seals** — and 95 refers back to 68 |
| **بعيد** | 83 (the stones), 89 (the people of Lot) | "Not far" — the same pattern twice |
| **ب-ق-ي** | 86 (*baqiyyatu'llāh*), 116 (*ūlū baqiyya*) | What God has left / what remains in a human being |
| **ر-ك-ن** | 80 (a support sought), 113 (a support forbidden) | The same root, two directions |
| **ر-ش-د** | 78, 87, 97 | Sought / made a mockery of / denied |
| **اعملوا على مكانتكم** | 93 (Shuʿayb), 121 (the one addressed) | The narrative's sentence changes hands |
| **م-ح-ط / خ-ب-ر / ب-ص-ر / غ-ف-ل** | 84-92, 111, 112, 123 | Four different words, one watchfulness |
| **ص-ل-ح / ف-س-د** | 85, 88, 116, 117 | The moral axis of the sūra |

### Where this sūra does not speak with certainty

| Verse | What was not done |
|---|---|
| 7 | *wa kāna ʿarshuhū ʿalā'l-māʾ* — no argument about modality was entered into concerning the throne and the water |
| 40 | *wa fāra'l-tannūr* — no preference was adopted as to what the oven is |
| 42-46 | The details of the reports about Noah's son were not entered into; no preference was adopted between the readings of *innahū ʿamalun ghayru ṣāliḥ* |
| 69-73 | No reports were passed on concerning the identity of the messengers and Abraham's guests |
| 78 | No preference was adopted as to the scope of the expression *hāʾulāʾi banātī*; the two views of the exegetes were passed on |
| 86 | No preference was adopted in the construction *baqiyyatu'llāh* |
| 89 | No preference was adopted as to whether *bi-baʿīd* is of time or of place |
| 107-108 | No preference was adopted in the exception *illā mā shāʾa rabbuk*; no ruling was issued |
| 114 | No legal identification of the times of prayer was made; no ruling was issued on the scope of *al-ḥasanātu yudhhibna'l-sayyiʾāt* |
| 118-119 | No preference was adopted as to the referent of the demonstrative in *wa li-dhālika khalaqahum* |

---
